## WHAT THIS FILE IS

Canonical reference for every Claude Code session working on the Ber Wilson platform. Lives in the project root as `CLAUDE.md`; Claude Code reads it automatically.

**Builder:** Richard (EVP, Ber Wilson) — builds in Claude Code terminal. Not a developer.
**Golden rule:** Never introduce complexity without demonstrated need. Every decision reversible or portable.

> **Version 2.0 note:** This file was rewritten on 2026-06-22 to match the code as it actually is. The v1.0 reference described a Claude-Haiku/Sonnet app that no longer exists — runtime AI is now **Google Gemini**. If anything below disagrees with the code, the code wins; fix this file.

## HOW TO DEPLOY TO PRODUCTION (read this when Richard says "deploy" / "ship it" / "make it live")

**This exact folder — `/Users/richardwhite/berwilson-platform` on the Mac Studio — IS production AND the git repo.** Editing here + the three steps below is a full deploy. There is no separate server. (Set up 2026-08-25: Richard works directly on the Studio; the old MacBook→Studio rsync flow and the stale `~/Documents/berwilson-platform-main` copy are both retired/deleted.)

When Richard asks to deploy, run these from `/Users/richardwhite/berwilson-platform`:
```bash
git add -A && git commit -m "<what changed>"          # save + version
git push origin main                                   # back up to GitHub (berwilsonai/berwilson-platform, public)
export PATH="$HOME/.node/bin:$PATH" && npm run build   # build (PATH must include ~/.node/bin)
launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.berwilson.platform   # restart the live app (launchd keeps it on :3000, tailnet-only)
zsh deploy/tailnet-setup.sh                            # assert both tailscale serve listeners + env/hostname agreement
```
**Why that last line:** the serve config is Tailscale's state, not ours, and a logout/upgrade/reinstall wipes it. The old assertion lived in `deploy-to-studio.sh`, which was retired when the Studio became the repo — so from then until 2026-09-16 **nothing re-established it on a deploy**. `tailnet-setup.sh` restores both listeners (443→app, 8443→kong), warns if the node key has an expiry set (an expired key silently drops the platform off the tailnet), and fails loudly if `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL`/`APP_URL` no longer match the live hostname. **Changing tailnet changes the MagicDNS suffix, and `NEXT_PUBLIC_*` is baked in at BUILD time — so a tailnet move needs `--fix` then a full rebuild, never just a restart.**
Then confirm: `git log --oneline -1` and check the app responds. If `npm run build` fails, do NOT kickstart — the old build stays live; fix the error first. Always `git pull --ff-only` before starting a fresh editing session. Full host/infra detail lives in the `self-hosted-mac-studio-deployment` memory.

## 1. PROJECT IDENTITY

**Company:** Ber Wilson — vertically integrated construction, development, and USA prefab steel manufacturing. Salt Lake City, UT.
**Website:** berwilson.com
**Platform:** Internal executive intelligence tool for two executives managing a multi-sector construction pipeline (government contracting, infrastructure, real estate development, prefab manufacturing, institutional).
**Core problem:** Two people managing billions in pipeline across federal bids, PE negotiations, JV structures, subcontractor relationships, and manufacturing coordination. They need a single AI-powered source of truth that thinks like a construction COO.

**Where it's headed:** The near-term job is a clean CRM — track projects, tasks, people, and the company itself so the executive team can steer from a high level. The long-term job is an intelligence layer: load an emailed opportunity (text or RFP docs) into "Ber AI," have it assess the opportunity against Ber Wilson's capabilities and appetite, and recommend whether to pursue and spin up a project. The proposal-intake flow (§6) is the first working slice of that.

## 2. TECH STACK

| Layer | Technology | Notes |
|-------|-----------|-------|
| Database + Auth | **Self-hosted Supabase** (Postgres, Auth, Storage) on the Mac Studio | Docker (Colima) lean stack, tailnet-only at `:8443`. RLS enabled on every table (but see §8 — app traffic uses the service role). pgvector enabled. Cloud project paused as rollback safety net. |
| Frontend | Next.js 16 (App Router, TypeScript) | Server components default. Client only for interactivity. |
| Styling | Tailwind CSS v4 + shadcn/ui (`@base-ui/react`) | No custom CSS unless unavoidable. |
| Runtime AI | **Local Qwen via LM Studio** (`src/lib/ai/local.ts`) | All runtime AI. Gemini path dormant behind `AI_PROVIDER` flag (web research only). See §5 + AI Model Rules below. |
| Microsoft Graph | Microsoft Graph API (OAuth) | Powers `/calendar` meeting prep, party enrichment, and **on-demand Email Research** (`/email-research` — human-triggered `$search` over the connected mailbox, human-confirmed intake). The automatic email-to-task scraper was removed 2026-06-25 and stays removed. |
| Vector Search | pgvector inside Supabase Postgres | `text-embedding-qwen3-embedding-0.6b`, 768-dim (truncated + renormalized). |
| File Storage | Supabase Storage (`documents` bucket) | Organized by project / entity / site ID. |
| Deployment | **Mac Studio, tailnet-only** (launchd + `tailscale serve`) | `zsh deploy/deploy-to-studio.sh` from the MacBook. Vercel deleted 2026-07-07; `git push` = GitHub backup only. Crons are launchd agents on the Studio. |

### AI Model Rules (CURRENT — FULLY LOCAL since 2026-07-07)

**The platform is fully self-hosted and fully local-AI as of the 2026-07-07 cutover** (Richard's decision: absolute security, nothing leaves his hardware). Production = the Mac Studio, tailnet-only: app at `https://richards-mac-studio.tail9acc02.ts.net/`, self-hosted Supabase at `:8443`, LM Studio on localhost:1234. **(The MagicDNS suffix changed `tail0e5306` → `tail9acc02` on 2026-09-16 when the tailnet moved to the berwilson.com Workspace org; the Studio is now `100.102.45.39`. Dated entries below still name the old values — that is history, not current state.)**

- **All runtime AI** → `qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b` via LM Studio's OpenAI-compatible API (`AI_PROVIDER=local`, `src/lib/ai/local.ts`). Expect ~30–60s on extraction-class tasks (reasoning-heavy model, ~75 tok/s generation).
- **Embeddings** → `text-embedding-qwen3-embedding-0.6b`, truncated+renormalized to 768 dims (schema unchanged). The whole knowledge base was re-embedded locally at cutover (213 chunks). **Never mix embedding models** — a model change means wipe + re-embed (`deploy/reembed.mjs`).
- **PDFs** → local text extraction via `unpdf` (no model call for transcription); images need a vision model loaded in LM Studio.
- **Web research / enrichment** (`research.ts`) → blocked in local mode unless `LOCAL_ALLOW_WEB_RESEARCH=true` (uses Gemini + Google Search; only the query leaves, never platform data). Currently ON — Richard kept the Gemini key for the Enrich Profile buttons on contacts/vendors (verified live 2026-07-07).
- The Gemini path in `gemini.ts` still exists behind the flag but is dormant; cloud Supabase project `qauclkrdejgtpywqixho` is **paused** (restorable safety net); the Vercel project is **deleted**. Every AI call still logs to `ai_queries`.

Anthropic Claude is not used at runtime (removed 2026-06-22).

**Dormant Gemini-path reference (pre-cutover roles; today only web research touches Gemini):**

- **`gemini-2.5-flash`** → Extraction, classification, document/cert summarization, synthesis, briefs, enrichment, research. The workhorse. Routed through `callGemini` / `callGeminiWithFile` in `src/lib/ai/gemini.ts`.
- **`gemini-2.5-pro`** → The construction-executive agent's main reasoning loop (`src/lib/ai/agent.ts`).
- **`gemini-embedding-001`** (768-dim) → Embeddings for RAG (`src/lib/ai/embeddings.ts`, direct v1beta REST call).
- **Web research** → Gemini with Google Search grounding (`src/lib/ai/research.ts`). Perplexity was the original plan and was never adopted; the file header comment still references it — ignore that.
- **Opus / any Claude model** → not used here. (Claude Code, the dev tool, is separate from the app's runtime AI.)

Every AI call logs to the `ai_queries` table (user, prompt, response, model, tokens, latency) — keep that contract when adding calls.

## 4. DATABASE SCHEMA

**Source of truth is `src/types/database.ts` (generated).** Run `npm run gen-types` after every migration. The schema has expanded far past the original core tables; do not trust a hand-maintained list — read the generated types.

Migrations live in `supabase/migrations/` (41 as of this writing, numbered chronologically).

### Conventions (unchanged)
- All PKs: `id uuid default gen_random_uuid() primary key`.
- All timestamps: `timestamptz default now()`, UTC.
- RLS enabled on every table.
- `activity_log` is append-only — no UPDATE/DELETE policies, ever.
- `updated_at` auto-maintained by trigger; tracked tables auto-insert into `activity_log` via trigger.

### Table groups (read database.ts for columns)
- **Core CRM:** `projects` (with `parent_project_id` hierarchy, `bid_due_date`, `win_probability`, capture fields), `project_players`, `milestones`, `updates`, `documents`, `dd_items`, `financing_structures`, `compliance_items`, `project_dependencies`.
- **Tasks (2026-06-25):** `tasks` (real task model — title/what/why/how/assignee_id/project_id/due_date/status/completed_at), `task_notes` (per-task notes feed), `team_members` (assignee list, seeded Richard/Eric). Replaces the old `updates.action_items` JSON for the task UI.
- **Shared children (2026-09-23):** `project_players`, `milestones`, `dd_items`, `financing_structures`, `entity_projects`, `compliance_items` and `research_artifacts` hang off **either** a project or an opportunity — nullable `project_id` + nullable `opportunity_id`, with a check constraint (exactly-one for the first five, at-most-one for the last two). `milestones.stage` is plain **text**, not the `project_stage` enum, so one table carries both pipelines. Never assume `project_id` is set on these.
- **Directory:** `parties` (people + orgs via `is_organization`), `contact_aliases`, `entities` (legal entities/vendors), `entity_projects`, `party_entities`, `certifications`.
- **Capital raise (2026-07-10):** `investors` (relationship pipeline, links to `parties`), `investments` (investor × target: parent company or project, optional `spv_entity_id` → `entities`), `investor_notes`.
- **Steel CRM (2026-07-25):** `steel_deals` (prefab steel deal pipeline: quote→engineering→order_placed→delivered→paid, lead source, salesperson FK→team_members, sqft/$SF/value), `steel_deal_notes`. Own role `steel_sales` sees only this module.
- **Dino (2026-07-28):** internal operating company (acquired plumbing/HVAC co, dinoservicepros.com — NOT a vendor). `dino_revenue` (source_type internal|external; internal → FK project_id, external → client_name; per-job or periodic lump), `dino_payments` (the $150k/12-mo obligation schedule), `dino_notes`. Tracks the internal-vs-external revenue split (show Dino its revenue increasingly comes from Ber Wilson) + money we owe them. Admin-only. See build-status.
- **Company:** `company_profile`, `media`.
- **AI / intelligence:** `ai_queries`, `chunks` (pgvector), `agent_conversations`, `agent_messages`, `research_artifacts`, `review_queue`, `risk_scores`, `proposal_intake_sessions`, `stored_briefs`, `portfolio_briefs`.
- **Microsoft Graph:** `email_tokens` (OAuth — calendar/enrichment/email research).
- **Audit:** `activity_log` (append-only).
- **RPCs:** `match_chunks`, `match_parties_by_name`, `match_projects_by_name`.

## 5. AI ARCHITECTURE

### Unified Gemini client — `src/lib/ai/gemini.ts`
- `callGemini({ task, systemPrompt, userMessage, userId, promptVersion?, maxTokens?, jsonMode? })` — text in, text/JSON out. `jsonMode` defaults true (uses `responseMimeType: application/json` + strips stray code fences).
- `callGeminiWithFile({ systemPrompt, prompt, file: { mimeType, dataBase64 }, userId, logLabel?, promptVersion?, maxTokens?, jsonMode? })` — multimodal: a PDF/image plus a text instruction. Used by document and certification summarization.
- Both return `{ data, model, tokensIn, tokensOut, latencyMs }` and log to `ai_queries` (fire-and-forget). When the model returns valid JSON, `data` is the parsed object; otherwise `data` is the raw string — callers should branch on `typeof data === 'object'`.

### Extraction shape
Extraction prompts return structured JSON (summary, action_items, waiting_on, risks, decisions, mentioned_parties, mentioned_projects, confidence). See `src/lib/ai/prompts/extraction.ts`.

### Agent — `src/lib/ai/agent.ts` + `agent-tools.ts`
`gemini-2.5-pro` agentic loop (up to 5 tool-call rounds). Injects the Ber Wilson company profile into context so the agent knows the company's qualifications without a tool call. Tools are declared in `agent-tools.ts` and executed via `executeToolCall`. Conversations persist to `agent_conversations` / `agent_messages`.

**Construction Executive Agent persona:** senior EVP/COO, 25+ yrs government contracting, large-scale development, design-build GC, prefab, construction finance. An owner-operator, not a consultant. Four lenses on every recommendation: **Commercial** (can we win it?), **Operational** (can we deliver it?), **Financial** (can we get paid, at what margin?), **Compliance** (can we protect the downside?). Response protocol: Situation → Risks → Recommendation → Next Decision. Never ungrounded guesses; never hide risk; never substitute for legal/tax counsel; always distinguish facts, estimates, and judgments.

### Hybrid retrieval (RAG)
Query → SQL filter → vector search (`match_chunks` RPC over `chunks.embedding`) → re-rank (recency/confidence/cosine) → top chunks into context → grounded answer with citations → log to `ai_queries`.

### Proposal matching — `src/lib/ai/proposal-matching.ts`
Dedupes an inbound opportunity against existing `projects`/`parties` (solicitation #, trigram name, location, client). Does NOT judge fit — that's `fit-assessment.ts`.

### Company context + fit assessment — `src/lib/ai/company-context.ts`, `fit-assessment.ts`
- `getCompanyContext()` builds one prompt-ready markdown block from `company_profile` + active certs (identity, capabilities, bonding, **pursuit profile**: target sectors, project-size range, geographies, delivery/contract vehicles, differentiators, disqualifiers, past performance). Single source of truth — the agent and fit assessment both use it, so they judge against the same picture. Returns `hasPursuitProfile` so callers know when the profile is too thin to judge confidently.
- `assessFit(extraction, userId)` scores an opportunity against that context (Commercial/Operational/Financial/Compliance lenses) and returns `{recommendation: pursue|consider|pass, fit_score, summary, strengths, concerns, gaps, key_questions, profile_incomplete}`. Wired into `api/proposals/intake` (non-fatal) and surfaced as a card in `ProposalIntakeWizard`. **Quality scales with how completely the pursuit profile is filled in on `/company`.**

---

## 6. PROPOSAL INTAKE (the intelligence on-ramp)

Already built, end to end:
1. `/proposals/intake` — `ProposalIntakeWizard` uploads RFP text/docs (chunked upload via `api/proposals/upload-chunk`).
2. `api/proposals/intake` — runs extraction + `proposal-matching` against the company profile, produces an assessment and a proposed set of projects/parties/entities.
3. `api/proposals/confirm` — on approval, creates the projects, contacts, and entities in one transaction.

This is the spine of the "email an opportunity → Ber AI assesses → you decide → project created" loop. As of 2026-06-22 the intake also runs a **fit assessment** (§5) — a pursue/consider/pass recommendation scored against the company pursuit profile, shown at the top of the review step. The remaining work is **data, not plumbing**: fill in the pursuit profile on `/company` (target sectors, size range, geographies, delivery/contract vehicles, differentiators, disqualifiers). Until then the assessment self-flags as low-confidence (`profile_incomplete`).

## 7. CONVENTIONS

### Code
- TypeScript strict mode. **Zero `any` escapes as of 2026-07-12** (was ~115 → ~23 → 0); don't add any back. Jsonb inserts use `as unknown as Json`; enum filters cast to `Database['public']['Enums'][…]`; polymorphic JSX wrappers use `ElementType`.
- Server components default; `'use client'` only when interactive.
- All DB access via Supabase clients — no raw SQL in components.
- API routes handle AI + mutations; pages fetch via server components.
- Error boundary + `loading.tsx` on every route.

### Naming
- Database: `snake_case`. TypeScript: `camelCase` vars/functions, `PascalCase` types/components.
- Files: `kebab-case` (non-components), `PascalCase` (components). API paths: `kebab-case`.

UNSET = quotes stay in the platform's own deal folder. Writing here works under drive.file because it is a SHARED DRIVE folder moose@ can add children to — see google-drive.ts

**The MICROSOFT_* vars are gone** (2026-09-21): zero references in `src/` and none in `.env.local` since the 2026-08-23 move to Google Workspace. `MICROSOFT_SECRET_EXPIRES` was documented as driving a 30-day health warning; the health page never read it.

`ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` and `PERPLEXITY_API_KEY` are no longer used — remove from any new env files. The n8n-era vars (`N8N_*`, `INGESTION_INBOUND_SECRET`) are gone from Vercel (verified 2026-07-03). `NEXT_PUBLIC_SITE_URL` is no longer referenced anywhere (the agent self-fetches that used it were refactored to direct lib calls 2026-07-03).

### Git
- Main branch: `main`. Pushing is GitHub backup only (Vercel deleted 2026-07-07) — **deploying = `zsh deploy/deploy-to-studio.sh`** after pushing.
- Conventional commits: `feat:`, `fix:`, `refactor:`, `docs:`. Never commit `.env.local`.

## 8. SECURITY / COMPLIANCE

- Middleware (`middleware.ts`) gates every route: unauthenticated users are redirected to `/login`. Public exceptions: login/auth, the risk-scores cron, and the Microsoft OAuth callback.
- **RLS is defense-in-depth, not the active boundary.** App traffic — including ~33 server pages — uses the service-role admin client (`lib/supabase/admin.ts`), which bypasses RLS. The auth boundary is the middleware. This is an accepted trade-off for a 2-user internal tool; if the team grows or sensitive data lands, move pages to the RLS-respecting server client (`lib/supabase/server.ts`).
- `documents.classification` flags standard vs sensitive. All AI calls logged to `ai_queries`.
- US-only infrastructure. **Do not store CUI** until a GovCloud migration is done. GovCloud trigger: annual DoD revenue > $2M OR a contract requiring CMMC L2. Stack is portable by design (standard Postgres, no vendor lock-in).

---

## 9. KNOWN DEBT / AUDIT NOTES (2026-06-22)

Captured so future sessions don't rediscover them:
- ~~Scope sprawl~~ **LARGELY RESOLVED 2026-07-03:** the Equity & Valuation and Portfolio site-hierarchy modules were removed entirely (~13.5K lines across code + generated types), along with background checks, vendor scorecards/reviews, the Procore stub, and dead scraper-era tables/functions. Nav is 15 → 13 destinations. If a CFO joins and needs basic finance tools/reports, build a fresh purpose-built section then — do not resurrect the old modules.
- ~~Overlapping "attention" surfaces~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-03:** `/attention` folded into Dashboard (Needs Attention panel + sidebar badge on Dashboard), `/capacity` folded into `/tasks` (per-person workload chips); both old routes redirect. `/timeline` left primary nav (linked from the Projects toolbar). One attention engine remains: `/api/attention` (also feeds the agent's `get_attention_items`).
- **Two directory concepts** — **surface consolidated 2026-07-03:** one nav destination (`/contacts` = "Directory" with Contacts | Vendors & Contractors tabs via `?tab=vendors`; the `/vendors` list redirects there, detail/new routes stay). The *data model* is still two tables (`parties` + `entities`); a full data merge into `parties` remains optional future work — only if the split causes real pain.
- ~~Type drift~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-12:** every stale `as any`/`: any` removed (the generated types had long covered `project_dependencies`/`stored_briefs`); `src` now has zero `any` escapes. Keep it that way (§7).
- ~~Speculative early-build features~~ **RESOLVED 2026-07-03:** Procore stub, background checks, and vendor scorecards/reviews removed. Party/entity enrichment (Gemini research) is real and stays.
- ~~Legacy `action_items`~~ **FULLY RETIRED 2026-07-03.** Reads moved to the `tasks` table earlier (via `src/lib/tasks/queries.ts`); writes stopped 2026-07-03 — manual-paste extraction creates real tasks at save time (`src/lib/tasks/from-action-items.ts`) and review-queue approval converts legacy pending items. `20260704000004_drop_action_items.sql` drops the column (code shipped first; dual-schema safe). The extraction *prompt* still returns `action_items` JSON — that's the contract feeding task creation, not schema debt. Old email-era tables `processed_emails`/`graph_subscriptions` and `updates.outlook_web_link` are dropped by `20260704000001_simplification_drops.sql`.
- **Pre-existing lint noise (recount 2026-07-05, after the lint-debt pass):** two `react-hooks/purity` errors in `src/app/dashboard/page.tsx` (`Date.now()` in a server component — rule misfire, runtime fine); 12 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors (load-on-mount fetch / localStorage hydration / sync-form-on-modal-open patterns — each needs a per-component rewrite to satisfy the React Compiler; deliberately left, they work; recount 2026-07-12); 6 `@next/next/no-img-element` warnings (Supabase Storage images — switching to `next/image` needs `remotePatterns` config; do it if image cost/LCP ever matters). Everything else was cleaned 2026-07-05 (76 → 21 problems; 20 as of 2026-07-12).
- **`team_members` is a 4th people-concept** (alongside parties/entities/stakeholders), kept tiny for fast task assignment. **Partly reconciled 2026-07-21:** `team_members.party_id` now links a task owner to their `parties` contact (quick-add + meeting owner-promotion both find-or-create the linked contact), so owner and contact are one person. Full table merge still deferred — tasks keep `assignee_id → team_members.id`; do a full merge only if the split causes real pain.

## 10. CONTACTS / DIRECTORY (original intent)

`parties` is the global contact directory. People and organizations both live here (`is_organization=true` for firms). A party is global — editing it updates everywhere. Contact detail shows: bio/contact info, every project they're linked to (via `project_players`) with role, updates/documents mentioning them, DD items assigned, compliance items owned. Routes: `/contacts`, `/contacts/[id]`.

Note the drift flagged in §9: `entities`/vendors partly duplicate this. The *surface* is consolidated (one Directory destination, 2026-07-03); when touching directory data code, still prefer consolidating toward `parties`.

---

## 11. DO NOT

- Add a separate vector database (pgvector is fine for years).
- Use LangChain, LlamaIndex, or any AI orchestration framework.
- Build microservices, use Docker/containers, or add Prisma/Drizzle (Supabase client + generated types only).
- Reintroduce a second AI provider SDK — runtime AI is Gemini. (Removing Anthropic was deliberate.)
- Pre-build features for future phases.
- Store CUI until GovCloud migration is complete.
- Never let mail become a record **without human review**. The webhook pipeline removed 2026-06-25 was banned because it *silently turned inbox mail into records*; that prohibition stands and is the invariant to protect.
  - **Amended 2026-08-23 (Richard's explicit direction):** the platform now DOES read both mailboxes automatically on a schedule — `/api/cron/email-sweep` sweeps all of `moose@` and `tuaone@`, summarizes every thread, and groups them into candidate deals. This is a deliberate reversal of the "human triggers each mailbox search" half of the old rule, made to populate the CRM from years of existing correspondence.
  - **What did NOT change:** the sweep only ever stages `pending` sessions. Creating a project, opportunity, party, or task still requires the human confirm step in Email Ingestion. No automatic record creation, ever.
- Reintroduce Microsoft Graph / Outlook. The platform moved to Google Workspace 2026-08-23 (service account + domain-wide delegation, read-only scopes). There are no stored mail tokens and no OAuth flow to maintain.

## 12. BUILD STATUS

**Reality:** well beyond the original Phase 1/2 plan. Live and in daily use, **self-hosted on the Mac Studio** (`100.86.79.4`, Tailscale-only) against self-hosted Supabase under Colima, with AI served by LM Studio on the same machine. Vercel is no longer the runtime.

**Working:** projects (CRUD, pipeline/program views, hierarchy, all detail tabs), **interactive project map (/map — offline basemap, illustrated markers, rail corridors, present mode)**, **task handoffs (waiting-on) + printable weekly report (/reports/weekly/print, per-person pages)**, **opportunities**, **investors (capital raise pipeline: relationship stages + per-deal commitments vs parent co / project SPVs; named raises w/ tranche schedules + per-raise dashboards; task tags, Ber AI tools + RAG, attention + daily-brief wiring)**, **objectives steering board (Now/Soon/Possibly + PDF export, wired into tasks/dashboard/brief)**, **steel CRM (/steel — prefab steel deal pipeline w/ its own `steel_sales` role, 2026-07-25; one-click quote generation from a Drive-hosted Google Doc template → PDF on the deal + in Drive, 2026-09-16)**, **dino (/dino — internal operating-company revenue tracker: internal-vs-external split + $150k payment schedule, admin-only, 2026-07-28)**, dashboard (single attention surface, opens with Now objectives), timeline, **team tasks** (per-person workload, project/opportunity/objective tags), **one Directory (Contacts | Vendors tabs) + business-card scanner (photo → on-device OCR → researched contact)**, company profile (thin), review queue, activity log, manual-paste extraction (action items → real tasks), intel (RAG + streaming agent) + **ambient Ask Ber AI dock (⌘J, every page)**, **one Intake destination (`/intake`: Email | Proposal tabs, 2026-07-17)** — proposal intake → assessment → project creation, and Email Intake (in-platform **Gmail** sweep → report → opportunity/project + people + tasks). **Calendar/meeting-prep and mail both run on Google Workspace via per-mailbox OAuth (Microsoft Graph removed 2026-08-23); the email-to-task scraper was removed (see below).** Equity & Portfolio modules removed 2026-07-03 (see below).

**Done 2026-09-23 (opportunities carry the same child records as projects; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: "Opportunities should have all the same fields as projects (documents, players etc…) so we can add the appropriate information."** An acquisition needs the people on the deal, a diligence checklist, a capital structure, the entities involved and a timeline at least as much as a construction project does — and there was nowhere to put any of it.
- **⚠ FIVE CHILD TABLES WERE HARD-WIRED TO `project_id NOT NULL`, so the only way to record a deal's players or diligence was to INVENT A PROJECT.** `project_players`, `milestones`, `dd_items`, `financing_structures` and `entity_projects` each now take a nullable `opportunity_id` beside `project_id` with a `num_nonnulls(...) = 1` check; `compliance_items` and `research_artifacts` take it as `<= 1`, because a NULL there already means a company-level obligation rather than a missing scope. **The shape is the one `tasks` (06-27) and `documents` (meetings, steel deals) already use — NOT parallel `opportunity_players` / `opportunity_milestones` tables**, which would fork every query, route and component; forking is exactly what cost this codebase the `runDocumentAiPass` drift and nine copies of `formatDate`.
- **⚠ THE UNIQUE CONSTRAINTS STOPPED GUARDING THE MOMENT `project_id` WENT NULL.** `project_players` carries `unique(project_id, party_id, role)` and `entity_projects` `unique(entity_id, project_id, relationship)` — and Postgres treats NULLs as distinct, so the same person could be added to a deal twice in the same role with the constraint sitting right there. Matching **partial** unique indexes on the opportunity side close it (verified live: the second identical player returns **409**, not a duplicate row).
- **⚠ `milestones.stage` WAS A POSTGRES ENUM, AND ONE ENUM CANNOT CARRY TWO PIPELINES.** Widened to plain `text` (`using stage::text`, existing values survive verbatim), following the convention every vocabulary added since opportunities/steel/leads already uses. `MilestonesTab` now takes `stages` / `stageLabels` / `advance` as **props** rather than importing the project vocabulary, so a deal's milestones group under its own 6-stage pipeline and "advance" PATCHes `status` the way a project's PATCHes `stage`. **Checked first that nothing else reads the column** — across the whole `src` tree only that one grouping expression did.
- **⚠ AN ENUM CHANGE MADE `project_id` NULLABLE IN TWO PLACES THAT ASSUMED OTHERWISE, AND `tsc` CAUGHT BOTH.** The timeline is the project Gantt and now skips deal milestones explicitly. The calendar does the opposite and **shows them**: a milestone with a target date is a deadline whoever owns it, so `CalendarEvent` gained an optional `href` and an opportunity milestone links back to `/opportunities/[id]/milestones` instead of a `/projects/` URL that would 404.
- **The opportunity detail page is a tabbed record now, not a nine-section scroll.** `ProjectTabBar` became `RecordTabBar` (base path + tab list as props) and both records share it, so the "show the tabs that have rows, hide the rest under More" rule from 09-22 applies to deals for free — **verified live: a bare opportunity shows Overview/Notes/Documents, and after one row landed in each table the bar grew Players, Milestones, Financing, Diligence and Entities & Vendors, each carrying its count.** The players table was lifted into one `RecordPlayers` rather than copied.
- **⚠ THE BRIEF PRINT VIEW SITS OUTSIDE THE ROUTE GROUP, DELIBERATELY.** `(detail)/layout.tsx` wraps the tabs; `brief/print` is its sibling, so a document carried into a meeting does not come out with a tab bar across the top. (Worth knowing: `projects/[id]/brief/print` **is** wrapped by the project layout and does get the header and tabs — pre-existing, untouched, and the same route-group move would fix it.)
- **The brief and Ber AI can see the new data, or it would be invisible where it matters most.** `assembleOpportunityBrief` reads all five children and reports them through formatters **extracted from the project assembler rather than copied** — verified on real rows: MILESTONES, DUE DILIGENCE, CAPITAL STRUCTURE, COMPLIANCE and PEOPLE ON THIS RECORD all render, and the project brief still renders its own unchanged. `query_opportunity` returns players/milestones/diligence/capital_structure, and its own description says so. Absence is stated too: the gaps block now calls out an untracked deal timeline, an empty diligence checklist and a missing capital structure.
- **⚠ THE EVIDENCE-DOCUMENT PICKER IS DELIBERATELY ABSENT ON A DEAL'S COMPLIANCE ITEMS.** `compliance_items.evidence_doc_id` is a foreign key to `documents`, and an opportunity's files live in `opportunity_documents` — offering them would have produced a 23503 on save. Caught by reading the constraint rather than by running it; the item's notes can name the file until that FK is widened.
- **Every write route now decides scope in one place** (`src/lib/records/scope.ts` `scopeFromBody`, mirroring the database's exactly-one rule at the edge so a bad body is a 400, not a 500) and guards it through one `canAccessRecord`. Four of the five routes had **no viewer check at all**, relying on the middleware allowlist; they carry their own now, per the 09-22 posture that the layer whose job is not to trust the one above should not.
- **Verified against the live database and the deployed app, not fixtures:** every constraint exercised in rolled-back transactions (both-scopes refused, neither refused, duplicate refused, cascade leaving **0 orphans**); all 12 opportunity routes rendering **200 authenticated** with the brief print confirmed chromeless; all 8 project routes re-checked for regression; a full round-trip creating a player, milestone, diligence item, capital structure, entity link and compliance item through the real API, then the deal deleted — **every table back to its exact pre-test count, 0 residue**; project-scoped writes re-run and reverted; 307 unauthenticated on pages and 401 on the API over the tailnet. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint **at its real measured baseline of 20 problems (14 errors, 6 warnings), unchanged** — note CLAUDE.md had been claiming 27.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** the project-only scalar fields (solicitation number, contract type, delivery method, bid/award/NTP dates, win strategy, incumbent, competitors) — a deal already has the equivalent of each that means anything to it (probability, thesis, lead, counterparty, target close), and bid-and-build fields on an acquisition would be noise on a form; an investor roll-up on the deal's Financing tab (`investments` target the parent company or a **project**, never an opportunity, so there is nothing to sum); and a `updates`-style feed on opportunities (progress Notes is that feed, and the tab is labelled Notes for exactly that reason).

**Done 2026-09-23 (the queue can now be finished from the queue — 0 of 193 items were actionable in place, now 189; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's ask: a cleaner, simpler UX, and put as much of the time-consuming work as possible on the local model. Measuring inverted the second half of the question.**
- **⚠ THE LOCAL MODEL IS 93% IDLE BECAUSE IT HAS FINISHED, NOT BECAUSE NOTHING WAS ASKED OF IT.** 1,752 calls / 23.2 busy hours across 14 days = **6.9% utilisation**, statistically unchanged since the 6.3% measured on 08-25 despite everything built since. The last several hourly sweeps report `moreWork: false` with **every** model-bound phase at zero — summarize 0, apply 0, embed 0, predecide 0. There is no backlog to chew. **Adding a model phase here would have been manufacturing work**, and this codebase has already removed three such passes after measurement (09-22).
- **⚠ THE PLATFORM PRODUCES DECISIONS AND CONVERTS ALMOST NONE OF THEM, AND THE CAUSE WAS THE UI, NOT THE RECOMMENDATION.** `/decide` held **193 items** (79 leads of which 71 `pursue`, 76 staged sessions of which 69 `create`, 38 flagged matches). Conversion: **5 intake sessions confirmed ever** against 136 decided — and 52 of the 56 dismissals were the model's own auto-dismiss, so a human has settled almost nothing — and **0 leads promoted or forwarded out of 1,268 scored**. `promoteLead()` had shipped on 08-25 and **had never once run in production**.
- **⚠ THE MODEL HAD ALREADY DONE THE WORK AND THE SCREEN MADE YOU DO IT AGAIN.** A representative pending session carries a complete project: name, `pursuit`, `government`, **$625M**, contract type, delivery method, a real description, 4 people with roles and emails, 3 tasks with assignees and due dates, `suggested_record: "project"`, confidence 0.95. Nothing left to type. To agree you had to leave `/decide`, open a 25-input form on another route — **which dropped the `predecision` on the way in**, so the verdict you were shown vanished at the moment you needed it — scroll, and press Create. Average correspondence behind one session is **12,327 characters**, so re-forming the judgement costs ~12 minutes. 193 items at that price is why nothing converted.
- **⚠ `merge` WAS A LABEL WITH NOTHING BEHIND IT.** Pre-decide has returned `disposition: 'merge'` with a `merge_target_name` since it shipped, and `/decide` and `/intake` both rendered it — while there was **no merge control in the review form and no merge branch in the confirm route**. Six sessions sat on a recommendation whose only available action was to dismiss it. The machinery existed and had simply never been called this way: `linkClusterToRecord` ties a session's threads to a record with `linked` certainty and the apply phase then posts the correspondence and imports attachments — confirm has called it since day one, **with the id of a record it had just created**. Handing it an EXISTING id is the merge. New `POST /api/email-ingestion/merge`, ~30 lines over existing code.
- **Resolving the merge target is a string comparison, not a judgement, and deliberately so.** `match_candidates` already carries `project_id` + `project_name` and the model chose from that closed list, so a name it returns either IS a candidate or was invented — and inventing is exactly the case that must not resolve. `resolveMergeTarget` matches the WHOLE name case-insensitively (never a substring), and **refuses when two candidates share one**, the same ambiguity rule the thread router holds. All 6 live merge sessions resolve to real existing projects; 11 unit cases cover the refusals.
- **⚠ REVIEW ROWS ARRIVED UNDECIDABLE, AND THE TEXT THEY NEEDED WAS ALREADY IN THE ROW.** 38 items rendering as "Inferred email match" with no title, no note and no verdict — while `review_queue.ai_explanation` has held a complete sentence naming the record all along (*"Matched to this project by same deal name — Myton Rail. Approve to post it and index it…"*). The page simply never selected it. No model involved; the rows now title from the matched project and carry the explanation.
- **One definition of what a session would create** (`src/lib/email-ingestion/defaults.ts` `buildConfirmBody`). It existed twice in spirit and nowhere in fact — the review form derived it from the extraction in four rules and the only way to act was to render that form. The confirm route's own `ConfirmBody`/`PartyAction`/`TaskAction` types moved here and the route imports them, so the request shape has exactly one declaration. Two copies of a mapping this load-bearing is how the 09-17 `runDocumentAiPass` fork drifted and silently discarded 413,000 characters.
- **⚠ THE READINESS GATE IS DETERMINISTIC BECAUSE MEASURING KILLED THE MODEL VERSION.** A "is this draft complete" pass was designed and dropped: run over all 76 live sessions, **65 of 69 `create` sessions already carry a name for their suggested kind and exactly 4 do not**, and a name is the only thing the form hard-blocks on. Value is absent on 51 of 65 and sector on 7 — both optional at the schema and in the form, so treating either as a blocker would send a human to a form with nothing to do. A model call would have re-decided what a `!= null` answers. **The 4 blocked rows show their reason and offer no Accept**, so there is never an Accept that 400s.
- **Accept from `/decide`, symmetric with the existing dismiss.** intake `create` → the existing confirm route with the draft; intake `merge` → the new merge route; lead → `promoteLead` at the target its route already implies; review → `{resolution:'approved'}`. Accept confirms once and dismiss does not — the asymmetry is the point, since setting aside is reversible and creates nothing while accepting writes a record, contacts, tasks, documents and publishes to Drive. **The draft is FETCHED on click rather than shipped with the page**: a full draft is a couple of kilobytes and most rows are never accepted.
- **⚠ CLAUDE.md §11 IS UNTOUCHED AND THIS IS THE REASON IT STILL HOLDS.** Nothing here creates a record automatically. The click IS the human review the invariant requires; what changed is that it lands where you are standing instead of three pages away. The review screens remain for when you want to edit first, and now show the recommendation that sent you there.
- **The lead promote target comes from the route the triage already chose** (`promoteTargetFor`): construction→project, steel→steel, corporate→opportunity, dino→forward. **`unknown` deliberately maps to null** — a lead the triage could not place is exactly the one a human should place, and a one-click guess between four record types would misfile deals silently. Live: 72 construction, 6 steel, 1 corporate.
- **The weekly brief now says the queue exists.** Richard's own answer to why zero leads converted was that he had not been looking, and nothing ever told him — arrivals are announced once through a latch that correctly never repeats, and the accumulated pile was never mentioned again. `summarizeDecideQueue()` shares `countDecideItems`' filters and the page's `decideWeight` (extracted to `src/lib/decide/rank.ts`, pure, imported by both), so the brief cannot claim a different queue or a different top item from the screen it links to. **Verified agreeing exactly: 193 = 193.**
- **Verified against the live database, live Drive and the deployed app — not fixtures.** 11 unit cases on the merge resolver plus `buildConfirmBody` run over all 76 real sessions with per-session shape assertions (including that no party is set to `link` without an id). Then **full round-trips on real records, each reverted**: an intake accept created a project with **14 players, 5 tasks, an update, a document, 51 chunks and 8 thread_links seeded at their real message counts** (9, 1, 14 — not zero, so history cannot re-post); a merge linked one thread with `linked` certainty at 3 of 3 and **left projects at 15, because merging creates nothing**; a lead promoted with both the firm and the estimator linked as players; a review approve posted and indexed 13 chunks. Every refusal path exercised (409 re-merge, 400 non-merge, 404 unknown, 400 no id, **401 unauthenticated**, 409 draft-on-confirmed). **All four reverted to exact pre-test counts — 0 residue across projects/parties/tasks/updates/documents/chunks/players/thread_links**, both test Drive folders trashed, and the test lead's Gmail label restored to match the other 71. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint **at the documented 27 baseline, unchanged**.
- **Rendered and clicked on the deployed app:** 193 rows, **189 carrying an Accept** (the 4 without show their blocker), chips agreeing with the badge, deadline-first ordering, merge rows naming their target, review rows reading as real records, and the confirm dialog stating what it will do — cancelled without confirming, all rows intact. `/decide` renders in **0.17–0.38s**, in line with the rest of the app, so building 76 drafts server-side costs nothing.
- **⚠ WORTH KNOWING: a nested same-app iframe will not hydrate this list**, so the 08-27 mobile harness could not measure it. Mobile sizing was confirmed from computed styles instead with the media query verified inactive (`matchMedia('(min-width:640px)')` false): the Accept button computes to **44px** at `h-11 sm:h-7` and its label to `display:none`, matching the existing dismiss control the 08-27 pass verified at 44×44. No horizontal overflow at 386px.
- **⚠ Richard's manual step, and it is now the only one:** **4 staged sessions cannot be accepted from the list** because the correspondence never states a name — *LOI*, *Text*, *Due Diligence Letter*, *Energy Development Group*. Each says so on its row; opening it and typing a name is the whole job.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** a bulk "accept all ready" (Richard's explicit choice — one click creates one record); any new model phase (see the measurement above); merge for a candidate naming an opportunity (`match_candidates` carries only `project_id` today, so it falls through to the review screen rather than guessing); and auto-creating records from mail — **§11 stands, unchanged**.

**Done 2026-09-22 (info@ inbox: 8,045 threads → 189; unsubscribed from 26 senders; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard: "I mainly want you to clean up the info@berwilson.com inbox. It has soo much random trash… We only want to consider leads, rfp's, etc… get rid of everything else. The tuaone and moose emails will be managed by eric and richard personally. You are in charge of the info@berwilson.com email to keep it clean."** Standing authority over one mailbox, and the deal mailboxes explicitly out of scope — which is just as well, since the platform holds read-only Gmail scopes there (trashing a test message returned 403).
- **⚠ THE JUNK LIST IS NOT `leads.status='spam'`, AND USING IT WOULD HAVE CUT THE BID FEED.** That column is a BID-FIT verdict. Among its 886 rows: **137 threads from bidnet.com and 36 from mybidmatch.com — plan rooms, and therefore a live lead source** — plus ~35 real people including a counterparty from the Stockton correspondence. New `junk-senders.ts` classifies BULK MARKETING instead, and **the list maintained by hand is the PROTECTION list, deliberately**: an entry wrongly added there costs a little clutter, an entry missing from a junk list costs a bid invitation. Protected are our own domain, `.gov`/`.mil`, bid boards and plan rooms, **any sender that has ever produced a real lead** (evidence beats any list — a plan room protects itself by having worked once), any sender whose mail is already filed onto a record, and anything that is not list mail at all.
- **⚠ THE SCAN WAS BUILT THE WRONG WAY ROUND AND COST AN HOUR BEFORE I NOTICED.** Reading all 8,045 threads to learn each sender is ~170ms per thread plus pacing — **80 minutes**. Gmail can search BY sender, so one query per candidate domain finds all of its inbox threads at once: **182 candidates → 90 seconds**. Candidates come from a 501-thread sample of the inbox plus every sender the lead pipeline has ever seen; a high-volume sender cannot hide from a 6% sample, and the long tail is what the routine pass is for.
- **⚠ READING THE VERDICTS BEFORE ACTING CAUGHT FOUR FAMILIES THE CLASSIFIER WOULD HAVE DESTROYED.** Each was a distinct bug, now fixed and unit-checked:
  1. **VERP / sender rewriting.** `sender+sortiz=utah.gov@outreachsystems.net` was read as the relay's domain, so **94 threads of Utah APEX Accelerator bulletins and Hill AFB Industry Partner Exchange invitations** — sent by utah.gov, and squarely "leads and RFPs" — were classified marketing. The real sender is now decoded out of the local part, restoring the public-sector protection.
  2. **Shared relays.** 14 threads on `ccsend.com` were **four unrelated organisations** (a contractor lead service, the Utah Valley Chamber, an Arizona licensing school). A relay domain identifies the plumbing, not the sender, and can never carry one verdict.
  3. **Mixed senders.** `bulk` was a boolean set by ANY thread carrying List-Unsubscribe, so a domain sending marketing AND something else was condemned by its marketing half: **google.com** (Ads promos beside real "Folder shared with you" notices) and **microsoft.com** (Learn promos beside account security codes) both came out junk, putting 56 threads of real notices at risk. It is now a SHARE measured against every thread — google.com 7%, microsoft.com 4%.
  4. **A 0.95 threshold was itself wrong**, sparing Houzz, Home Depot and Total Wine on a single non-bulk message each.
- **⚠ THE REAL FIX WAS NOT A THRESHOLD BUT THE GRAIN OF THE DECISION: it is made PER THREAD.** Only mail that individually carries List-Unsubscribe is ever touched, so a receipt or a Drive share survives even when its domain is condemned. **Verified live: Home Depot 50 spammed / 19 kept, Houzz 94/23, RocketLawyer 31/20 — 93 non-list threads deliberately left behind.** That is what lets `BULK_SHARE_REQUIRED` be a judgement about the SENDER rather than a safety margin; 0.6 sits in the measured gap between the mixed platforms (4-50%) and the marketing senders (63-100%).
- **Unsubscribe is RFC 8058 one-click ONLY, and needs no Gmail write scope at all** — it is an HTTPS POST to the sender's own endpoint. It refuses a bare `List-Unsubscribe` link (that is meant for a human and may be a preferences page, where a blind POST can do something else), refuses `http` and `mailto`, and will not follow a redirect off the sender's origin.
- **Professional bodies were spammed but NOT unsubscribed** (`uli.org`, `southjordanchamber.org`, `nationalcapacd.org`, `thinkwood.com`, `govology.com`). Richard's rule is "unsubscribed OR routed to spam" — either satisfies it, and spam is the fully reversible half. Ber Wilson may hold paid memberships there, and silently cancelling a member benefit is not what "clean up the inbox" asked for.
- **Old mail was ARCHIVED, not spammed.** 7,221 of the remaining 7,399 threads were over 90 days old and already read — finished business, not junk. Archive keeps them in All Mail, searchable, in the platform's own database, and one click from return; spam would auto-delete them in 30 days. **`messages.batchModify` takes 1,000 ids per call, so 8,414 messages took 9 requests rather than 8,414.**
- **⚠ THE AGE RULE SWEPT NINE OPEN `pursue` LEADS OUT, AND BEING OLD IS NOT BEING FINISHED.** A bid invitation nobody has ruled on belongs in front of a human — Walmart #7450 Depot, Sky Mountain Golf, a 5-storey steel package, Fort Polk teaming. All nine restored to the inbox; the seven `pass` leads correctly left archived.
- **RESULT: info@ went 8,045 → 189 threads.** What remains is what Richard asked for: **74 BuildingConnected bid invites and addenda, 22 BidNet, PlanHub, MyBidMatch, Thumbtack customer leads**, plus real people and our own mail. **26 senders unsubscribed, 647 marketing threads spammed, 0 refused.** Verified afterwards that **nothing important reached spam** — BidNet 0, MyBidMatch 0, BuildingConnected 0, PlanHub 0, Utah APEX 0; the only non-marketing hits in a spam search were **pre-existing Gmail spam** (cold outreach, a Google verification code), confirmed thread by thread against the applied list.
- **Ongoing without anyone:** a new `hygiene` phase runs last in the daily 5:30am lead sweep on a **30-day window** (wide enough to see three messages from a sender and call it a pattern, narrow enough to stay cheap). It scans `in:inbox`, which makes it **self-limiting and stateless** — a thread moved to SPAM leaves the scan, so nothing is reconsidered and no "already handled" ledger has to live anywhere. A sender who ignores the unsubscribe reappears and is unsubscribed again, which is correct rather than a bug. Off via `LEAD_MAILBOX_HYGIENE=off`.
- **Fixed at the source while here: `oauthAccessToken` had NO retry**, so one dropped connection failed every Google feature in the platform — sweeps, briefs, Drive syncs — with an error that reads like a credential problem. This box's link drops often enough that it killed two runs of the inbox scan. `googleRequest` was hardened on 2026-09-20 by moving its token mint inside its own loop; **every other caller of `getAccessToken` was still exposed.** A real auth failure still throws immediately — verified rejecting an unknown mailbox in **0ms** rather than after four backoffs.
- **New Lead Inbox Hygiene health card.** The phase is non-fatal inside the sweep, so its failure is silent by construction and the only symptom would be the inbox quietly filling back up; it warns above 1,500 threads.
- **Reversal is preserved, not assumed.** `~/berwilson-data/backups/info-hygiene-plan-*.json` lists every family and thread id spammed; `info-archived-*.json` lists all 8,414 archived message ids. Re-adding `INBOX` to exactly those ids restores the previous state.
- **Verified:** 33 unit checks including all four near-miss families; `tsc` + `next build` clean; **eslint 27 problems, zero in any file touched**; deployed twice with tailnet serve asserted.
- **⚠ Richard's manual steps:** (1) **3 senders could not be unsubscribed** — `officedepot.com` and `jooble.org` offer no one-click endpoint, and `mccleerycompany.com` hit a network blip; their mail is spammed and Gmail will learn, but a manual unsubscribe would be cleaner. (2) The five professional bodies above were **spammed but not unsubscribed** — if you want out of ULI or the South Jordan Chamber entirely, do it from their own emails. (3) One test message remains in `moose@` (subject "Ber Intelligence self-loop verification"); the platform cannot delete it, by design.

**Done 2026-09-22 (junk stops at the Gmail edge; only LIVE records receive mail and documents; notifications reach Chat; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's ask: the platform is an automated executive assistant reading all the mail — how do we stop it cluttering Ber Intelligence and the Drive?** Prioritise `moose@`/`tuaone@` over `info@` (which "gets a lot of junk mail and RFPs we won't be interested in"), only pull updates and documents onto an ACTIVE project, notify each other when a record gains something, and get rid of the junk. His picks (AskUserQuestion): **"live" = any project not lost/closed/on_hold** (a bid you are chasing still pulls in its drawings), **label AND archive** info@ junk in Gmail while keeping the row auditable, and notifications to **Google Chat + the in-app bell**.
- **⚠ MEASURED FIRST, AND HALF THE PREMISE WAS WRONG. The Drive was already clean:** 5 of 294 documents in `_Unsorted`, 262 of them imported FROM Drive (the team's own filing), 32 hand-uploaded, and **zero traceable to an email attachment**. `info@` was already quarantined on its own pipeline and cron and can never reach a project. And **moose@/tuaone@ already had priority** — they are the only mailboxes that get summarized, clustered and staged. The junk was in the two mailboxes he called cleanest: **743 of 1,054 deal threads (70.5%) were newsletters** — pumpkins, oil-change reminders, Mark Miller Toyota review replies — each costing a 25-50s local-model call to reach that verdict.
- **⚠ THE PLATFORM WAS EMAILING ITSELF.** The weekly brief, lead digest and task digest go to `LEADS_NOTIFY_EMAIL=moose@,tuaone@` — the mailboxes the deal sweep reads — so every one came straight back in and was summarized as deal correspondence. **28 since 2026-08-26 and growing daily.** Outbound mail is now stamped `PLATFORM_SENDER_NAME` ("Ber Intelligence") in its From **display name**, and a deterministic `prefilter.ts` settles it to `skipped` with no model call. Recognition needs the **name AND an internal sender together** — the address alone matches genuine mail between the two executives, the name alone could be spoofed from outside. **Every** message must be platform-sent, so a human replying to a digest reopens the thread. Drafted replies are deliberately NOT stamped (`buildRawMessage.fromName` is opt-in): a draft is written for a human to send under their own name.
- **Gmail-side exclusions now cover the DEAL sweep, not just leads.** `leadExclusions()` → `mailExclusions(pipeline)`; `buildQuery` already accepted them and `fetch-phase` was passing them only for `'lead'`. **Measured over 90 days: 963 → 753 threads, 210 never fetched, stored or read (~2h of local-model time).** Split is lopsided and worth knowing: **moose@ drops 1 of 152; tuaone@ drops 209 of 811** — the marketing is almost all in tuaone@. It also gives the deal mailboxes the same one-gesture escape hatch info@ has: a Gmail filter applying `bw-filtered` retires a repeat sender with no code change.
- **⚠ THE PREFILTER IS DELIBERATELY ONE RULE LONG, and measuring is why.** The obvious second rules do not survive the real corpus: a Drive notice *"Folder shared with you: Mira Vista - American Fork"* was summarized `relevance='deal'` with that deal name, and *"Invitation: Gov Pointe Teaming USACE"* likewise with two attachments. Both look like chrome; both are real signal. The bar for a new rule is that the class **cannot** carry deal signal, not merely that it usually does not.
- **ONE definition of "live"** (`src/lib/records/live.ts`) replaces four places that had drifted: apply-phase posted correspondence onto any record, Drive filing filed into any record, the publish reconcile published any record, and only `project-folder-sync` filtered at all — in JS, on two of the three dormant statuses. **The JS filter is preserved and its comment now says why: `status=not.in.(closed,lost)` is NULL for a NULL status and PostgREST drops it**, and one project has no status set. A **dormant link is KEPT and `applied_message_count` is NOT advanced**, so reviving a record replays what it missed (mail arriving while a pursuit is parked is usually why it gets un-parked) — but `last_applied_at` IS stamped, or a dormant link would sort to the front of every batch forever and starve the live records behind it. Explicit human "Publish to Drive" bypasses the gate (`includeDormant`); the automatic passes do not.
- **⚠ THE APPLY BATCH COULD NEVER REACH MOST OF ITS OWN BACKLOG — found by running it, not by reading it.** A caught-up link was skipped inside the loop **without its `last_applied_at` moving**, so it kept its place at the front of the order forever; the same 100 links were re-read every run and everything behind them was unreachable. Live: **44 links held unapplied correspondence, only 3 fell inside the first 100, and the run reported `linksConsidered: 0`** while Myton Rail sat at 0 of 3 messages and DUBHES at 0 of 7, since 2026-09-18. That is also why attachment-bearing threads never imported their files. The delta is a **cross-table comparison PostgREST cannot express**, so the batch is now chosen in two steps: a light paged scan in the batch's own order — **deliberately without `raw_markdown`**, which is every conversation's full body — then the full embed for the chosen ids. `pendingFound` reports the real backlog rather than the slice. **Drained live: 44 → 0, 5 attachments imported and indexed, 0 documents left pending or errored.**
- **Notifications reach Chat as well as the bell, and now cover email.** Every notification ever written was still unread, which is what a channel most of the company cannot open looks like. `broadcastEvents` mirrors the same events through the existing `notify({channel:'chat'})` seam — same `GROUP_ABOVE = 4` grouping and the same `soleActor` exclusion, because two surfaces disagreeing about what happened is worse than one. **Drive link first, platform link second: the post is for the people who are NOT in the app.** New `correspondence` kind fires when the sweep files mail onto a live record with certainty — never for an `inferred` guess, which already has a destination in the review queue. And the hand-run Drive import rang no bell while the identical nightly path did; fixed.
- **⚠ THE FIRST NOTIFICATION BODY WAS HEADER CHROME, caught by reading the live output rather than the code.** It led with `"### 2026-09-07 16:05 — BER WILSON COMPANY <info@…> To: From: …"` and taught the reader nothing. `firstProse()` skips both `renderThread`'s own heading and the header block an Outlook/Gmail forward pastes into the body, bounded so a message that genuinely is all headers still shows something. It now reads *"Response from Stockton Mayor · Mayor Joe, Hello sir. Happy Labor Day! The requested information was intended to understand your water situation…"*.
- **New `opts.announce`.** Correspondence from three weeks ago is not news, and announcing forty records at once teaches people to ignore the bell — the one thing a notification channel cannot recover from. The backlog drain passed `false` (**notifications verified unchanged at 15 throughout**); steady-state runs always announce.
- **info@ junk now leaves the inbox.** `INBOX` is a system label like any other to the API, so archiving rides along with the verdict — **no extra scope, no re-consent**. Only the closed verdicts (`Filtered`/`Pass`/`Closed`); Pursue/Consider/Promoted stay in the inbox where a human must act. **Restoring INBOX is conditional on the PREVIOUS label having been one we archived under**, which distinguishes "the platform changed its mind" from "a human archived this and the platform must not undo it" — and needs no new column to know the difference. **Backfilled 1,179 threads, 0 failed: info@ INBOX 9,058 → 8,001, labels untouched, re-run a clean no-op.** Paced at 150ms because Gmail meters by cost-units-per-minute; the select is **paginated**, because 1,179 rows silently truncate at PostgREST's 1000-row cap.
- **`summarize` no longer takes the whole clock** (`SUMMARIZE_SHARE = 0.5`). It used to take `remaining()` outright, so the moment there was any backlog `apply`, `embed` and `predecide` were handed 0 and tripped `outOfTime` on their first iteration. A phase that never runs reports nothing, which is how this stayed invisible.
- **⚠ THREE PLANNED CHANGES WERE BUILT AND THEN REMOVED AFTER MEASURING THEM. Each is recorded in the code where someone would re-add it.**
  1. **A noise gate on routing.** The premise was "13 newsletters got filed onto real projects". Reading them killed it: they are a **DocuSign receipt for the IAN acquisition LOI**, *"Fwd: Helper Data Center Risk Analysis"*, a Drive share of the Helper UT folder, GridEdge meeting notes, a cancelled Stockton meeting — and **11 of the 13 were matched by a human-set alias**, the strongest signal the matcher has. **`relevance` grades whether a thread is substantive PROSE, not whether it is junk**; automated-in-form and about-a-real-deal are independent, and conflating them drops exactly the notices worth keeping.
  2. **A 30-day age-out on staged intake sessions**, by analogy with `drainLeadQueue`. Of 74 pending sessions **exactly two** are older than 30 days — and both are the best items in the queue, *"IAN Acquisition & EMP/Microgrid IDIQ Pursuit"* and *"Fort Polk Military & Infrastructure Pursuits"*, each carrying Ber AI's own `create` recommendation. **The analogy fails because a bid invitation is perishable and a staged acquisition is not.** The queue is not undrained; it is the working set.
  3. **An "unrouted deal mail" worklist on `/decide`.** All 112 such threads are already in a staged cluster: **69 appear on /decide already** as their cluster's intake session, **43 were declined**. Zero invisible work — the section would have been pure duplication, which is the clutter the whole pass is against.
- **Verified against live Gmail, live Drive and the live DB, not fixtures:** 40 unit checks (both status enums incl. NULL and an unknown future value, the prefilter's spoof/human-reply/bid-invitation cases, exclusions for both pipelines, and that a display name cannot break the From header apart); a full **dormant → live round-trip on a real project** (on_hold: `dormantSkipped:1`, nothing posted, no notification, count NOT advanced, rotated to the back — then active: update posted, notification raised with a readable body, count advanced) with **0 residue**; a real Gmail label+archive **and un-archive** round-trip read back out of Gmail (UNREAD and CATEGORY_UPDATES untouched, restored identical); a real Chat post delivered; and **a real email sent from the platform, read back, and confirmed skipped by the prefilter** — `From name: "Ber Intelligence"`, verdict `Generated by Ber Intelligence`. `tsc` + `next build` clean; **eslint 27 problems, zero of them in any file touched** (all pre-existing `set-state-in-effect` / `no-img-element` / `purity` / `scripts/`). Deployed twice, tailnet serve asserted, all 8 sweep phases confirmed running on the deployed build.
- **⚠ WORTH KNOWING: the platform cannot clean up after itself in moose@/tuaone@ — by design.** Trashing the one test message returned **403 insufficient scopes**: the deal mailboxes hold read-only Gmail scopes and only `info@` carries `gmail.modify` (`LEAD_ONLY_SCOPES`). That is the scope tiering working, and it is exactly why labelling/archiving the deal mailboxes was NOT built — it would need the broadest Gmail grant in the file plus a re-consent, to tidy an inbox that is Richard's rather than the platform's. **One test message remains in moose@**, subject *"Ber Intelligence self-loop verification …"*, body says it is safe to delete; the prefilter will skip it forever.
- **⚠ Richard's manual steps:** (1) delete that one test message if it bothers you; (2) the **27 opportunity documents still `pending`** are unchanged and remain the pre-existing M&A call from 2026-09-22 (indexing them makes the Englert/Gold mine deal searchable); (3) the **74 pending intake sessions are the real queue** — measurement says they are recent and substantive, not stale, so the lever is deciding them on `/decide`, not automating them away.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** deleting the 743 noise threads or the 886 junk leads (both are the audit trail for the filters, and the standing posture is keep the row, gate the behaviour); labelling/archiving the deal mailboxes (see the scope note above); loosening the routing guards to raise the 7.5% link coverage (the ambiguity margin and two-token gate exist because relaxing them misfiled mail); and auto-creating records from mail — **§11 stands, unchanged**.

**Done 2026-09-22 (simplification pass — the queue had no drain, the router asked twice, nine date formatters; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's question: anything else to simplify and streamline.** Measured the live database rather than reading the build log, and the answer was not a code-size answer.
- **⚠ THE PLATFORM GENERATES DECISIONS FASTER THAN ANYONE MAKES THEM, AND NOTHING CONVERTS.** Of **1,216 leads ever scored, ZERO have ever been promoted** to a project, opportunity or steel deal; **97 pursue/consider leads expired unreviewed**; intake stands at 5 confirmed against 73 pending; the Decide queue held **282 items**. The pipeline makes ~4,000 model calls a month and the human end has never once said yes. The leads are *good* — Hill AFB B509, U of U Arapeen, a UDOT PEMB routed to steel, fit 72-85 — so this is a drain problem, not a filter problem.
- **⚠ AND THE DRAIN COULD NOT REACH MOST OF THE QUEUE: `expireStaleLeads()` filtered `.not('bid_due_date','is',null)`**, so a lead with NO bid date could never satisfy it — and **69 of the 73 open pursue leads carried no bid date**. Its own comment claimed it "drains the queue"; for the overwhelming majority the rule never fired. Measured further: only 24 of those 69 threads even mention a deadline, because a bid invitation keeps its dates behind a plan-room login. They are structurally undatable, so an age-out is the ONLY thing that can ever close them. `drainLeadQueue()` keeps the dated rule and adds three: undated leads age out at 30 days, `pass` leads close as `ignored`, and a job posted twice collapses. **Live: 99 open → 76, idempotent on re-run, 0 promoted/forwarded touched, all 23 changes re-labelled in Gmail.**
- **⚠ AGE IS MEASURED FROM `created_at`, NOT `updated_at`** — the `update_updated_at` trigger fires on re-scoring, re-labelling and Gmail sync, so an undated lead's `updated_at` keeps moving and it would never age out. Same class as measuring a stalled thread by a column its own writes bump.
- **⚠ THE DEDUPE ESTIMATE IN THE PLAN WAS WRONG AND MEASURING KILLED IT.** Titles that *look* duplicated ("Ground-Up Development" vs "Construction", "RFI Responses" vs "RFI Clarifications") are not, and a fuzzy matcher would sometimes merge two genuinely different bid packages. Exact normalised title within one route collapses **3 groups, 4 rows** — small, and deliberately so: a surviving duplicate is one click to dismiss, a silently closed distinct bid is lost. Sky Mountain Golf appears under **six** normalised keys because six different GCs invited us; not merging those is correct.
- **⚠ AN ALIAS MATCH IS NOW `linked`, NOT `inferred` — the single biggest cut to the review queue.** Setting "Also known as" is a person stating in advance that mail calling the deal that name belongs there: a filing decision expressed as a rule. Staging it for review asks the same question twice, which is the argument `/api/thread-links` already makes for hand-filed links. **All 75 unresolved review rows were this one reason, unread since 2026-09-09**, and 73 of 106 inferred links were aliases. Solicitation-number matches likewise (an identifier, not a similarity). Guards unchanged and re-verified by unit test: the **whole** alias must appear, and `AMBIGUITY_MARGIN` still refuses when two records share one. **Review queue 75 → 31**, and what remains is genuine name-similarity worth a human. **Decide 282 → 181.**
- **⚠ EMAIL WAS BEING INDEXED TWICE, AND THE CURATED COPY WAS WINNING.** `apply-phase` embedded every filed thread body into `chunks` — but the mail is already fully indexed in `thread_chunks`, which `search_correspondence` reads. That put raw email into the CURATED index: exactly the pollution the separate table was created for on 2026-09-14. Measured on a real question ("Sandpoint cottage permit set and lateral takeoff for 15 homes") the top three hits were **a URL tracking token at 0.740, a signature block at 0.697 and a message header at 0.673**, with the answer nowhere in the top eight. 197 chunks removed; the same query now returns the actual cottage permit drawing, and the mail still leads `search_correspondence` at 0.706. **Nothing lost, both halves better.**
- **⚠ NINE `formatDate` COPIES, EIGHT WITHOUT THE TIMEZONE FIX THAT EXISTS BECAUSE IT WAS A BUG.** Two of the eight formatted DATE columns — `tasks.due_date` through `dueLabel`, and `objectives.target_date` on the steering board — so **a task due the 1st rendered as the 30th** west of UTC. One implementation now, with `empty`/`year` options that exist only to preserve what each screen already displayed. `formatBytes` had six copies and three behaviours, so 800 bytes read "800 B" on one screen and "1 KB" on another. `ConversationList`'s copy is renamed `formatRelative` — it returns "Yesterday", so it was never the same function.
- **⚠ TWO NOTES TABLES TOOK THE AUTHOR'S NAME FROM THE REQUEST BODY** and shipped a "Your name (optional)" box to fill it, so a note could be posted under anyone's name. The other four stamp it server-side with a comment saying why. Fixed.
- **⚠ THE OPPORTUNITY UPLOAD ROUTE WAS THE LAST FORK OF THE DOCUMENT AI PASS**, against an explicit rule in `document-pipeline.ts` ("never fork this pass per table; add a target"). It summarized before storing the extracted text inside a bare `catch {}` — the 2026-09-09 failure that cost 413,000 characters. **Rewiring it surfaced a live bug: `runDocumentAiPass` RETURNED 'complete' without ever writing it.** `embedDocument` sets `embedding_status` as a side effect; `embedOpportunityDocument` does not, and the column only reached that table on 2026-09-21 — so **no opportunity document had ever been marked complete** (34 pending / 3 processing / 0 complete), which the health card reads as passes dying. Status is now settled by the function rather than by whichever embedder it called; 10 rows that had finished but were never marked are corrected. **27 genuinely unindexed opportunity documents remain and are now visible as such.**
- **⚠ 56 API GUARDS PASSED WHEN THERE WAS NO VIEWER AT ALL.** `getViewer()` returns null for an unauthenticated request, so `viewer && !viewer.isAdmin` was false exactly when nobody was there. Nothing was exposed — the middleware 401s first, verified live — but it is the wrong default for the layer whose job is not to trust the one above. 44 plain checks, 12 compound (narrowing preserved), 8 early returns. **The 28 routes with no in-route guard were DELIBERATELY LEFT**: cross-referencing every one against `ROLE_API_PREFIXES`, exactly 3 are reachable by a non-admin (`/api/objectives*`, for `executive`) and that is intended. The invariant that safety rests on a restrictive allowlist — and that `matchesPrefix` is **not method-aware**, so allowlisting a read grants every mutation under it — is now written where it will be read.
- **A project shows the tabs it actually has.** Across all 15 projects, Diligence, Financing, Entities & Vendors and Meetings had rows on **zero**, Milestones and Tasks on one each, while every page rendered ten tabs. Tabs with rows show with their count; the rest wait under More. The tab components are untouched — hiding is reversible. The tab you are standing on always renders, or picking an empty one made it vanish from under you.
- **The weekly brief cron reported failure on every success.** It was the only one of thirteen agents not at exit 0. It declared no `maxDuration`, so curl cut at 290s while the handler kept running (Next does not abort on client disconnect) and wrote the brief anyway. Now 1800s, matching the Drive crons and above `LOCAL_AI_TIMEOUT_MS`.
- **Deleted with zero callers, each verified not inferred:** `utils/activity.ts` (body was `export {}`), `api/ai/classify` + its orphaned 91-line prompt, `api/webhooks/procore`, `api/ai/queries`, `api/eval/report`, `api/steel/source-folder`, seven unused `ui/` primitives, and the `/attention` `/capacity` `/email-research` redirect stubs. Migration `20260922000001` drops six steel columns (APPLIED). **Routes 153 → 148, pages 72 → 69.** `SYSTEM_USER_ID` was redeclared in ten files and now lives in one.
- **⚠ WORTH KNOWING: ACROSS 4,397 LOGGED AI CALLS THERE ARE ZERO RATINGS**, and `api/eval/report` — the only reader — was unreachable. `api/eval/rate` and its thumbs control are left alone (removing a live affordance is a product call), but it is now a write path with no reader. Separately, **Ber AI has taken 34 questions in three months** against 42 tool declarations costing ~18% of the context window every turn, at least six of which can never return anything. My read is that it is unused *because* it is slow.
- **⚠ THE INVESTORS MODULE WAS NOT TRIMMED, AND THE EVIDENCE IS WHY.** It was approved for trimming on the strength of `investments = 0` — but the backup showed `raises` holds a hand-entered **$88M plan across four tranches (25/25/25/13M) with target dates**, beside 7 investors and 18 lender documentation requirements. That is a module mid-setup, not an abandoned one, and removing the ledger would leave a tranche plan with no way to record progress against it. Richard confirmed: leave it alone. **Rule: check what a table CONTAINS, not just how many rows it has, before calling it unused.**
- **Also measured and deliberately not changed:** the health page (1.4s against 0.08-0.2s elsewhere, from ~9 live Google calls — but splitting it adds a mode and a button to a page three people open occasionally, and caching it defeats the reason you refresh it); `cron-drive-watch` vs `cron-drive-sync` (one endpoint, two schedules with different budgets — merging means one schedule choosing a budget by time of day, more logic not less); `notify/` vs `notifications/` (confusing names, genuinely different things — outbound channels versus the in-app bell); `lib/utils.ts` vs `lib/utils/` (81 importers of `cn()`, the shadcn convention).
- **CLAUDE.md §7 was wrong in both directions:** four `MICROSOFT_*` vars documented with zero references since the 2026-08-23 Google move, and `MICROSOFT_SECRET_EXPIRES` claimed to drive a health warning the page never read — while **twelve live vars went undocumented**, including the entire Google credential surface. `getAccessToken()` picks its MODE from which of those are set, so the valid set is mode-dependent; that is now stated, because asserting one specific var in a health check has already shipped as a bug once.
- **Verified:** `tsc` and `next build` clean throughout; eslint **at 20, one below the documented 21 baseline, unchanged by any of this**; unit tests for the title normaliser (11 cases), the date formatter (9), and match certainty (5, including the ambiguity refusal and a partial alias); live round-trips for the drain (idempotent), the router (a fresh alias match routes `linked` with `applied_message_count` 0, then restored), the opportunity AI pass (text + summary + chunk + status, **0 residue**), and the steel insert without the dropped columns. Authenticated renders confirmed the tab bar on two real projects and 401/200 on six routes. **All test data removed — 0 residue.** Backups taken and read back before every destructive step.
- **⚠ Richard's manual step:** 27 opportunity documents have never been indexed and are now correctly visible as `pending` — they are M&A material (the Englert/Gold mine files), so indexing them would make that deal searchable. The uncommitted lead→task work found in the tree was two edits short of running (`TaskRow` had no `lead_id`, and `TASK_COLUMNS` did not select it, so the Lead tag would silently never have rendered); both are fixed and it is committed.

**Done 2026-09-21 (every project linked to a Drive folder; aliases set from evidence — the two levers pulled; DATA + FOLDERS, no code):**
- **Richard's instruction: link the remaining 9 projects and set the aliases** — the two manual levers the filing build had flagged as worth more than further code.
- **⚠ THE SUGGESTION RANKING WAS CONFIDENTLY WRONG AND WOULD HAVE LINKED EVERY PROJECT TO ITSELF.** Running `/api/drive/suggest`'s own logic returned a **1.00 exact-name match for six of the nine** — every one of them `My Drive / Ber Intelligence / Projects / <the record's name>`, which is **the platform's own publish target**, created by `publishRecordToDrive` and named after the record. Of course it scores 1.00: we named it. Linking a source folder to it would have pointed the importer at the platform's own output. **A perfect name match is the expected signature of a folder we created ourselves — resolve the full parent chain before trusting a score.**
- **The honest finding: 8 of the 9 had no team folder at all.** Only `Myton Compute Campus` (30 files, under `General Contracting / Build Projects / USA | Canada / Utah`) was a real unlinked team folder, and nothing had ever pointed at it. Box Elder, Case Garage, Camp Williams, Homestead, Tonga, Zero Point and AERC exist nowhere in the shared drive; Pre-Fab Steel SLC's only near-hit was `Manufacturing / Ber Wilson - Prefab Steel`, which holds **commission plans and sales training** — a business line, not a project, and linking it would have imported training material onto a project.
- **So the 8 folders were CREATED, placed by business line** (Richard's pick over a flat shelf): government/defense work under `Military Developments`, geography under `General Contracting / Build Projects / USA | Canada / {Utah, Idaho}` and `/ APAC`, steel under `Prefab Steel Projects / Utah`. `ensureFolder` is find-then-create and verified correct on this shared drive, so re-running cannot produce a second copy; path resolution matches on the **trimmed** name because folders here carry trailing spaces (`"Utah "`, `"Prefab Steel Projects "`). All 8 confirmed `canAddChildren: true`. **Projects linked to a Drive folder went 6 of 15 → 15 of 15.**
- **⚠ `Myton Compute Campus` WENT TO MYTON DEVELOPMENT, NOT MYTON RAIL, AND THE REASONING IS WORTH KEEPING.** Its rail subfolder holds exactly the three documents already on Myton Rail (CRISI decision brief, Crisi cover page, StracNet UBUR submission), which argues it is Myton Rail's — but Myton Rail is **already linked** to `Myton - Utah`, and re-pointing it would make the 9 documents filed there this morning vanish from the new listing. `reconcileVanished` would see 9 of 10 gone, **correctly refuse as a folder problem**, and report it every night forever. The folder's own centre of gravity (`Business Plan Myton 1` — Combined Master Plan, Community Benefits Plan, Ute Tribe Engagement) matches Myton Development's "large-scale master plan" description. **Flagged, unchanged: the two Myton records overlap heavily and may be one deal — that is Richard's call, not something to infer from a folder name.**
