- **Aliases were derived from what the mail actually says, not invented.** Read the summarizer's own `deal_name` across every unrouted thread first; the corpus is **long-tail** (almost every name appears once), which is itself the finding — there is no large pool of mail waiting on a single missing alias. Each candidate was then checked for collisions against every other record's name and aliases before anything was written: **zero collisions**. 23 new aliases across 11 projects and 2 opportunities. **`Myton` was deliberately NOT given to Myton Development** — it collides with Myton Rail and the guard would refuse it anyway.
- **Result: 55 threads requeued, 25 newly matched, 10 correctly refused as ambiguous.** Every match was audited by hand before applying and all were defensible: `IAN` pulled 12 genuine acquisition threads (SBIR Phase 3, Qlocity MNDA, the DocuSigned staged-control LOI, DTRA proposal package), `Sandpoint` pulled 7 onto Zero Point (cottage permit set, lateral takeoff for 15 homes), `Lahaina` reached the Hafoka residence, `Antelope` and `Uintah Basin` reached Myton Rail. **21 documents imported onto records that had never seen them.** Matches are `inferred`, so they stage as pending with a review-queue row — the designed posture, not an oversight.
- **Worth knowing: a third copy of the Giovanni material sits in personal Drives** — `Helper UT / Giovanni Resilience Campus` owned by tuaone@ and `Helper UT / DUBHES Helper / …` owned by moose@, both outside the shared drive. DUBHES is linked to the shared-drive `PQC Giovanni Campus`, so these are not imported; they are the cross-source duplication problem in a new place.
- **⚠ Still the ceiling, and unchanged by any of this: the remaining stranded attachments are in threads whose deals have no record at all** — "Idaho cottage development", "5-Story Building", "USACE Contracts", "Fort Polk contract", "Davis-Monthan AFB MC-130J Hangar", "Bethpage Groundwater Treatment Plant". No alias reaches them because there is nothing to alias them to. Creating records is a judgement about what is a real pursuit, which is Richard's, not the platform's.

**Done 2026-09-21 (documents file themselves into the TEAM'S own Drive folders, in the right subfolder; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: pull documents out of email and put them in the correct subfolder both in the platform and in Drive, hands-off, with a miscellaneous folder for anything that doesn't obviously belong.** He showed the `PQC Giovanni Campus` folder — Deeds, Land Legal, Previous Land Offers, Pre Construction… — and asked whether the platform can see and create in Drive as needed.
- **⚠ THE PREMISE WAS MEASURED BEFORE BUILDING, AND ONE HALF OF IT WAS WRONG.** That subfolder structure is **aspirational, not current**: of 15 projects, **exactly one** has it. Most project folders have no subfolders at all (Myton, HAFB, Vallejo, Hafoka, Mira Vista, Camilla); the ones that do use sets specific to that deal — Tooele Steel House has `Business Documents | GET Turkiye Cold Form Steel Acquisition | USACE Fort Johnson Contract 20MM Design Build`, IAN has `Partners & Acquisitions`, West Wendover has `Due diligence`. **A single hard-coded taxonomy would have been wrong for 14 of 15 projects.** Hence hybrid (Richard's pick): classify against the record's OWN folders, and create a standard set only where there are none.
- **Write access was never the blocker.** `canAddChildren: true` on every one of those subfolders, `drive.file` + `drive.readonly` already granted. Confirmed the 09-16 finding generalises: on a SHARED drive `drive.file` governs which files the app may touch afterwards, while adding a child is the impersonated user's own right. **`canDelete` is false, so cleanup trashes rather than deletes.** Also verified `ensureFolder`'s find-half returns the right folder on the shared drive, so it will **not** create duplicates there — that had never been exercised, since the steel-quote feature only ever uploaded.
- **⚠ FOLDERS ARE LINKED AT THE BUSINESS-LINE LEVEL, NOT THE PROJECT LEVEL — found by running it, not by reading the schema.** DUBHES points at `Helper - Utah`, whose single child `PQC Giovanni Campus ` is the folder actually holding the taxonomy. Filing against the link as given would have offered "PQC Giovanni Campus" as the one candidate and never reached Deeds or Land Legal at all. New `resolveFilingRoot`: **a folder holding NO files and EXACTLY ONE subfolder is a shelf, not a filing choice** — there is no decision to make there, so step through it (bounded at 3 hops). A folder with files of its own is somewhere people already file, so it stays put however many subfolders it has. Verified against all six linked projects: Helper and Trump City descend correctly, **Stockton (7 loose files plus two subfolders) correctly does not.**
- **⚠ THE LOOP GUARD IS THE LOAD-BEARING PART, and `import.ts` had predicted this exact day in a comment:** *"the day a project's source folder and its published folder are the same one, every published file would return as a duplicate of itself."* Verified against the real skip sets: `published` is built from `drive_published_id NOT NULL **AND drive_file_id IS NULL**`, so stamping only `drive_published_id` would **not** have protected anything. A filed document therefore stamps **all three** — `drive_file_id`, `drive_modified_at`, `drive_published_id` — and the importer finds it in `known` and short-circuits. **The timestamp matters as much as the id**: `driveFileUnchanged(null, …)` is false, so a row filed without it is re-downloaded, re-summarized and re-embedded every night. `uploadToFolder` never requested `modifiedTime`; it does now. **Proven live: `added=0, unchanged=1, updated=0`, one row, stable across repeated imports.**
- **The classifier refuses rather than guesses.** `chooseFolder` accepts a folder name **only if it is verbatim one of the candidates** — no fuzzy matching, no trimming into a match (near-misses in this codebase have historically been trailing-space bugs). Below `FILING_CONFIDENCE = 0.7` it goes to **`_Unsorted`**, because a document misfiled into a folder the team trusts is worse than one waiting to be sorted: nobody goes looking for a file they believe is already filed. A **deterministic filename pass runs first** so routine documents cost no model call at all, and it defers the moment two candidate folders match — ambiguity is the thing the whole module exists to refuse. Measured live against the real PQC taxonomy: broker opinion → `Independent RE Brokers Market Analysis` (0.95), geotech → `Pre Construction` (0.90), `Scan_0043.pdf` → `_Unsorted` (0.10), and an HR handbook correctly refused outright. Model calls are **5–18s**, not the 30–60s feared, because the prompt is small.
- **⚠ TWO FIXES THAT ONLY THE DRY RUN OVER REAL DATA REVEALED.** (1) `invite.ics` files arrive as email attachments and became documents — four of them were about to be filed into project folders. Non-documents (`.ics`/`.vcf`, `text/calendar`) are now skipped; the denylist is deliberately narrow, since a `.dwg` is unreadable to the platform and still absolutely a document. (2) **Platform-generated summaries were being filed into folders a human curated** — `Email research — Helper.md` was headed for `Master Proposal - Land Development` at 0.70. They now go to `_Unsorted` whenever the taxonomy is the team's own. **`isStandardSet` compares the folder set by content rather than trusting the `created` flag**, which is true only for the very first document filed and would have made the first generated summary behave differently from every later one.
- **Opportunities could not be linked to a Drive folder at all** — the column existed on projects and steel_deals and was simply missing here, so the M&A deals (exactly where loose documents arrive by email) could not participate. Migration `20260921000001_drive_filing.sql` (**APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded**) adds `opportunities.drive_source_folder_id/_url`, `drive_folder_path` on both document tables, and brings `opportunity_documents` to parity (`drive_file_id`, `drive_modified_at`, `embedding_status`, `superseded_at`, unique index on the Drive id).
- **Fixed alongside: an opportunity's email attachments got no summary, no extracted text and no embedding.** `importNewAttachments` ran `runDocumentAiPass` for projects and merely inserted for opportunities. And `setStatus` returned early for `opportunity_documents` "because it has no embedding_status column" — true until this migration, and the reason an opportunity document could never be seen as stranded and so was never retried.
- **`drive_folder_path` finally persists what the importer always knew.** `listFolder` has computed each file's subfolder path all along and used it only for the arrival notice; it is now stored, so the platform knows the team's own taxonomy for a record rather than rediscovering it.
- **Destination branches inside `publishRecordToDrive`**, which keeps all five existing callers correct for free and is the only way to guarantee a document never goes to both places: filing stamps `drive_published_id`, so whichever pass runs first, the other skips it. Records with no linked folder keep publishing to `Ber Intelligence` exactly as before.
- **Verified against live Drive and the live DB, not fixtures:** 15-case classifier check + 17-case rules check; a full scratch tree on the real shared drive exercising both taxonomy branches (existing folders reused with **no standard set imposed**; a bare folder given the set **once** and reused on the next run), already-filed documents skipped, an unlinked record a clean no-op, and **0 residue** afterwards. Then the real thing: **31 documents filed across 5 projects**, read back **out of Drive** — the NDA sitting in `Land Legal` beside what the team filed by hand, the proposals in `Master Proposal - Land Development`, `_Unsorted` created only where something genuinely could not be placed. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint **20 problems, one BELOW the documented 21 baseline**.
- **New `Drive Filing` card on `/settings/health`**, reporting coverage rather than pass/fail — filing is best-effort and never fails the import that called it, so an unfiled document otherwise looks exactly like a quiet week. The documents tab shows each file's Drive folder as a chip, amber for `_Unsorted`.
- **⚠ THE REAL CEILING IS THREAD ROUTING, NOT FILING — and it is where the remaining value is.** Of 449 threads carrying attachments, only **52** are linked to a project or opportunity: **128 attachments reachable, 789 sitting in unrouted threads**. Filing cannot place a document whose email was never attached to a record. **Richard's two levers, both already built:** link the **9 of 15 projects with no Drive folder** (the picker ranks the likely folder — one click each, though `Myton - Utah` and `Stockton, Utah` are genuinely ambiguous between two records and the guard will correctly refuse them), and **set `match_aliases`** on the records that have none (American Energy Rail, Case Garage, Myton Development, Myton Rail, Pre-Fab Steel SLC). Do both before judging the output, or it will look like the feature is doing very little when the constraint is upstream.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** nested subfolder classification (`Land Legal` itself has 4 children — classifying into paths multiplies the failure modes, and a human moving a file deeper is a cheap correction); two-way sync (Drive must never become a second source of truth); filing for steel deals (`steel_deals.drive_source_folder_id` exists and **nothing reads it** — a genuine dead end, worth closing separately).

**Done 2026-09-20 (continued audit — the sweep's last three phases had never run; retrieval had no way to scope a question; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **⚠ THE WORST FINDING OF THE PASS, AND IT WAS IN A LOG, NOT THE CODE: across 211 logged hourly sweeps, the ROUTE, APPLY and EMBED phases had never run once.** All three are in `ALL_PHASES` and the built bundle contained them; the serving process simply predated the deploy that added them, and **`next start` loads the build at boot — a rebuild without a `launchctl kickstart` changes nothing.** So for roughly nine days correspondence was routed, filed and indexed **only when a human ran it by hand**, which is exactly why 63 threads sat unrouted at the start of this session and summaries that failed on 2026-08-28 were never retried. Confirmed fixed by invoking the cron directly: all eight phases now run.
- **⚠ AND NOTHING WOULD EVER HAVE SAID SO — the check that should have caught it measured the wrong thing.** `probeThreadRouting` warned only above a **1,000-thread** backlog, and the backlog sat at a few dozen. **A count that has stopped growing is indistinguishable from a quiet inbox.** Age is not: routing is deterministic and clears hundreds of threads in seconds, so a thread summarized six hours ago and still unrouted can only mean the phase is not running. The check now says precisely that and names the restart to try. **Verified by reproducing the real state** — and the first attempt at that test silently passed, because the `set_updated_at` trigger bumps `updated_at` on the very write that un-routes a thread; reproducing it needed `session_replication_role = replica`. **A test that cannot fail is not a test.**
- **⚠ RETRIEVAL COULD NOT BE SCOPED TO TWO OF ITS OWN RECORD KINDS.** `match_chunks` filtered by project or entity and nothing else, so: an **opportunity** — a first-class record, one of which carries 260 chunks — could not be scoped at all (the 09-19 opportunity brief had to work around it by pulling vectors into Node and ranking them by hand); and there was **no company-only mode**, because `filter_include_company` is an OR branch that only widens a project-scoped search and is therefore **dead code when the project filter is empty**. Measured: asked "what certifications do we hold", the top hit was a Stockton question-tracker CSV header at 0.575 while the credential material sat at rank 17. Migration `20260919000001` adds `filter_opportunity_ids` and `filter_company_only`, both defaulted so every existing 5- and 6-argument call is unchanged (**verified live**). Company-only searches 924 chunks instead of 2,706 and returns the USACE CQM credential and the Utah licences. `company-knowledge.ts` drops its **`NO_PROJECT_SENTINEL`** — a fake UUID passed purely to force the RPC down its company branch — for the real filter.
- **⚠ MEASURED BEFORE BUILDING, AND IT KILLED THE OBVIOUS FIX.** Four chunks were appearing in 7–9 of 10 *unrelated* queries (a CSV header of empty cells, a flattened org-chart page, a 44-character cover page). The tempting move is to detect and penalise these "semantic magnets" — either by prose ratio or by distance from the corpus centroid. **Both were wrong, and measuring showed why:** a <0.10 prose filter would have dropped 321 chunks including the platform's own generated investor snapshots and real data tables, and the chunks *closest to the centroid* turned out to be **the executive brief, the capability statement and the credentials section** — the most important company documents in the index, near the centroid precisely because the whole corpus is about Ber Wilson. Penalising hubness would have deleted the right answers. The real defect was the missing scope, not the chunks.
- **`get_record_brief`** — the deterministic assembler exposed as ONE agent tool (fuzzy name resolve, ambiguity refusal with candidates, 26k-char cap), because the 09-19 pass built it and then still left the agent choosing fifteen retrievals for itself. **And because tool choice is nondeterministic, the fact that corrects a stale answer now travels inside the structured tools too:** `query_project_data`, `query_opportunity`, `query_steel_deal` and `query_lead` all carry an authoritative recency block — filed thread count, newest filed date and subject, and an explicit instruction never to date silence earlier than it. Verified live on all four kinds.
- **The Decide queue can now be cleared without leaving it.** 238 items, each previously costing a navigation round trip; a queue that expensive is one people stop opening — the same argument the notification design makes about a bell that fires for everything. Each row gains a dismiss control wired to the **existing** endpoints (a lead becomes `ignored`, kept so the triage stays auditable; a session is dismissed; a flagged extraction is rejected). Optimistic with revert-and-toast, because a row that vanishes without being written reads as handled when it was not. The control is a **sibling of the row's link, never nested** — a button inside an anchor is invalid markup. All three round-tripped live against real records, then restored, **0 residue**.
- **Health names the records the router cannot see.** Aliases are now the lever that pulls stored mail onto a record, but nothing said WHICH records needed one. The Email → Records check now names open projects and opportunities with **no alias and no filed correspondence** — live: Dino Plumbing, Gold mine, IAN (EMP) Technology aquisition, Myton Development.
- **Fixed: one momentary network blip killed a whole Drive sync, ten times over.** `googleRequest` retried HTTP statuses but a network-level `TypeError: fetch failed` escaped the loop entirely, so the Studio's DarkWake windows produced runs with **all 106 knowledge files failed**, each reading like a credential problem. Network-shaped failures now retry with the same backoff (token minting moved inside the loop, since it dies the same way); a genuine config error still throws immediately rather than making the operator wait out the backoff for the message they need.
- **Fixed: failed thread summaries were permanent.** Every observed failure is transient, yet the only retry lived behind an API flag nobody calls, so five threads sat failed since 08-28. `retryStaleFailures` now runs in the hourly sweep, bounded twice so a poison thread cannot eat the budget: **at most once a day per thread**, and **only mail under 30 days old** — `last_at` never moves on a dead thread, so it ages out of retry by itself. Live: 4 requeued, the lead-pipeline one correctly left alone.
- **Also:** the task-digest subject read "1 task need you" (every variant now agrees in number; the digest itself is working and will send Monday — 1 overdue task for Richard).
- **Swept clean, measured not assumed:** every one of **689 `.select()` sites** verified column-by-column against the live schema — **0 nonexistent columns** (the class that made a whole report section permanently blank in July); both tables with **two FKs to the same target** checked for PGRST201 ambiguity (`project_dependencies` correctly hints its constraint names; `steel_deals` never embeds `team_members`); no `::` casts in PostgREST filters; every `.neq()` target confirmed NOT NULL. Page loads **0.17–0.23s** across every major surface (health is 1.4s because it makes live network probes). Steel commission math re-verified against all six live deals — no deal reads 100% margin, nothing shows as owed on an uncollected deal. Agent fixed per-turn overhead is **21% of the window**, healthy.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint **20 problems, one BELOW the documented 21 baseline**; all ~30 authenticated routes 200 with no error markers after deploy; tailnet serve asserted; 0 unrouted threads; every `matchChunks` call-site shape exercised live (portfolio / project / opportunity / company-only / entity) plus the four retrieval tools.
- **⚠ THE DECIDE BADGE COUNTED A THIRD OF ITS OWN PAGE.** It predates the Decide surface — it was the Review Queue badge — and still counted `review_queue` alone, reading **68 against a page holding 219**. A badge that under-reports its destination by three and a half times sets an expectation the page immediately contradicts. New `countDecideItems()` mirrors the page's filters, including the one that is easy to get wrong: the page drops `pass` leads, and a plain `.neq()` would **also drop every unscored lead**, since PostgREST treats NULL as not matching — an untriaged bid invitation being exactly the thing that most needs deciding. The dashboard KPI read "68 in review" beside it, so **two numbers for one question sat on the same screen**; both now use the shared counter and the label says "waiting to decide". Verified badge and page agree exactly (219 = 72 intake + 79 leads + 68 review).
- **The Decide dismiss control shipped at 32px on mobile, below this app's own 44px bar** (set by the 08-27 mobile pass). Verified at a real 386px viewport via an iframe — **44×44, centre hit-tests to the button, no horizontal overflow**, and visible without hover since touch has none.
- **End-to-end validation on a record that was dark this morning:** asked for the status of the Hafoka residence, the agent returned the permit set, the NBE engineering quote with its expiry, the steel kit figure, the Unico HVAC quote, Dino declining the plumbing — and dated last contact to **2026-08-18**, which is exactly the newest filed thread ("Plumbing Estimate Needed"), confirmed against the database rather than taken on trust. None of that correspondence was filed before this session.
- **The Drive picker now SUGGESTS the folder instead of making you hunt for it.** Nine of fifteen projects have no Drive folder linked, so their documents are invisible to every search — and they were left unlinked because the names are genuinely ambiguous and the team's tree nests business line → project, making one folder three or four rounds of guessing. **That ambiguity is knowledge only a person has, so this suggests and never links:** new `findFoldersByName` searches Drive by the record's own name and aliases (Archive shelves dropped, parent name resolved), `GET /api/drive/suggest` ranks by name overlap, and the picker shows what actually distinguishes candidates — **parent path, file count, newest file**. Counts are fetched only for the shortlist, since `summarizeFolder` walks the tree. **Verified against the real Drive:** Homestead Commercial and Box Elder Quantum Campus each return one exact match at 1.00; Myton Development returns its own folder first with the weaker place-name matches below; **Zero Point correctly returns nothing strong, because no such folder exists**. Eyeballed in the picker. Nothing was linked — that stays Richard's call, and is now one click.
- **⚠ Richard's manual steps:** (1) **9 of 15 projects still have no Drive source folder linked**, so their documents are unreachable — linking stays deliberately manual because folder names are ambiguous, but the picker now ranks the likely folder for each, so it is one click per project from the Documents tab. (2) The four records named by the health check need aliases; note **"Myton Development" cannot simply take "Myton"** — it collides with Myton Rail and the ambiguity guard will correctly refuse it, so it needs a distinguishing short name. (3) Four steel deals still lack a site address and scope summary, so they cannot generate a quote.

**Done 2026-09-19 (audit pass — the router had no way back to old mail, opportunities had half a CRM, the weekly brief reached nobody; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: evaluate the whole app — architecture, UI, UX, bugs — and fix what we missed, with special attention to how we query the three sources (platform, email, Drive).** Build gauntlet was already clean (tsc 0, eslint at its documented 21, all 43 authenticated routes 200 with no error markers), so everything below came from exercising the platform against live data.
- **⚠ SETTING AN ALIAS DID NOTHING TO MAIL ALREADY READ — the 09-18 feature only worked going forward.** The router examines threads where `routed_at` is null, which is the right frontier (otherwise it re-reads the newest page forever), but it means an alias added today says nothing about the 2,112 threads routed yesterday. The 09-18 pass papered over this by re-running the router by hand; nobody else would know to. New `requeueThreadsForAliases` clears the marker on threads whose subject or summarized deal name contains a newly-added alias, fired from both project and opportunity save paths — and **only for aliases that are actually new** (snapshot before the write), so an unchanged save does not re-examine the mailbox. The prefilter is a deliberately coarse ilike: it decides only what is worth re-scoring, and the router's own guards still make the decision. Verified live — adding "Hafoka" requeued exactly 15 threads.
- **⚠ THE ALIAS FILE WAS ALSO NEARLY EMPTY, AND THAT WAS THE REAL CONSTRAINT.** Only Stockton had aliases; every other record was invisible to mail that used its short name. Measured first with a read-only dry run over all 1,753 unlinked threads, then set the 12 verified ones. **39 threads filed that nobody had filed, every one defensible**: 13 Hafoka (the whole Lahaina Maui residence — steel criteria, plumbing estimate, drywall, plans), 13 GridEdge (the entire MNDA negotiation), 6 DUBHES, 3 Helper/Giovanni, 2 Homestead, 1 Trump City. The ambiguity guard held throughout — "Myton" still matches nothing, because Myton Rail and Myton Development score identically. Apply then imported **25 attachments** onto those records.
- **⚠ OPPORTUNITIES HAD NO ALIAS FIELD AT ALL**, so the acquisition pipeline — where a deal is *always* called something shorter than its record name — could not participate in any of it. Added to the form (same `TagInput` contract as projects) and to `parseFields`, written only when the form carried the field so another door cannot wipe it.
- **⚠ AND OPPORTUNITIES HAD NO CORRESPONDENCE PANEL, though the entire chain already supported them** — `thread_links` takes a record kind, `/api/thread-links` accepts `opportunity` in its `KINDS`, `RecordCorrespondence` types the prop. Only the mount was missing, so a whole record kind silently lacked the feature its plumbing was built for. Mounted on the detail page; `loadFiledThreads` extracted to `src/lib/email-sweep/filed-threads.ts` and shared with the project Updates tab so the two cannot drift. Verified: 13 filed threads render, 3 suggestions offered.
- **Briefs now cover opportunities** (`assembleOpportunityBrief`). Same spine as projects — record fields, stated gaps, notes, documents by summary, verbatim passages, ALL filed correspondence plus a labelled semantic sweep, and a recency block measured from filed mail only — with the children that differ (notes not updates, `opportunity_documents`, no milestones/diligence/financing). The shared halves (`gatherCorrespondence`, `contactRecencyLines`, `correspondenceLines`) were **extracted from the project assembler rather than copied**, because the 09-17 `runDocumentAiPass` fork is exactly how two copies drift. Passages are cosine-ranked **in code**: `match_chunks` filters by project only, and an opportunity's chunk set is small (260 at the largest), so scoring in process costs less than a migration buys. New `/opportunities/[id]/brief/print`, and `GenerateBriefButton` generalised to a record kind. **Live on GridEdge: 16 documents, 13 filed threads, a 20k pack, 84s** — and the brief correctly leads with the rejected $5M SAFE, the staged IP counter-valuation and the MNDA venue/IP holdouts.
- **⚠ THE MIGRATION WAS INCOMPLETE AND ONLY RUNNING IT SHOWED IT.** Adding `stored_briefs.opportunity_id` typechecked, deployed, and then every insert failed on `stored_briefs_brief_type_check` — a CHECK from the original migration listing three legal values. The API logged it and returned the brief anyway (the storage write is deliberately best-effort), so the only symptom was a print page that stayed empty. **Rule: adding a discriminator column means checking for a CHECK on the discriminator.**
- **⚠ THE WEEKLY BRIEF WAS WRITTEN EVERY MONDAY AND READ BY NOBODY.** The dashboard panel loaded from **localStorage**, so the cron's brief appeared only in a browser that had personally generated one — on every other device the flagship morning read showed "No brief yet today". It also rendered the markdown as raw text (`##` and `**` printed literally) and marked a brief stale after one day, when the cadence went weekly on 08-31. Now server-rendered from the latest stored portfolio brief with its date, `BriefMarkdown` for rendering, an 8-day staleness bar, and manual generation persists so a refresh reaches everyone. Verified: the dashboard serves Sep 14's brief, TL;DR first.
- **⚠ ONE MOMENTARY NETWORK OUTAGE KILLED A WHOLE DRIVE SYNC, TEN TIMES OVER.** `googleRequest` retried HTTP statuses but a network-level `TypeError: fetch failed` escaped the loop entirely — so the Studio's DarkWake windows produced runs with **all 106 knowledge files failed**, ten times in the log, each reading like a credential problem. Network-shaped failures now retry with the same backoff (and token minting moved inside the loop, since it dies the same way); a config error still throws immediately rather than making the operator wait through the backoff for the message they need.
- **⚠ FAILED THREAD SUMMARIES WERE PERMANENT.** Every observed failure is transient — LM Studio unloaded, `{"error":"terminated"}`, a stalled stream — yet the only retry lived behind an API flag nobody calls, so five threads sat failed since 08-28. New `retryStaleFailures` runs as part of the hourly sweep, bounded twice so a poison thread cannot eat the budget: **at most once a day per thread** (`updated_at`), and **only mail under 30 days old** — `last_at` never moves on a dead thread, so it ages out of retry by itself. Verified live: 4 deal threads requeued, the lead-pipeline one correctly left alone.
- **⚠ `get_record_brief` — THE AGENT'S RETRIEVAL WAS STILL A COIN FLIP, and this is the load-bearing fix.** The 09-18 pass built a deterministic assembler and then left the agent choosing 15 retrievals for itself; asked where GridEdge stands, it called `query_opportunity` alone and answered from notes. The assembler is now ONE tool call (fuzzy name resolve, **ambiguity refusal with candidates**, 26k-char cap) and the prompt routes briefing questions to it first. **And because tool choice is nondeterministic, the fact that corrects a stale answer now travels inside the structured tools too**: `query_project_data` and `query_opportunity` carry an authoritative recency block — filed thread count, newest filed date, its subject, and an explicit instruction never to date silence earlier than it. Verified live on GridEdge: 13 filed threads, `2026-09-17`, "Re: MNDA for GridEdge (Next Steps)".
- **Queue honesty: 33 pending "updates" from the retired Outlook scraper (all 2026-05-05) had no review-queue row**, so `/review` could never surface them and nothing would ever settle them — they simply inflated the count forever. Settled as rejected; the reviewable set is now 66 and the number on screen is the number a human can act on.
- **Verified against live data throughout, not fixtures:** a read-only alias dry run before any write; the router re-run producing exactly the 39 threads the dry run predicted; the opportunity assembler and a full HTTP brief generation (84s) with the print page rendering from the stored row; `retryStaleFailures` exercised live; `get_record_brief` exercised on all three paths (hit, ambiguous, miss) plus a full local-model agent turn; the Google fetch path smoke-tested after the retry change; all 43 routes re-swept 200; the dashboard eyeballed over the tailnet with **zero console errors**. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint **at its documented 21-problem baseline, unchanged**.
- **⚠ Richard's manual step, still the lever: set aliases on the remaining records.** Twelve are set (Stockton, Hafoka, Helper/Giovanni/DUBHES, Wendover/Trump City, TEAD/Tooele Army Depot/Forge 42, Quantum Campus, Homestead, Camp Williams, Tonga, Zero Point, GridEdge, Golden Summit/True North). Every alias now pulls that record's old mail onto it **immediately on save** rather than at the next hand-run. Platform-wide linkage is 414 of 2,175 threads.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** briefs for steel deals and leads (the assembler is record-shaped and they have different children; the agent falls back to the documented four-part manual sweep, which the prompt now says); automatic alias suggestion (a machine guessing a record's short name is the ambiguity problem the guards exist to refuse); and retrieval-time changes to `match_chunks` (the dedupe-collapse added 09-18 measured clean, and `chunks` duplication is down to 2 groups, both Drive-vs-Drive and correctly left for the Archive gesture).

**Done 2026-09-18 (a brief that assembles its own evidence; correspondence reaches its record; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's question: three sources of truth (platform, Drive, Gmail) — is "tell me what I need to know about the Stockton project" architected to give the best possible answer, readable, read-aloud, exportable?** Measured before answering, and the architecture was sound in one half and missing a layer in the other.
- **⚠ THE AGENT ANSWERED THAT QUESTION WELL AND GOT ITS HEADLINE WRONG.** 135s, 15 tool calls, a genuinely good brief — the kill conditions, the Soldier Canyon water decline, the Jacobs Smelter exposure. Its **top-ranked risk** was "the town has gone quiet since June 23". **Eleven later threads existed**, including *"Response from Stockton Mayor"* from eleven days earlier. The mail was fetched, stored, and indexed, and `search_correspondence` returns it as the **top hit at 0.605** — the model simply reached for keyword thread search, read one thread of nineteen, and concluded. **Not a missing answer: a confident wrong one, in the house style, ranked first.** That is the failure this whole pass is designed against.
- **⚠ THE STRUCTURAL CAUSE: `thread_links` HAD NO READER.** The join table answering "what correspondence belongs to this record" existed, was populated, and **no agent tool and no page read it**. So every answer about a record was a fuzzy re-search of 2,162 threads. New **`get_record_correspondence`** reads it, and the agent prompt now says to run it *and* `search_correspondence` before describing any record as stalled, dating the claim from the later of the two.
- **⚠ AND THE FILE WAS NEARLY EMPTY: 369 of 2,162 threads linked (17%); Stockton had 1 of its 19.** The router is deliberately conservative (ambiguity means no match) and that is right, but the other eighteen threads say *"Stockton Project (Power Grid)"* or *"Water Questions from Ber Wilson"* — they never use the record's full name. New **`match_aliases`** on projects/opportunities: a human asserting the short form, which is evidence the matcher cannot derive. A one-word alias may match alone (a person said so) but **the ambiguity guard still refuses when two records share one** — verified live, "Myton" against Myton Rail and Myton Development matches nothing. **Stockton went 1 filed thread → 9, including the mayor's reply.** Edited on the project form via the existing `TagInput`.
- **⚠ THE `/api/ai/brief` PROJECT PATH READ EIGHT STRUCTURED TABLES AND ZERO DOCUMENTS AND ZERO EMAIL.** Stockton has 0 tasks, 0 milestones, 0 dd_items, 0 financing and no value — so the Generate Brief button produced almost nothing while 88,498 characters of proposal and 19 threads sat one table over. Replaced by **`src/lib/briefs/record-brief.ts`**, which assembles the pack in code — record, structured children, documents by AI summary, **all filed correspondence**, and a semantic sweep for unfiled mail — and leaves the model only the writing. **Same sources every time, 90s instead of 135s.** The old `PROJECT_BRIEF_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and its message builder are **deleted**, not left beside the new one.
- **⚠ RECENCY IS MEASURED FROM FILED MAIL ONLY — caught by reading the first real output.** The semantic sweep casts wide by design, and on the first run it surfaced an unrelated *"Pandora - St George Bid Reminder"* as the newest thread, which would have reported the deal as touched nine days ago when nobody had touched it. A loose match is worth listing; it is not evidence of contact. The pack now states the filed-mail date, tells the writer not to date silence earlier than it, and explicitly says matched-but-unfiled threads are not activity on this record.
- **Absence is stated, never implied.** The pack carries a RECORDED-DATA GAPS section and the prompt forbids reading an empty milestone table as a healthy schedule — it means the schedule is untracked. Open items visible only in correspondence are printed "not tracked".
- **⚠ DUPLICATES WERE SPENDING RETRIEVAL SLOTS, MEASURED ON THIS EXACT QUESTION.** Asked for Stockton's status, the top 6 chunks held the same passage **twice at 0.658 and twice at 0.642 — four of six slots for two documents**. Live counts: 262 duplicated rows in `chunks`, **964 in `thread_chunks` (17%)**. Two halves: retrieval-time collapse (over-fetch, dedupe, trim) in **both** `matchChunks` and `searchCorrespondence`, and `scripts/dedupe-documents.mts` for the rows themselves. **6 cross-source duplicates retired, 161 chunks freed**; after it, the same query returns 6 distinct sources including two that had been crowded out.
- **⚠ THE SCRIPT REFUSES TO RETIRE A DRIVE-SOURCED COPY, and this is load-bearing.** `importDriveFolder` treats a superseded document still present in its folder as "returning" and restores it, so superseding that copy is undone overnight. Where **both** copies are in Drive (2 of the 8 groups) it says so and leaves them for the Archive gesture — which is the documented convention and the only thing that works there. It reuses `supersedeDocument` rather than hand-rolling the two writes.
- **⚠ SUGGESTING UNFILED MAIL BY MEANING WAS THE WRONG TOOL, and running it proved it.** The first version reused the brief's semantic sweep and returned three unrelated bid invitations, while the thread holding the water constraint never appeared — a proposal reads like a proposal whatever it is for. Now a keyword scan over the record's **name and aliases** against subject and body: exactly the 10 remaining Stockton threads, **0.47s instead of 2.9s** (no embedding call). Names are resolved server-side from the id so a caller cannot pass one that disagrees with the aliases.
- **`POST/DELETE /api/thread-links` + a Correspondence panel on the project Updates tab.** A human-filed link is stored **`linked`, not `inferred`** — inferred means the platform wants agreement, and asking someone to review their own filing decision asks the same question twice. `applied_message_count` is seeded at the thread's current length so filing does not re-post the whole history into the record's feed. Admin-only by default-deny.
- **The brief prints and reads aloud.** `/projects/[id]/brief/print` renders the **stored** brief (a document carried into a meeting must be the one already agreed with, and generation has no business inside a print view), with a provenance line naming how many documents and threads it was built from. `BriefMarkdown` was extracted so the modal and the page share **one renderer** — two renderers of one document is two things to keep in step.
- **⚠ THE RESULT, VERIFIED ON RICHARD'S OWN QUESTION: it now finds what nobody knew.** Re-run, the agent called `get_record_correspondence` and `search_correspondence` and surfaced the mayor's 2026-09-06 message — uncomfortable with "what and how we reached this point", a local news outlet asking why a data centre is being built in Stockton, being named to Governor Cox without permission, and **asking to postpone the Oct 6 Planning Commission presentation**. Confirmed verbatim in the source, not hallucinated. That is a live relationship problem the platform could not previously see.
- **Verified against live data throughout, not fixtures:** 11-case unit check on alias matching and dedupe (incl. the ambiguity guard, and an alias not stealing another project's mail); the router re-run producing 9 filed threads; the assembler and the full brief generated end-to-end (90s, 22k-char pack, 33 sources); an authenticated HTTP run against the deployed app — `/api/ai/brief` 200 in 94s with all seven sections, the print page 200 and chromeless with its provenance line, the Updates tab rendering the panel, a **full file → re-file (idempotent) → 400/404 → unfile round-trip** leaving the count back at 9. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint **at its documented 21-problem baseline, unchanged**.
- **⚠ Richard's manual step, and it is the lever: set aliases on the other projects.** Only Stockton has them (`Stockton`, `Power Nexus`). Every project's Edit form now has an "Also known as" field, and each alias pulls that project's unfiled mail onto its record at the next sweep. Platform-wide linkage is still 375 of 2,175 threads — that number moves as aliases are set.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** briefs for opportunities and steel deals (the assembler is project-shaped; `get_record_correspondence` and the panel already take a record kind, so extending is adding a loader, not a design); a brief for the whole portfolio through this path (the existing portfolio brief is unchanged); and automatic filing of matched mail without a human — the suggestion list is one click, and §11 stands.

**Done 2026-09-17 (intake was silently discarding every Gmail attachment; records now publish to Drive at confirm; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's report: an Email Intake search for Taylor Englert created the opportunity perfectly but pulled in NONE of the many documents Taylor had sent, and he wants those syncing to Drive.** Both halves were real, and the first had a cause much larger than the one record.
- **⚠ THE PLATFORM HAD NEVER SEEN AN ATTACHMENT SENT FROM GMAIL.** `walkParts` classified a MIME part as inline chrome with `Boolean(contentId) || disposition.startsWith('inline')` — and **Gmail's own composer stamps a Content-ID (`<f_mu53shjq0>`) on EVERY file a person uploads.** Measured on the Englert threads: all 17 attachments carry an explicit `Content-Disposition: attachment` under `multipart/mixed` AND a Content-ID, so every one was discarded — a $-value PSA, an 11MB state lease, four technical reports, geophysics maps. `attachmentCount` was 0 on all four threads while the mailbox held 17 files.
- **The fix is that Content-Disposition is authoritative and checked FIRST** (`classifyAttachmentDisposition`, 11 unit cases). A Content-ID only counts as evidence when no disposition is stated **and** the part sits under `multipart/related` — the container that actually means "the body references these". Measured across 135 real messages in all three mailboxes: **60 files newly visible (40 PDFs, 4 .docx — NDAs, a development agreement, proposals, parcel abstracts), 125 still correctly inline and 100% of those images** named `image001.png` / `dev-global-jobs-logo.png`. **Zero PDFs or .docx remain misclassified, and nothing that was an attachment became chrome.**
- **⚠ THE BLAST RADIUS WAS EVERY ATTACHMENT PATH, not just intake:** the sweep's `apply-phase` (documents onto linked records), `score-phase` (lead bid packages), and `thread-embeddings` (attachment text in correspondence search) all filter on `isInline` and were therefore blind to the same files.
- **⚠ AND THE HISTORICAL CORPUS COULD NOT HEAL ITSELF: `email_threads.attachment_count` is a STORED GATE**, read by both `apply-phase` (`> 0` before importing) and `thread-embeddings` (`> 0` before indexing). A thread stored while the classifier was wrong holds 0 forever, so fixing the classifier only helps mail fetched from now on. New `scripts/recount-thread-attachments.mts` re-reads only threads Gmail itself reports via `has:attachment` (a safe superset), and clears `embedded_at` **only where the count GREW** — a thread that merely shed a misread logo has no unindexed content and must not cost a local-model pass. **Ran live: 566 checked, 174 grew, 0 shrank** ("EDCU Reports" 0→16, "GridEdge DC Package" 0→12, "Powers Mines" 0→11, "Parcel Abstracts" 0→7). Threads registering attachments went **291 → 441**; 174 queued for re-embedding, draining through the sweep's embed phase. Paced at 250ms/read because Gmail meters by cost-units-per-MINUTE and an unpaced run exhausts even `googleFetch`'s retry; a skipped thread keeps its old count, so re-running is the repair.
- **⚠ A SECOND COPY OF THE DOCUMENT AI PASS STILL HELD THE BUG FIXED ON 2026-09-09.** `attachments.ts` called `runDocumentAiPass` for project documents but carried a **hand-rolled copy** for opportunity ones — and that copy summarized BEFORE storing the extracted text, so a summary that threw took the text with it. Pointed at the Englert opportunity it discarded **413,000 characters** across three technical reports while reporting nothing wrong. The copy is deleted: `runDocumentAiPass` now takes a `target` (`documents` | `opportunity_documents` + its id), because the two tables differ in exactly three ways — no `embedding_status`, no `confidence`, a different embedder — and a parameter cannot drift out of sync the way a second copy did. **Rule: never fork this pass per table; add a target.**
- **⚠ A THIRD DEFECT: THE 15MB FULL-TEXT CEILING IS A GEMINI CONSTRAINT AND WAS BEING APPLIED TO THE LOCAL PATH.** Gemini receives the file base64-inlined in the request; local mode makes **no request at all** — `unpdf` reads the buffer in process. So a 20.5MB report holding 50,231 readable characters was stored with a summary and no text. Split into `PDF_FULLTEXT_MAX_BYTES` (15MB, Gemini) and `PDF_FULLTEXT_MAX_BYTES_LOCAL` (100MB, memory-bound). This bit **project** documents too: `PLANS Hafoka Residence` holds **141,322 characters** and had none stored.
- **Drive publishing now fires at CONFIRM, both intake doors.** The nightly reconcile would have caught these records — tomorrow morning. A record created at 9am should be readable by the team at 9am, and the team is precisely the people who cannot reach this tailnet-only platform. Best-effort and ahead of the AI pass (Drive carries the file, not the summary); the reconcile still retries anything that fails. Company-scoped meeting minutes are excluded deliberately — governance material, and `publishRecordToDrive` has no company shelf.
- **An empty attachment list is now STATED, not hidden.** The review screen rendered the section only when `attachments.length > 0`, which made "no files were sent" indistinguishable from "staging dropped every file" — exactly how this bug survived. It now says so, and names the two legitimate reasons (portal links, the 25MB staging cap).
- **Richard's record repaired with the production code, not a script's copy of it.** New `scripts/import-thread-attachments.mts` drives the real `searchThreads` → staging folder → `promoteStagedAttachment` → `processPromotedDocumentAi` → `publishRecordToDrive` chain (re-runnable; a file already on the record by name+size is skipped). **17 attachments imported onto the Alaska Golden Summit opportunity, 18 files (72MB) in Drive** at `Ber Intelligence / Opportunities / …`, confirmed `domain: berwilson.com, role: reader`. Plus `scripts/reindex-opportunity-documents.mts`, which `opportunity_documents` had no equivalent of (`documents` has `/api/documents/[id]/reindex`).
- **Verified against live Gmail, live Drive and the live DB — not fixtures:** 11-case classifier check; old-vs-new measured across 135 real messages; a fresh research-staging run on "NDA" staging **8 real documents where it would previously have staged 0**, with 0 residue after cleanup; **256 chunks across 6 documents** on the Englert opportunity and RAG confirmed retrieving the geology verbatim out of `True North Ore-Paul Jenson.PDF` at 0.676 for a question only that file answers; the 12 remaining files confirmed genuinely unreadable (scanned maps, images, one password-protected PDF — `PasswordException`). `tsc` + eslint clean on every touched file.
- **Backfill finished clean.** The unpaced first run left 5 threads on Gmail's per-minute quota; the paced re-run checked all 566 again with **0 failures** and found just **1 more** thread that had grown — so 175 total, and the pacing change is verified rather than assumed. Final state: **442 threads register attachments (was 291)**. Also repaired via `runDocumentAiPass`: both `PLANS Hafoka Residence` copies now carry 141,322 characters / 79 chunks each (they are the cross-source duplicate problem documented on 2026-09-09, not a new fault).
- **⚠ Worth knowing:** ~800 threads sit in the embed queue (the 175 re-queued plus an existing backlog), draining at 50/run through the hourly sweep over a few days — correspondence search improves as it does. And **12 of the 17 Englert files have no extractable text and never will**: the state lease and both geophysics PDFs are image-only scans, `SNL_Letter_Englert_Results.pdf` is **password-protected** (`PasswordException`), and the rest are photos. The files are on the record and in Drive; they are simply unreadable without a vision model.

**Done 2026-09-16 (one-click prefab steel quotes — template → Doc → PDF → the deal → Drive; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: connect to the drive holding the quotes, and generate a quote from a template with one click** — variable inputs like $/SF — making sure every deal carries the inputs a quote needs. His picks (AskUserQuestion): quotes land in the **platform's own Drive folder** while the team's folders are **read** (not the full `drive` scope), wording lives in a **Google Doc template** he edits without a deploy, the **two-rate shape** (materials package $/SF + installation $/SF + turnkey total) is universal, and **reps generate while a below-floor deal is marked DRAFT** rather than blocked.
- **⚠ MEASURED THE PREMISE BEFORE BUILDING, AND IT WAS STRONGER THAN STATED.** On the reference document (Mira Vista, 4 pages, 14 tables) the square footage appears **5 times**, the total **4 times**, and the installation rate **5 times across two different phrasings** (`$15.00/SF` twice, `$15.00 per square foot` three times). Retyping a rate and missing one occurrence is the real failure — a quote that contradicts itself is worse than a slow one. The two phrasings are why `{{INSTALL_RATE}}` and `{{INSTALL_RATE_PLAIN}}` are separate tokens.
- **⚠ THE DRIVE "HOLDING ALL THE QUOTES" DID NOT EXIST.** Searched live: **no file or folder named "quote" anywhere** moose@ can see. `Estimation & Quote Templates` holds two Sheets, neither prefab steel. The team's real home is `Prefab Steel Projects / Utah / <Deal>` — human-created, holding **plans**, with one prior quote PDF under Camilla. Mira Vista was hand-made in Word.
- **⚠ `drive.file` ALONE IS SUFFICIENT — the `documents` scope is NOT needed, and this build deliberately does not use it.** Verified by minting a token **narrowed to `drive.file`** (Google honours scope narrowing on refresh) and re-running everything: `documents.get` 200, `documents.batchUpdate` 200, `files.copy` 200, `export?mimeType=application/pdf` 200, `permissions.create {domain,writer}` 200. moose@ does hold `documents` (read+write on EVERY Doc in the account, granted Aug 2026 for a one-off repair, zero runtime call sites since) — building on `drive.file` means this feature survives a re-consent that drops it, and never becomes the reason to keep the broadest grant in the file. **`replaceAllText` reaches table cells AND the page footer** (the company phone lives only in the footer and was replaced correctly).
- **⚠ THE ONE REAL DEFECT: WORD→DOCS MERGES ADJACENT TABLES.** Converting the .docx straight over produced a **5-page** document with a near-blank page 3: the source has **14 tables and exactly 6 `</w:tbl><w:tbl>` adjacencies**, and the converted Doc had **8 tables** — each coloured callout absorbed as an extra ROW of the table above it, inheriting that table's narrow first column (**115pt instead of 523pt**). SCHEDULE BASIS, OWNER-CREW INSTALLATION and BER WILSON INSTALLATION all collapsed. **Fix: a 1pt empty paragraph between each adjacent pair** (`scripts/build-quote-template.mjs`, checked-in artifact `assets/steel-quote-template.docx`) → **14/14 tables, every callout full-width, back to 4 pages**, visually indistinguishable from the Word original. This removed the "re-author the callouts as Docs constructs" work entirely and dropped the top-ranked risk to minor.
- **The template is SEEDED FROM THE .docx AND TOKENIZED IN THE DOC, not in the XML.** Word splits a phrase across several `<w:r>` runs, so "44,400 SF" is rarely one string in the file; the Docs API matches rendered text instead. The `TOKENIZE` map's **order is load-bearing** (`44,400 square feet • 3 floors` before `for 44,400 square feet` before `44,400 SF`; `$15.00/SF` before `$15.00 per square foot`; `Company: Four Amigos…` before the bare company name). **It must be app-created** — `drive.file` is a per-file grant, so a Doc uploaded by hand could never be copied. Lives at `Ber Intelligence / Templates / Prefab Steel Quote Template`, shared **domain-writer on that one file** so wording changes need no deploy. Seeded live: **all 29 tokens present, zero leftover reference values.**
- **⚠ `steel_deals.install_fee` IS NOT THE CUSTOMER INSTALL RATE.** It is the flat **$250-500 paid TO THE SALESPERSON**; the quote's "$15.00/SF installation" is a **$666,000 customer price**. Three orders of magnitude apart, both plausibly "the install fee", and it ends up on a signed document. New `customerInstallPricePerSqft(lines, squareFeet)` derives it from the assembly lines — **no new column**, because `value` is already Σ line prices and a second stored copy would be free to disagree with the lines, with `value`, and with the commission math.
- **Cost leakage is structural, not disciplinary.** `QuoteInput.lines` is `{service_type, description, price, sort_order}` — **a type with no cost field, so a leak is a compile error** — and the frozen `steel_quotes.inputs` snapshot inherits the guarantee (steel_sales reps can read that table). The loader names its columns in one literal (`select('*')` would pull cost straight in). `quote-guard.ts` is the deliberate inversion: it takes the FULL row because it needs the cost figures in order to look for them, and exports the document's own `text/plain` to scan what a reader would see. Also added exact **`formatMoney`** — `formatValue` abbreviates, so the old quote page printed **"$1.8M" on a page a customer signs**.
- **Rounding rules that matter on paper:** total rate = `kitRate + installRate` (the two already-rounded values), **not** `round(total/SF)`, so the Rate column adds up when a customer checks it; amounts are authoritative and never re-derived from a rounded rate; the 5% milestone absorbs the remainder so the column sums exactly. `square_feet` of 0 **blocks** — a document whose whole shape is $/SF is not a quote.
- **Generate is cheap; ISSUE is the commitment — and the approval gate rides on that split with no new machinery.** A re-run **replaces a standing draft in place**; once issued, the next run is revision 2 and never touches the issued row's snapshot, Doc or PDF. Reps generate freely, but **issuing a below-floor quote requires `canSeeSteelFinancials`**. `{{DRAFT_BANNER}}` is a **footer prefix**, so "DRAFT — NOT FOR ISSUE" lands **once per page** (verified: 4 banners on a 4-page PDF, 0 on a clean one) and collapses to nothing otherwise; the filename carries DRAFT too, so it is recognisable in a Drive listing without opening it.
- **⚠ THREE BUGS FOUND BY RUNNING IT, NOT BY COMPILING IT:** (1) the settle-update's error was **unchecked**, so a failure left the row in `generating` and the partial unique index **latched the deal forever** — every later attempt reporting "already being generated"; (2) replacing a draft **inserted a twin row before retiring the old one**, colliding with the unique index on `(quote_number, revision)` — a replacement now **updates that row in place**; (3) a revision **drew a NEW quote number** instead of inheriting its parent's, which defeats the point of a revision.
- **⚠ READINESS FOUND A REAL PRODUCTION GAP: `company_profile.phone` WAS NULL**, so no quote could have been generated at all. Set to 385-436-5507 from Richard's own document. `quoteReadiness(deal, lines, estimator, company)` backs **both** the button's disabled state and the printed reasons — 8 blockers, 5 warnings — so they cannot drift.
- **Migration `20260916000001_steel_quotes.sql` (APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded):** `steel_quotes` (number from a **sequence**, not `max()+1`; `(quote_number, revision)` unique; a **partial unique index on `(deal_id) where status='generating'`** as the concurrency latch, aged out after 10 min so a crash cannot latch a deal permanently) plus five `steel_deals` columns — `site_address`, `floors`, **`scope_summary`** (deliberately NOT a reuse of `description`, the internal Scope & Notes field: "customer is desperate, hold the price" must never reach a signed document), and `drive_source_folder_id/_url` mirroring the projects convention. `documents` needed nothing — `steel_deal_id` and the widened `documents_scope_check` already cover a quote PDF.
- **The old printable HTML quote page is DELETED, not kept.** Two renderers of one document is two sources of truth, and that one was already wrong twice over (abbreviated money; `QuoteDate` computed **today in the BROWSER**, so a quote made at 11pm MST printed tomorrow's date for a reader elsewhere). `/steel/[id]/quote` is now the deal's **Quotes** surface, so the existing button and anyone's muscle memory still land somewhere sensible; `SteelQuoteToolbar.tsx` deleted (the org-structure print page has its own `ForceLightTheme`) and `layout.tsx`'s chromeless exclusion narrowed back to `/print`.
- **Verified against the real account and the deployed app, not fixtures:** 60 unit checks reproducing the Mira Vista document exactly ($33.00/$15.00/$48.00 per SF, $1,465,200 / $666,000 / $2,131,200, milestones 333,000/166,500/133,200/33,300 summing exactly); a live 6-part lib test (generate 8s → regenerate replaces the draft in place, 1 row + 1 PDF → issue → next run is rev 2 keeping the number → DRAFT filename → readiness blocks → **2 concurrent generates = 1×200 + 1×409**); and a full **authenticated HTTP run against the deployed app with a real minted session** (page 200 with the Generate button, generate 9.9s, PDF signed-URL 200, issue 200, **double-issue 409**, below-floor **rep 403 / admin 200**, notification raised, bad input 400). Generated PDFs re-read: **4 pages, 0 unreplaced tokens**, every figure in all five places. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint clean on every touched file (the 1 survivor is the documented pre-existing `prefer-const`). **All test data removed — 0 residue** across quotes/deals/documents/notifications and both test folders deleted from Drive.
- **SAME DAY — per-line $/SF pricing, and quotes that work without installation (Richard's follow-up; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
  - **His ask: price line items off $/SF by default (type $3, the total follows from the building SF), keep manual entry as an option, and handle that some projects have us assemble while others do not.**
  - **`steel_deal_services.price_per_sqft` is the RATE; the extended total is DERIVED SERVER-SIDE from it.** Lines default to **$/SF** with a per-line toggle to a lump sum — per-line because a deal legitimately mixes the two (steel by the foot, freight as a lump). Deriving in `parseFields` rather than trusting the form's hidden field means a stale or tampered value cannot put a wrong number on a quote, and **correcting the square footage re-derives every per-SF line** instead of leaving totals stating a figure that no longer follows from the size.
  - **⚠ THE DEAL-LEVEL Price/SF INPUT IS GONE.** It sat directly above the materials line that priced the same steel — one number in two boxes, free to disagree. The column survives (the detail page's money band and the floor check read it) but is now **derived from the materials lines**.
  - **Existing lines backfilled to the rate model — but only the 6 of 8 where `rate × size` returns the EXACT price already quoted.** Camila's $130,000 engineering line and the LA deal's $750,000 materials line do not divide cleanly (they were typed as totals), so they stay lump sums rather than being silently moved by a rounding trip.
  - **⚠ ONE TEMPLATE STILL, NOT TWO.** A supply-only quote strips the regions between `{{IF_INSTALL}}` / `{{END_INSTALL}}` — the field-installation inclusion bullet, Schedule Comparison, the whole Delivery Options block, and the payment-milestone table. A second template would have meant the 7 exclusions, 4 quote terms and 8 shared inclusions living in two Docs, edited separately, free to diverge. **Verified first**: a single `deleteContentRange` spanning three tables removes all of them cleanly and leaves the neighbours untouched. Markers are shaped like tokens (`{{UPPER_SNAKE}}`) deliberately, so one that survives is caught by the same guard that catches an unreplaced token instead of reaching a customer.
  - **⚠ DOCS REFUSES TO DELETE A RANGE ENDING ON THE NEWLINE OF A LIST ITEM.** Measured on the real template: the lone bullet promising field installation is rejected at `[start, end]` while all three other ranges succeed. The fix is to consume the **PRECEDING** newline (`[start-1, end-1]`); trimming the trailing one instead (`[start, end-1]`) is *worse than failing* — it leaves an empty bullet whose marker swallows the next heading, rendering "PANEL DELIVERY" as a bullet point. Deletions are applied **one request per range, back to front** (a batch is atomic, so one rejected range would discard the others).
  - **The price table KEEPS its installation row on a supply-only quote and says "Installation (by others — not included) · Not included · By others".** A customer reading that learns something; a missing row just looks like an omission. "Turnkey" becomes "Materials" throughout via `{{QUOTE_KIND}}`/`{{QUOTE_KIND_UPPER}}`, and the cover summary's installation clause is its **own token** so a supply-only quote simply does not claim it — no invented prose anywhere.
  - **Readiness no longer blocks on a missing installation line** (it did in the morning's build). Net effect on the live queue: three of the four real deals now need only a site address and a scope summary.
  - **⚠ A FAILED SEED USED TO BLOCK EVERY RETRY.** `seedQuoteTemplate` created the Doc, then verified it; a failed verification left the half-built Doc behind, which `findQuoteTemplate` then found by name so every subsequent attempt refused with "already exists". Now trashed on failure. Found because the guard correctly caught a token I had declared but never placed in the template.
  - **Verified:** 71 unit checks; both quote kinds generated end-to-end and read back **out of the PDF** (turnkey: $34.00/$15.00/$49.00 per SF, all four install sections present; supply-only: all four gone, "Not included / By others" present, 0 unreplaced tokens and 0 surviving markers in both); authenticated render check of the create form, the edit form (rate pre-filled from the backfill) and readiness on every real deal. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint at the documented 21-problem baseline. **All test data removed — including 8 Drive artifacts my own teardown had missed**, because deleting a steel deal's rows does not remove what was already published to Drive.
- **⚠ Richard's manual step: the four older steel deals still need a site address and a scope summary** (those columns did not exist before today), and Mira Vista is not in the CRM at all. The Quotes page names exactly what is missing per deal. His own "Test quote for prefab steel" deal is complete and quotes cleanly. Template to edit: `Ber Intelligence / Templates / Prefab Steel Quote Template`.
- **SAME DAY — quotes file into the team's OWN Drive folder; DEPLOYED:**
  - **Richard created `Prefab Steel Projects / Utah / Quotes folder` and asked for all quotes to sync there.**
  - **⚠ THIS OVERTURNED THE CONSTRAINT THE WHOLE FEATURE WAS DESIGNED AROUND** (see the corrected note at CLAUDE.md:389). Writing into that human-created folder works under `drive.file` because it is on a **shared drive** where moose@ has `canAddChildren`. Tested four ways before building: create-with-parent under a narrowed `drive.file` token (200), the same under the full stored token (200), create-then-move (200), and a shortcut (200). The earlier "we cannot write there" note was about a My Drive folder and does not generalise.
  - **⚠ TWO SEARCH TRAPS worth knowing:** the folder is named `"Quotes folder"`, not "Quotes", and its ancestors are `"Prefab Steel Projects "` and `"Utah "` — **with trailing spaces** — so `name = 'Prefab Steel Projects'` finds nothing. Fetching by id works; use `contains`, not `=`, when hunting for a folder by name in this drive.
  - **New `STEEL_QUOTES_FOLDER_ID`** (following the `GOOGLE_*_FOLDER_ID` convention). Unset = quotes stay in the platform's own deal folder, a configuration choice rather than a fault.
  - **One deliverable, one copy.** The generated PDF is uploaded there and its id stamped into `documents.drive_published_id`, which is precisely what stops `reconcileDrivePublishing` from ALSO copying it into `Ber Intelligence / Steel Deals / <deal>`. The working Google Doc stays in the platform's folder — it is the intermediate, not the deliverable.
  - **⚠ FIXED A PRE-EXISTING ORPHAN: replacing a draft left its published PDF in Drive.** `retireDraftArtifacts` deleted the `documents` row and the storage object but never the Drive copy, whose id lived on the row it had just deleted. Harmless while quotes landed in a folder nobody opened; in a flat Quotes folder it means two files under the same quote number. Now trashed (trash, not delete — `canDelete` is false on the shared drive).
  - **Both existing quotes moved into the folder** (Q-2026-1008 and the Mira Vista quote Richard generated mid-session), so "all quotes" is true and not just "all future quotes".
  - **Verified live against the real folder:** generate → lands there; `drive_published_id` confirmed pointing at that copy so the deal folder does not duplicate it; regenerate a draft → **exactly one file**, no stale twin; issue then regenerate → rev 2 sits **beside** rev 1, which is right because rev 1 was sent. Toast links straight to the filed PDF. **All test artifacts trashed — folder verified empty before and after.**

- **SAME DAY — rebuilt to Richard's clearer 2026-09-16 template (payment-breakdown layout); DEPLOYED:**
  - **He supplied a new reference PDF. The substance is the PAYMENT BREAKDOWN:** the document now states what is owed AT SIGNING and what follows it, instead of four undifferentiated installation milestones. **`INSTALL_MOBILIZATION_FRACTION` separates the 50% mobilization from the three progress payments**, because it is collected at signing together with 100% of the materials — `DUE_AT_SIGNING = kit + mobilization`. Two new tables back it (Amounts Due on p2, Payment Summary on p4) with tokens `DUE_AT_SIGNING` / `EXECUTION_CALC` / `INSTALL_MOBILIZATION` / `REMAINING_PAYMENTS`. Verified against Mira Vista's real figures: $1,465,200 + $333,000 = **$1,798,200** at signing, then 166,500 / 133,200 / 33,300, summing to $2,131,200 exactly.
  - **⚠ HE SENT A PDF, NOT A .docx.** Rather than convert a PDF (which loses tables, colour and the logo), `scripts/build-quote-template.mjs` was rewritten from a spacer-inserter into a **body rebuilder**: it splits the original .docx into top-level blocks, locates each by its text, and reassembles them in the new order with new cell text and the `{{TOKENS}}` written in. Every style, colour, border, the logo and the page footer carry over **untouched**. `--audit` re-checks the invariant it depends on — one text run per cell, so positional retexting is safe.
  - **The seeder's TOKENIZE map collapsed from 28 ordered literal substitutions to ONE.** The body now ships with its tokens already in place, so only the page footer still needs substituting (it lives in `footer1.xml`, which the builder does not rewrite). That removes a whole class of ordering hazard — the old map's correctness depended on, e.g., `$15.00/SF` being replaced before `$15.00 per square foot`.
  - **⚠ A SECTION CAN BE WRAPPED IN MARKERS; A TABLE ROW CANNOT** — a range covering part of a table is not deletable. New `deleteMarkedTableRows` uses `deleteTableRow` for the two Payment Summary rows that only mean something when Ber Wilson is installing.
  - **`SF_PLAIN` and `INSTALL_RATE_PLAIN` are gone.** The new layout states the installation rate **only in the price table** — the prose restatements are exactly where a figure goes stale, which was the original argument for building this at all. Added `QUOTE_KIND_LOWER`, because "the full Turnkey contract amount" reads as a mistake mid-sentence.
  - **The info table gained Quote number and Valid through rows** (the builder can add table rows; `replaceAllText` could not). The new layout dropped the old Quote Terms block, and a quote with no number cannot be referenced over the phone while one with no expiry stands forever. Flagged to Richard as a deliberate addition to his layout.
  - **⚠ FOUND WHILE WRITING OUT THE INPUT MAPPING, AND THE LEAST INTUITIVE THING THAT COULD HAVE SHIPPED: the price table never printed the line descriptions the rep typed.** `generateQuote` never passed `kitScope`/`installScope`, so both rows always rendered boilerplate. N line items collapse to ONE row, so the row now names those lines (de-duplicated, joined), with the boilerplate only as a fallback for lines left unnamed.
  - **Two copy bugs caught by READING the generated PDF rather than the code:** "Simpson connectors, builder's risk **are** included" on a supply-only quote — the conditional clause has to carry the whole tail of the sentence, conjunction included — and "$1,020,000 — includes **$1,020,000 materials**", the whole contract said twice; supply-only now reads "includes 100% of the prefab material package".
  - **Verified:** 86 unit checks; both variants generated end-to-end and read back out of the PDF (4 pages each, 0 unreplaced tokens, 0 surviving markers); every page visually compared against his reference. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint at the documented 21-problem baseline. **All test data removed — 26 Drive artifacts swept across the session**, since deleting a deal's rows does not remove what was already published to Drive.

- **Deliberately NOT built:** writing into the team's own Drive folders (needs the full `drive` scope — read/write/**delete** on everything — plus a re-consent; the zero-cost bridge is a human dragging a **shortcut** to the app-created deal folder into `Prefab Steel Projects / Utah / <Deal>`, which the app cannot create for exactly the same reason); a materials-only quote variant (a second template, not a branch — this one is the *turnkey* quote and readiness blocks without an installation line); emailing the quote to the customer; and inbound import of the team's steel folders (`drive_source_folder_id` is wired and `PUT /api/steel/source-folder` exists, but `importDriveFolder` is still project-only).

**Done 2026-09-15 (a bell that tells you what a teammate just filed; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: know when a teammate uploads a document, as a notification he can dismiss or click straight through to.** His picks (AskUserQuestion): an **in-app bell in the header** (over email), **hourly during the workday** (over nightly), and **Drive uploads plus in-platform uploads** (over Drive alone).
- **⚠ THE ATTRIBUTION WORKS, AND IT WAS THE ONE THING THAT COULD HAVE KILLED THE FEATURE.** "Notify everyone except the uploader" needs Drive to say who uploaded, and `listFolder` had never asked. Probed before building: `lastModifyingUser.emailAddress` comes back under the existing `drive.readonly` scope as a real Workspace address (`tuaone@berwilson.com`, "Ericson Tua'one"), matching `team_members.email` exactly. Requested in the **same listing** as everything else — `webViewLink` too — so attribution costs nothing; asking per file would have been one round trip per document. **10 of 62 files in a real folder carry no `lastModifyingUser`**, so the fallback is load-bearing: an actor who matches nobody notifies EVERYONE, which is right — an upload by someone outside the team is the activity most worth knowing about, not the least.
- **⚠ THE GROUPING RULE IS WHY THE BELL WILL STILL BE ON IN A MONTH.** Linking a folder imports everything in it — the Helper/Giovanni folder holds **62 files** — and 62 rows landing on someone is a bell they switch off. Above four arrivals in one run the run is announced once ("Ericson Tua'one added 8 documents and revised 1 in Myton Rail") naming three files and linking to the record. Same reasoning the project feed already follows in posting one update per run rather than one per file. Below the threshold each document gets its own row, **carrying its AI summary as the body** — which is what makes it worth reading rather than a bare filename.
- **Mixed actors mean nobody is excluded.** If Eric and Richard both filed documents in the same hour, a grouped notification cannot claim one of them did it, so `soleActor` returns null and it goes to both — each of them wants to know about the other's. In the ungrouped path exclusion is still per document, so one run can correctly notify different people about different files.
- **One row PER RECIPIENT**, not one row plus a read-receipt table: the audience is two people and per-recipient rows make "my unread count" a single indexed count instead of an anti-join. Recipients are **active members with a login** — writing rows for someone who cannot see a bell fills a table nobody will ever read.
- **Migration `20260915000001_notifications.sql` (APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded):** `notifications` with a partial index on the inbox query. `document_id`/`project_id` cascade on delete — a notification whose link 404s is worse than no notification (verified: deleting the test project took its notifications with it). `actor_name` is denormalised text with **no FK**, deliberately: the actor is frequently not a team member (info@, an outside collaborator), and a foreign key would force those events to be announced anonymously. No `log_activity()` trigger — it dereferences `new.project_id`, and an audit-logged notification would double every event in the feed.
- **⚠ A MULTI-ROW INSERT IS ONE STATEMENT WITH A UNIFORM COLUMN LIST.** A test seed mixing rows that set `created_at` with rows that omit it failed with 23502 — the omitting rows got an explicit NULL, not the default. Production is safe because `notifyTeam` builds every row from one shape, but the constraint catching it loudly is the reason `created_at` keeps its NOT NULL here rather than following the nullable-with-default convention elsewhere in the schema.
- **Hourly business-hours Drive check** (`com.berwilson.cron-drive-watch`, 8am–6pm at :20, **installed + loaded**, in the deploy service loop). Reuses `/api/cron/drive-sync`, so no middleware change. **Measured: a full pass over 132 knowledge files and 6 project folders with nothing changed takes 21.8 seconds** — an unchanged file costs one entry in a listing and nothing else, so a quiet hour is a handful of Drive calls. A document filed at 10am now reaches a colleague by 11 instead of at tomorrow's 3:15am sync.
- **The in-app door was silent while the Drive door was not**, which would have taught people that filing in Drive is "seen" and uploading here is not. Both `/api/documents` and `/api/documents/upload` now announce, after the AI pass so the notification carries the summary. Meeting attachments deliberately do not — a recording is part of the meeting flow, which produces its own record.
- **⚠ `'skipped' in knowledge` NARROWED TO `never`** in the cron route: `DriveSyncProgress` has a `skipped` COUNT of its own, so the `in` check is true for both arms of the union. Discriminated on `arrivals` instead. A union whose arms share a property name cannot be narrowed by that property.
- **Every role carries the bell** — `/api/notifications` is allowlisted for all four non-admin roles, which is safe because both routes scope every read and write to `viewer.teamMemberId`. **A cross-inbox PATCH returns 404, not a 200 that silently did nothing** (selected back to prove ownership, added after the first version was verified to no-op).
- **The count is resolved server-side and handed to the header as a prop**, so the badge is right on first paint and a page load costs one indexed count; the list is fetched only when the panel opens, and a 90s poll plus a focus listener keeps the count honest in a tab left open all day. No new `set-state-in-effect` — the poll sets state from a timer callback, never synchronously in an effect.
- **Verified live, not just typechecked:** real Drive folder created → real file uploaded → real `importDriveFolder` → document row with a complete AI pass → notification delivered **to Eric only** with the right title, summary body, Drive link and a `document_id` that resolves; second run produced **0 arrivals**; project delete cascaded the notification away; Drive folder deleted. Full API round-trip under a real minted session (unread counts, read, dismiss, read_all, dismiss_all, 400 on a bad action, 404 cross-inbox, Eric's row untouched by Richard's dismiss_all). Server-rendered badge checked at 0 / 3 / 12 unread (**9+ cap**). Eyeballed in the browser over the tailnet: panel renders, unread rows tinted with a dot, Drive icon only where there is a link, and clicking a row navigated to West Wendover's Documents tab with the badge dropping 2 → 1. `tsc` + full `next build` clean; eslint clean on every touched file. **All test data removed — 0 residue** (notifications, projects back to 14, documents, Drive).
- **⚠ FOUND WHILE TESTING, WORTH KNOWING: `generateLink` CREATES the auth user when the address has no account.** Minting a test session for `moose@berwilson.com` silently created a third auth user — **Richard signs in as `info@berwilson.com`**, while his `team_members.email` says `moose@`. The stray was deleted (auth users back to 2). The mismatch is harmless for this feature, since exclusion compares Drive's email to `team_members.email`, not to the login. But an unlinked auth account resolves as `member`, so a stray login for a real person would silently see the member view.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** email or Chat delivery of these (his pick was in-app; the `notify()` seam is there if it is ever wanted), notifications for meetings/leads/tasks (a bell that fires for everything gets ignored — add sources once these prove out), and a health card (the bell's own contents are the check).

**Done 2026-09-15 (lead queue cleaned down to the pursue set — 194 open → 66; DATA ONLY, no code, no migration):**
- **Richard's ask: clear out the open leads, keep only the most valuable-looking ones.** His picks (AskUserQuestion): clear **pass + consider + duplicate postings**, and **mark them `ignored` rather than delete**.
- **`ignored`, not DELETE, and the codebase had already made this call.** Spam leads are deliberately kept as rows "so the filter can be audited"; deleting the pass pile would have contradicted that precedent and thrown away the only evidence of whether the triage was right. It also matters because **`fit_score` carries ±20 points of measured sampling noise** (documented 2026-08-26) — an irreversible cut on a noisy signal is the wrong trade. Reversal is one UPDATE.
- **The verdict, not the score, was the cleanup axis.** The three bands came back cleanly non-overlapping on live data — pursue 62-85, consider 45-70, pass 5-42 — and the documented re-scoring experiment found the *verdict* stable across runs while the numbers swung.
