Sampling the pass pile confirmed it: door hardware, low-voltage contracts, land surveying for the **New York** Racing Association, restrooms at **Detroit Metro**. Genuinely out of scope, not mis-scored.
- **⚠ 31 ROWS WERE REPEAT POSTINGS OF 21 JOBS** — addenda, rebids and "RFI Responses" on the same solicitation ("Richfield and North Sevier High" appeared **5 times**). Deduped on a normalised title with the ranking prefix stripped, keeping the row that **carries a bid date** (a dated addendum is the actionable copy; the undated original is not).
- **Result: 128 cleared, 66 kept — every one a pursue verdict, one row per job.** Partition asserted exact (128 + 66 = 194) before writing, and **0 promoted/forwarded leads were touched** (guarded explicitly). Exact post-counts: new 66 / ignored 128 / expired 111 / spam 729 = **1034, unchanged total**. `list_leads` fell to **1,923 tokens**.
- **⚠ BACKUP DELETED BY ITS OWN TIMESTAMP, CAUGHT IMMEDIATELY.** The first backup hit **PostgREST's 1000-row cap** (the table is 1,034), so it was silently incomplete; the paginated retry used `date +%Y%m%d-%H%M`, landed in the same minute, and the `rm` of the partial file removed the good one instead. Re-taken with seconds in the name and **read back from disk** before any write. **Rule: a backup filename needs seconds, and a backup is not a backup until it has been read back.** Full 1,034-row copy at `~/berwilson-data/backups/leads-preclean-20260915-074453.json.gz`.
- **Gmail was reconciled, because the DB and the mailbox must not disagree.** Status `ignored` maps to `Ber AI/Closed`, so a direct DB write would have left 128 threads still labelled Pass/Consider for anyone reading info@ without a platform login — the whole point of the label sync. `syncLeadLabels()` relabelled **128/128, 0 failed, 43s** (under its 250/run cap). Verified against the **Gmail API directly**, not the DB: `Ber AI/Pass` **0**, `Ber AI/Consider` **0**, `Ber AI/Closed` 233.
- **⚠ A 64-vs-66 GAP THAT WAS NOT A BUG, AND FOUND SOMETHING REAL.** Gmail's label search returned 64 of the 66 pursue threads. Not index lag, not a second mailbox (all 66 are info@), not the multi-lead path (66 distinct threads, 0 doubled). Diffing the two sets: **both stragglers are in TRASH** — labelled correctly, but excluded from `label:` search, which ignores trashed mail by default. **Worth knowing: two leads still open as `pursue` have had their source email thrown away by a human** (Sky Mountain Golf Clubhouse Addendum #4, Prinova TI). The platform has no idea a person already rejected them — a trashed source thread is an unread decision signal, and nothing currently reads it.
- **Verified:** full readback of the backup; exact per-status counts summing to the unchanged 1,034; the kept set asserted identical to the intended keep-list; labels confirmed through Gmail's own API. No code changed, so no build or deploy.

**Done 2026-09-14 (agent token diet — the lead list was eating the context window; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's question: doesn't searching 3 inboxes alongside the CRM make every answer token-heavy — should they be separate?** Measured before answering, and the premise did not hold. **They already never search together:** `match_chunks` reads `chunks` (2,313 curated), `match_thread_chunks` reads `thread_chunks` (3,755 email) and is called from exactly one place. A CRM question touches no email. The vector search itself costs the model **zero tokens** — it is a pgvector query; only the returned passages enter the prompt.
- **⚠ THE REAL WEIGHT WAS SOMEWHERE ELSE ENTIRELY.** Fixed cost per turn measured at **12,615 tokens** (system prompt 3,691 + company context 1,391 + 42 tool declarations 7,533) = **31% of the context window before the user types**. `search_correspondence`'s share of that is **353 tokens, 2.8%** — smaller than `list_leads` (400) or `list_investors` (372). Separating the surfaces would have saved ~2.8% and cost the whole point of the feature, which is that you cannot know in advance whether an answer lives in the CRM or in the mail.
- **`list_leads` returned 6,456 tokens — 16% of the window in ONE tool call.** Cause: 50 rows of a **187-lead** open queue, every field emitted null or not. Measured on live data, `estimated_value` is null on **186 of 187** and `bid_due_date` on **134**, so most of the payload was keys saying nothing. Nulls now omitted; the per-lead route destination sentence (identical for every lead of a route, ~12 tokens × 50) stated **once** instead of per row; default 50 → **20**. Result **2,089 tokens, a 68% cut.**
- **The truncation is now stated, not silent.** At 20 of 187 a bare list reads as the whole queue and "you have 20 leads" would be wrong by an order of magnitude — the same no-silent-caps rule the 09-14 sweep counter fix was about. The result carries `matched`, `showing`, and how to narrow.
- **`draft_email`/`draft_agenda`/`draft_status_report` were always ONE tool** — they shared a single `case` and a single `generateDraft()` call, differing only in a string, and `recipients ?? attendees` was already merging their one distinct param. Three declarations spent **516 tokens every turn** to express what one `kind` enum expresses in 180. Now `draft`, with `attendees` still accepted as an alias so a model remembering the old shape still lands its argument.
- **⚠ CORRECTED MID-TASK: `search_email_threads` is NOT redundant beside `search_correspondence`,** which is what I first proposed cutting. It carries a **`participant` filter** (every thread touching a domain or person) and returns thread-level metadata — message/attachment counts, cluster id, date range — none of which semantic body search can answer. Structural browse and semantic recall are different questions. Left in place. **Likewise `list_investors` is near-irreducible:** its 372 tokens are mostly enum *values*, which are the real DB strings and load-bearing.
- **Honest result: declarations 7,547 → 7,273 (−3.6%), tool results where the win actually was (−4,367 on one call).** Converting `list_leads`' prose params to enums saved almost nothing in tokens but does constrain the model to real values, so a filter can no longer silently match nothing.
- **⚠ LM STUDIO HAD DRIFTED AGAIN — 4th occurrence, and this time it was actively dangerous.** `lms ps` showed **context 65536, parallel 4** against the documented 40960/parallel 1, with the box at **0.12GB free and 16% memory pressure** — the exact state that OOM-killed the app (exit −9) on 09-14. **The finding that matters: `parallel` is the costly setting, not context.** Parallel N allocates N independent KV caches, and the platform is strictly sequential (`for (const row of rows)`, `summarize-phase.ts`), so parallel 4 bought nothing and cost 4×. Reloaded at **65536 / parallel 1** — a roomier window AND less memory than the drifted config. Single copy verified (no `:2`), embeddings and `search_correspondence` re-smoked clean afterwards. **Prefer fixing parallel over shrinking the window.**
- **Verified:** `tsc` + full `next build` clean; eslint clean on both touched files; the consolidated `draft` tool exercised live (rejects an invalid `kind`, produced a real 3,118-char agenda in 40s); `list_leads` and `search_correspondence` re-measured against the live DB after deploy. Deployed (app 200).

**Done 2026-09-14 (ask the inbox — semantic search over correspondence; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: stop hunting through email.** He wants to ask *"What are the details about the Hill AFB project with the onsite visit on September 15th? Do we need to register or fill out an application?"* — while keeping Ber AI's CRM answers focused on curated platform data. His picks (AskUserQuestion): **one Ber AI with a separate correspondence tool** (not a second page), **index everything except marketing**, **index attachments too**, and **name unreadable documents explicitly**.
- **⚠ MEASURED FIRST, AND THE NUMBERS MADE THE CASE:** of the threads mentioning Hill AFB, **15 match in the message BODY, 5 in the subject, 2 in the AI summary**. `search_email_threads` keyword-matches subject/deal_name/counterparty/summary/key_facts and **never the body**, so two thirds was unreachable — and a real question's phrasing ("do we need to register?") matches none of those fields at all. The answer was sitting in plain text the whole time: *"The sit visit is September 15 @ 9:00 am"* (sic).
- **⚠ THE LOAD-BEARING DECISION: a SEPARATE index (`thread_chunks`), not `chunks`.** `match_chunks` with an empty project filter returns **every row in its table** — email added there would leak into `search_knowledge_base` on the next query, the exact pollution the ask was guarding against. Two measurements settled it: email would be **~79% of a combined index** (~8,500 email chunks against 2,313 curated), so every CRM query working well today would compete against a corpus 3.7× its size; and a separate table means **zero changes to the four existing `match_chunks` call sites**. **Verified after the build: Stockton 0.693 and West Wendover 0.543 — identical to the 2026-09-09 baselines — and `chunks` still exactly 2,313.** Separation is a property of the schema, not a discipline.
- **⚠ THE FILTER BUG THAT WOULD HAVE SILENTLY DROPPED THE MAIL HE ASKED ABOUT.** The first version excluded marketing with `.neq('summary->>relevance','noise')`. Deal threads carry that label — **lead threads have no summary at all** (the lead pipeline triages into `leads` instead), so `relevance` is null for every one of them and a `.neq()` **drops nulls**. That silently excluded **all 992 lead-pipeline threads, including the Hill AFB bid invitations this was built for**; the backfill reported "288 threads to index" instead of 1,241, which is the only reason it was caught. Now: deal-side noise excluded by relevance, lead-side by `leads.status='spam'`, and a thread carrying *any* non-spam lead is kept (one email can describe several deals). A lead thread with no lead row yet is **kept**, not dropped — untriaged is not the same as marketing.
- **`extractPortalLinks` is what makes the gap honest.** Bid invitations keep the real terms in files behind a portal login: the Hill AFB threads have an attachment count of **zero** while naming five documents. The first version returned 13 references — 8 of them the per-trade "Reply to" buttons (Metal Framing, Ceramic Tile…), which would have buried the one file that matters. Links with no filename nearby are now discarded, leaving **exactly the 5 real documents** incl. `Attachment_12_Section L Instruction to Offerors`, the one holding the registration answer.
- **⚠ TOOL PAYLOADS ARE A REAL CONSTRAINT ON A LOCAL MODEL.** The first cut returned 12 passages × ~1,640 chars ≈ **5,000 tokens in one tool response**, and the agent calls several tools per turn. Default cut to 6 (hard cap 10) and each passage trimmed to 1,200 chars — evidence, not the whole thread, with `get_email_thread` as the escape hatch. Measured for comparison: `list_leads` returns **5,810 tokens**, which is worth knowing next time a turn stalls.
- **Verified end-to-end on Richard's exact question, and it did better than asked.** The agent autonomously called `search_correspondence`, returned the Sept 15 9:00am site visit attributed to Travis Boren / Guardian Construction, named the five BuildingConnected documents as not held — **and caught that Guardian sent a follow-up on 2026-09-04 saying they had "decided not to pursue this opportunity"**, so attending the site visit would have been wasted effort. That withdrawal sentence was confirmed verbatim in the source text, not hallucinated. Four threads across two weeks, synthesized into a decision.
- **Migration `20260914000001_thread_chunks.sql` (APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded):** `thread_chunks` (thread_id cascade, source body|attachment, attachment_name for citation, vector(768), HNSW cosine mirroring `idx_chunks_embedding` exactly), `email_threads.embedded_at` + partial index, and `match_thread_chunks` shaped like `match_chunks` but joining `email_threads` so a hit carries subject/mailbox/date/gmail_thread_id without a second round trip. `filter_after` is on the MAIL's date, not the chunk's — the reader means "what was said recently".
- **Attachments are indexed WITHOUT `runDocumentAiPass`** — that runs a ~48s summarization per file and search needs verbatim text, not a summary. That single omission is what makes attachment indexing affordable.
- **Invalidation reuses the 2026-09-09 machinery:** `embedded_at` is cleared wherever `routed_at` is, so a conversation that gains a reply is re-embedded rather than answering from a stale copy. Plus an `embed` phase in the sweep orchestrator (batch 50, 10-min ceiling) and a **Correspondence Index** card on `/settings/health` — this failure class is silent by construction: an unindexed thread never errors, it just never appears in an answer.
- **Backfill complete: 591 threads indexed — 1,827 body + 1,923 attachment chunks, 0 failed.** Attachment chunks OUTNUMBER body chunks, which is the point: asked what bonding is required, **4 of 4 hits came from inside attachments** (AIA A310 bid bonds, performance/payment bond terms, the Ber Wilson Master Subcontract insurance schedule) — none of it reachable before. Recall on the Hill AFB ground truth: **9 of 12 body-matching threads in the top 10, against keyword search's 5**, with the Site Visit thread the top hit at 0.698. 693 threads correctly excluded as spam leads; 611 as deal-side noise.
- **⚠ A COUNT THAT DISAGREED WITH REALITY.** The run reported `0 skipped` while silently passing over **693** spam-filtered threads — `withoutSpamLeads` stamped them and returned the rest without incrementing the counter, and the total only failed to add up because the announced figure (1,241) was checked against what landed (548). Fixed to count what it drops. **A progress number that under-reports is worse than none: it is the shape of a silent gap.**
- **⚠ THE BACKFILL KILLED THE APP, AND THE DEPLOY LOOKED BROKEN FOR A MINUTE.** Running the backfill, the resident model and a `next build` at once took the 36GB box to **0.1GB free**; launchd's exit code for the platform service was **-9** (OOM-killed) and the logs showed `TypeError: fetch failed` reaching Supabase — which reads exactly like a database outage but was not: all 8 containers were healthy throughout. **`kill -STOP <pid>` pauses a backfill without losing work** (it is resumable by design), the app restarts clean, then `kill -CONT` resumes it. **Rule: do not run a backfill and a deploy at the same time on this box; if the app 000s after a kickstart, check free memory and the service's exit code before suspecting Supabase.**
- **Cost, measured not estimated: 220ms per chunk warm** (the first call reads 2.2s because the model JIT-loads — never benchmark cold). 12 Hill AFB threads embedded in 14s.

**⚠ LM STUDIO DRIFTED AGAIN — 2026-09-14 (config only, no code):** `lms ps` showed **TWO resident copies** of `qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b` — the documented one (40960 ctx, parallel 1) plus a second `:2` instance at **65536 ctx, parallel 4**. That is 44GB of weights plus KV caches on a 36GB box, and it surfaced as `Local AI stream stalled — no data for 180s` on agent turns that called several tools. Diagnosed while building the correspondence index: the tool payloads were measured first (search_correspondence 2,574 tokens, `list_leads` **5,810**) and were not the cause. **This is the third occurrence; the tell is a model id ending in `:2` in the `/models` listing.** Fix: `lms unload qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b:2` (note `lms unload` takes no `-y` — passing it loads ANOTHER copy). **Check `lms ps` before concluding that slow or stalled agent turns are a code problem.**

**Done 2026-09-09 (audit pass — the platform was OFF THE TAILNET, and 16 company documents were permanently unindexed; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's ask: make sure the recent work is free of bugs and built to standard.** Build gauntlet was already clean (tsc, `next build`, eslint at its documented 21-problem baseline, no Finder duplicates), so everything below was found by exercising the platform against live data rather than by reading it.
- **⚠ FOUND FIRST, AND WORST: THE PLATFORM WAS DOWN FOR EVERY USER.** `tailscale serve status` returned **"No serve config"** — both listeners gone, while all 8 Supabase containers were healthy, the launchd service was running, and the app answered HTTP 200 on `localhost:3000`. Nothing was broken in a way any check could see. Because `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` points at the `:8443` listener, the app also could not reach its own database — so it served `/login` and failed at **every data path**, which reads as "the platform is broken" rather than "a proxy is missing". Restored both listeners; app and Supabase verified 200 over the tailnet.
  - **The deploy script only ever restored ONE of the two.** `deploy-to-studio.sh` ran `tailscale serve --bg 3000`; the `:8443` → kong mapping was set up **by hand at the July cutover and had never been in the script**, so a redeploy would have restored half the deployment and left the same symptom. It now (re)establishes both every time and **prints `serve status`**, so a half-configured proxy shows up in the deploy output instead of being discovered later as a mystery outage. **Rule: the serve config is Tailscale's state, not ours — assert it on every deploy, never assume it survived.**
  - Deliberately NOT wired into `/settings/health`: when `:8443` is down the health page cannot load at all (it reads the DB through that listener), so a check there could never fire for this failure. The deploy script is the only place the guarantee can live.
- **⚠ 16 COMPANY DOCUMENTS HELD A FILE, NO TEXT AND NO CHUNKS — invisible to Ber AI while every surface a human looks at showed them as present.** Among them the **Tooele Army Depot LOI**, a **Phase 1 environmental report**, a **DoD award letter (W9126G26D0003)**, the **SNA teaming agreement**, the **2026 title commitment** and the **GridEdge investor deck** — precisely the material the Drive sync exists to surface. **Two independent causes:**
  1. **A SUMMARY FAILURE DISCARDED THE EXTRACTED TEXT.** `runDocumentAiPass` stored text only AFTER summarizing, so a summary that threw jumped to the catch and settled the document on `'error'` — even though extraction had already succeeded. The summary is a convenience; the **verbatim text is what gets embedded and answered from**. Summarizing is now allowed to fail on its own, with the text still stored and indexed. (`UnreadableDocumentError` stays fatal — there is no text behind it.)
  2. **⚠ `syncDriveKnowledge` HAD NO STRANDED-DOCUMENT RETRY.** The 09-09 build added one to `importDriveFolder` (projects) and **not** to the company sync — and every single failure was a company document. Change detection correctly reported the bytes as unchanged, so nothing ever came back for them: **permanently, not until the next run.** `needsAnotherPass` moved into `document-pipeline.ts` beside the statuses it interprets, so there is ONE answer to "did this pass finish" instead of one per importer. **Rule: every importer needs the same answer; the one that lacks it fails silently and indefinitely.**
- **A document with neither text nor summary now settles as `'skipped'`, not `'error'`.** Both leave it unsearchable, but only one invites a nightly sync to fetch and re-read it forever — the trap the scanned survey plats fell into last week, which the new retry would otherwise have re-opened. **A+C had to ship together:** adding the retry without this would have re-created that loop.
- **Repaired on real data: 13 of 15 recovered, 2 correctly settled.** Phase 1 report **134 chunks**, the award letter **79**, the title commitment **23**, the SNA teaming agreement **9**. The two WELSKO patents are scanned image-only PDFs and now settle as `skipped` rather than being chased nightly. **Retrieval verified live** — Tooele LOI **0.672**, SNA teaming agreement **0.668**, Phase 1 report **0.594**, each the top hit for a question only it can answer. A full knowledge sync afterwards: `failed:0, unchanged:102, outOfTime:false`, and it **recovered a document stranded mid-pass** (`updated:1`) — the fix firing on a real stranded document found organically. **Documents went from 16 unindexed to 0** (200 complete / 25 correctly skipped).
- **New "Document Indexing" card on `/settings/health`** — this failure class is silent by construction, so it is now counted out loud (the same argument as the Drive publishing card). `skipped` is deliberately NOT a fault, or the check would be permanently red. **The `processing` case needed a rule:** a pass sets it for its own duration, so the check would cry wolf through every nightly sync — the syncs work **one document at a time**, so exactly one is in flight and more than one means passes are dying. `documents` has no `updated_at`, so that sequencing is the only thing available to tell working from stuck; **noted in the code, because a parallel importer would invalidate it.** Verified both ways against live data.
- **Fixed: a folder holding ONE document could never retire it.** The mass-vanish guard read `vanished > live/2`, and 1 is always more than half of 1 — so the Archive gesture silently did nothing and the refusal was reported again every night with no way to clear it. A single missing file is a filing decision; the guards above it have already ruled out the ways a folder breaks (a partial pass proves nothing, an empty listing is never evidence). **13-case unit check** over the guard and the status contract.
- **Fixed: restoring a document claimed to queue a re-index that does not exist.** Nothing sweeps for `embedding_status='pending'` — a Drive-sourced document is caught by its nightly sync, but one that never came from Drive has **nothing coming for it at all**. The response now says the re-index is required and where to get it. Only latent today because the single caller (`DocumentsTab`) already fires `/reindex` itself.
- **⚠ FINDING FOR RICHARD, NOT FIXED — 10 documents are indexed TWICE, and the Archive convention structurally cannot fix it.** The duplicates are **cross-source**, not Drive-internal: the *Ber Wilson Proper Business Plan 2026* exists as a hand-uploaded `reference` doc AND a Drive-imported `capability` doc (**132 chunks for one business plan**); the *Stockton Power Nexus proposal* arrived once as an email attachment and once from Drive (**100 chunks**); the *Myton-Rail companion memo* has **three** copies. Cause: hand upload, email-attachment import and Drive sync are independent doors, and each dedupes only within its own key (`drive_file_id`, or file name within one record) — so a file that came in by email and later appears in Drive is imported twice. This matters because a near-duplicate **doubles a document's chunks and biases every retrieval toward whatever was duplicated** — `supersede.ts` names this exact failure. **Deliberately not fixed:** CLAUDE.md records that automatic near-duplicate supersede detection was considered and rejected in favour of the Archive convention, and reversing that is Richard's call — but worth knowing that dragging the Drive copy to Archive retires the wrong one here, since the other copy is not in Drive at all.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + full `next build` clean; eslint **at its documented 21-problem baseline, unchanged** (12 `set-state-in-effect`, 6 `no-img-element`, 2 `purity`, 1 `prefer-const` — all pre-existing); 13-case unit check; deployed and smoke-tested over the tailnet (app 200, `/dashboard` 307, Supabase `:8443` 200, both serve listeners present).
- **Reviewed clean, no changes needed:** the route/apply/cluster-link correspondence chain (delta slicing, certainty→review posture, `applied_message_count` seeding, ambiguity-means-no-match, the overlap-vs-Jaccard split), the Drive vanish guards, `listFolder`'s archive skipping, the new `/api/drive/browse` and `/api/drive/source-folder` routes (both admin-only by default-deny), and the deal-intake parser.

**Done 2026-09-09 (Drive becomes a two-way document surface — link a folder, it imports nightly and posts what arrived; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: Eric files documents in Drive and none of them reach the platform. Scan daily, put them on the project, log a short update saying what each one is about — and what should happen to outdated documents?** His picks (AskUserQuestion): **link folders by hand**, **read the team's shared drive but keep publishing separate**, **Archive-subfolder convention plus a supersede flag**, and **yes, index the corporate tree** into the company KB.
- **⚠ THE TEAM'S REAL DOCUMENT HOME WAS A SHARED DRIVE THE PLATFORM HAD NEVER READ.** `Ber Wilson Proper` (~250 files) is organised by business line → project (`Housing Development / Mira Vista`, `Military Developments / Stockton, Utah`, `Manufacturing / Ber Wilson - Prefab Steel`). The platform read exactly ONE folder out of it — `Knowledge Library for Ber Intelligence`, holding **2 files** — while publishing into `Ber Intelligence` inside **moose@'s My Drive**, a folder the team does not use. Two document homes, and the team was only ever in one.
- **⚠ THE INBOUND PATH HAD EXISTED SINCE SEPTEMBER AND HAD NEVER ONCE HAD ANYTHING TO DO.** `syncDealFolders` read only `projects.deal_folder_id`, set solely by the website deal form, and **0 of 15 projects had one**. It ran nightly, imported nothing, and looked perfectly healthy. Seven projects had a *published* folder, which was never read back. **Rule: a coverage count is the only honest signal for a feature whose failure mode is doing nothing.** Hence the new `Drive → Projects` health card.
- **`projects.drive_source_folder_id` is deliberately a THIRD column, not a reuse of `drive_folder_id`.** One is where the platform publishes TO, the other is where the team files and we read FROM; conflating them would have publishing write into the team's own folders — which it cannot even do (see scopes below).
- **Folders are linked by hand because the names are provably ambiguous.** "Myton - Utah" has two candidate projects (Myton Development, Myton Rail), so does "Stockton, Utah", and West Wendover appears in **two separate trees**. The same lesson as the correspondence build: ambiguity must mean no match. A `DriveFolderLink` picker drills the shared drive one level at a time, showing file counts and newest dates (an empty shelf usually means go deeper), with a paste-a-URL escape hatch; linking imports immediately rather than waiting for the cron.
- **Retiring a document is a Drive gesture, because the people who know it is stale have no platform login.** `listFolder` skips any subtree named Archive/Archived/Old/Superseded/Obsolete/Deprecated/"Do not use" (whole-name match, case-insensitive — a project folder called "Old Mill Road" must not vanish), so dragging a file in makes it disappear from the listing, and `reconcileVanished` turns that into a supersession: chunks dropped so Ber AI stops citing it, row and file kept. Dragging it back out restores and re-indexes it. `PATCH /api/documents/[id] {superseded}` is the same action from inside the platform.
- **⚠ THE GUARDS AROUND VANISH-DETECTION ARE THE IMPORTANT PART, and one fired on its first real run.** A renamed, moved, re-permissioned or hiccuping folder returns fewer files than it holds, and obeying that would retire a project's entire document set silently. So: a partial pass proves nothing, an empty listing is never evidence, and losing **more than half at once is reported, not obeyed**. All three verified live.
- **⚠ BUG FOUND BY RUNNING IT, NOT BY COMPILING IT: the publish-loop guard skipped every file it had already imported.** An imported document is deliberately stamped `drive_published_id = its own Drive id` (so the publish reconcile never pushes it back), so a guard of "skip anything we published" matched every previously-imported file — defeating change detection and then making the whole folder look vanished. Fixed by building that set from `drive_file_id is null` rows only.
- **Also fixed while there: a document stranded mid-AI-pass could never recover.** A row with a file, no text and no chunks is invisible to the search it was imported for, and change detection correctly says "unchanged" forever — the same stranding this repo has now hit three times. Any prior whose `embedding_status` is not complete/skipped is retried; a retry is deliberately NOT announced in the feed.
- **⚠ `syncDriveKnowledge` claimed rows it did not own.** Its known-document map was built from EVERY document with a `drive_file_id`, so a file imported onto a project would be found as `prior` and have its storage path rewritten to `company/drive/…`. Harmless while the two syncs read different folders; a live corruption the moment they read the same shared drive, which is exactly what this build does. Now scoped to `is_company`, with project-owned files skipped rather than stolen (`documents.drive_file_id` is UNIQUELY indexed, so claiming one would fail the insert every night forever).
- **One update per project per run, never one per file** — linking a folder with 30 documents in it should read as a line in the feed, not a wall. It names what was added, what was revised, what was retired, and carries each document's own AI summary, which is the "what is it about" the ask was for. Written `embedding_status: 'skipped'`: every document named is indexed in its own right, and indexing the notice too would put a second thinner copy of each summary in front of the retriever.
- **Knowledge folders are now a comma-separated LIST**, so the corporate tree is indexed a shelf at a time rather than by pointing at a drive root. Nominated: Knowledge Library, Corporate, Estimation & Quote Templates, Prefab Steel. One unreadable folder no longer costs the others.
- **⚠ SCOPE CONSTRAINT worth knowing before anyone proposes "one folder per project":** moose@ holds `drive.readonly` (everything) + `drive.file` (app-created files only). **Reading the team's shared drive works today; WRITING into a folder a human created does not** and would need full `drive` — the broadest grant in the file — plus a re-consent. That is why publishing still targets `Ber Intelligence` and this build is read-only in that direction. **⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-16 — this is TRUE OF A MY DRIVE FOLDER AND FALSE OF A SHARED DRIVE ONE.** Measured with a token narrowed to `drive.file` alone against `Prefab Steel Projects / Utah / Quotes folder` on the `Ber Wilson Proper` shared drive: creating a file with `parents: [thatFolder]` returns **200**, as does moving an app-created file into it. On a shared drive the two permissions are separate questions — `drive.file` governs which files the app may touch *afterwards* (its own), while whether it may add a child is the impersonated user's own right, and moose@ reports `canAddChildren: true` there. No broader scope, no re-consent. The asymmetry that remains: `canDelete` is **false**, so cleanup has to trash rather than hard-delete.
- **Verified against the real Drive and the live DB, not fixtures:** 11-case archive-name check plus a real scratch tree proving archive subtrees are skipped, folder paths carried, and `includeArchived` still returns all 3; a real import of the **Hafoka permit drawing set** producing a correct AI summary, 1 chunk, and a feed update, with a second run a clean no-op and no second update; and a 15-check lifecycle on a throwaway project — 4 imported, one moved to Archive → **exactly 1 superseded with its chunks gone while its neighbour stayed indexed**, moved back → restored and re-indexed, then both mass-vanish and empty-listing guards refusing to act and saying why. **All test data removed — 0 residue** (projects back to 15, scratch Drive folders deleted). `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint clean on every touched file.
- **Linked live (4 of 15, the unambiguous ones):** Hafoka Residence, Helper/Giovanni (62 files — expect a couple of nights to drain at ~60s per document), Trump City (WW NV) → West Wendover, TEAD Forge 42 → Tooele. **Left for Richard, deliberately:** `Myton - Utah` and `Stockton, Utah` each match two projects; `Mira Vista`, `Camilla - Los Angeles` and `HAFB - Falcon Hill` have no project at all.
- **⚠ FOUR MORE FAULTS, ALL FOUND BY POINTING THE PLATFORM AT REAL DOCUMENTS FOR THE FIRST TIME — none by typechecking.** 132 corporate files and a 62-file project folder exposed what fixtures never would:
  1. **A SCANNED PDF WAS RECORDED AS A FAILURE, AND THE NEW RETRY THEN CHASED IT FOREVER.** The cadastral survey plats under Helper are images; `unpdf` extracts nothing and threw, landing them on `'error'` — which the stranded-document retry re-attempted every night, spending the whole budget on files that can never succeed and never reaching the documents behind them. New `UnreadableDocumentError` settles them to **`'skipped'`**, which is what that state already meant for a file type we cannot read. **The distinction that matters is not error-vs-success but retryable-vs-not.**
  2. **⚠ A LONG PDF BLEW THE MODEL'S CONTEXT AND FAILED OUTRIGHT — PRE-EXISTING, AND IT WOULD HAVE BITTEN ANY LARGE UPLOAD.** The local PDF branch of `callGeminiWithFile` concatenated a document's ENTIRE extracted text into the summary prompt with no cap, while the plain-text path beside it had capped at 30,000 chars all along. A real Phase 1 environmental report arrived at **84,135 tokens against a 65,536 window**; award letters and teaming agreements went the same way. Capped to match. Nothing is lost — that call produces a summary, while the verbatim text is extracted separately, stored whole, and embedded in chunks.
  3. **A file in two nominated folders, and separately a lost insert race, both violated the platform-wide unique index on `drive_file_id`.** Two distinct causes with one symptom: a dedupe guard inside the pass handles a multi-parented file, and a **23505 is now counted as skipped rather than failed** — two syncs genuinely overlap (a hand-triggered run beside the nightly cron, or a client that disconnected without stopping the server handler, which Next does not abort), and losing that race means the document IS imported.
  4. **⚠ `syncDriveKnowledge` COULD HAVE STOLEN A PROJECT'S DOCUMENT ROW.** Its known-document map was built from EVERY row with a `drive_file_id`, so a file imported onto a project would be found as `prior` and have its storage path rewritten to `company/drive/…`. Harmless while the two syncs read different folders; a live corruption the moment they read the same shared drive, which is exactly what this build does.
- **⚠ LM STUDIO HAD DRIFTED OFF ITS DOCUMENTED CONFIGURATION, and that was the single biggest cause of failures.** `lms ps` showed the chat model at **context 65536, parallel 4, TTL 1h** and the **embedding model not loaded at all** — against the 2026-08-25 intent of context 40960, parallel 1, no TTL, both resident. Four KV caches at 64k plus a JIT load of the embedding model on every document forced eviction, surfacing as `{"error":"terminated"}` and `Model is unloaded` on **16 documents in one pass**. Restored with `lms load qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b -c 40960 --parallel 1` + `lms load text-embedding-qwen3-embedding-0.6b`; the next pass had **zero model failures** and completions climbed 67 → 84. **`lms unload` takes no `-y`** — passing it silently loads a SECOND 22GB copy of the model (44GB on a 36GB box); check `lms ps` after any load. **Check `lms ps` against the documented config whenever local AI starts failing oddly — an LM Studio update resets it.**
- **Final state of the first real sync: 84 documents indexed, 17 correctly skipped as unreadable, 19 errors ~~still draining on retry~~ — ⚠ CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (see the entry above): those errors were NOT draining and never would have. The stranded-document retry was added to the PROJECT importer; every one of the failures was in the COMPANY sync, which had no retry at all — 0 new failures of any kind in the last clean pass.** The corporate knowledge base went from **1 document to 72** — the GridEdge and Instant Access Networks M&A material and their patents are now retrievable, and none of it was visible to Ber AI before.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** two-way sync (Drive must never become a second source of truth); deleting anything in Drive (it is the team's filing, not ours); automatic near-duplicate supersede detection (his pick was the Archive convention); source folders for opportunities and steel deals (the same `importDriveFolder` covers them when wanted).

**Done 2026-09-09 (correspondence keeps records current — threads unfreeze, mail reaches its record, one email can be many leads; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: the platform reads every email and creates leads, but nothing updates a PROJECT from recent mail** — and separately, referrers like Chuck (chucklmanto@gmail.com) describe several deals in one loosely-written email, all of which should become their own leads. Goal: every opportunity organized into its proper space, so creating a project pulls in the whole conversation and its attachments. His picks (AskUserQuestion): updates **split by certainty**, inference **only above a confidence bar**, **full historical catch-up**, and **one email describing five projects becomes five leads**.
- **⚠ THE ASK UNDERSTATED THE PROBLEM: stored correspondence was FROZEN AT FIRST CAPTURE.** `fetch-phase` upserted with `ignoreDuplicates: true` keyed on a fingerprint that is *the earliest Message-ID in the thread* (`gmail-search.ts`), so **a reply to a thread already stored was silently discarded** — `raw_markdown`, `message_count`, `last_at` and attachments stayed at whatever they were when first read. Measured against live Gmail before the fix: **3 of 40 sampled threads had grown unnoticed, one from 2 stored messages to 6**; 1,432 of 1,674 stored threads were single-message. Nothing downstream could be fresher than its thread, so no amount of routing would have helped. **Rule: a dedupe key derived from a conversation's FIRST message identifies the conversation, not its contents — never use one to decide "already seen".**
- **Fix:** `sweepPage` already fully fetches each thread *before* the dedupe check, so growth detection was free. `knownFingerprints: Set` → `knownThreads: Map<fingerprint,{messageCount}>`, and a known thread is skipped only when the incoming view is **not strictly larger**. Adopting a strictly larger view only makes the refresh **monotonic** — two mailboxes holding different slices of one conversation converge on the fuller one instead of overwriting each other on alternate runs. A grown thread goes back to `summary_state='pending'` (and `routed_at=null`); an unchanged one still does not, which was the original guard's whole point.
- **THE MISSING PRIMITIVE — `thread_links`.** Linkage existed only as three partial answers (`leads.promoted_*_id`, `thread_clusters.session_id`, `email_intake_sessions.created_record_ids`), none of which answers *"what record does this reply belong to?"*. A table, not columns, because one email legitimately belongs to several records. Carries `certainty` ('linked' = the thread IS that record, no ambiguity | 'inferred' = matched) which decides review posture **in one place** rather than being re-derived at each write site, and `applied_message_count` — **without which every refresh re-posts the whole conversation and a project's feed fills with the same mail night after night**. `record_id` is deliberately NOT a foreign key (it points at four tables); the apply phase drops a link whose record has gone instead of failing on it.
- **Three new phases, all deterministic and IO-bound** (1,617 threads route in ~12s, so they never compete with summarize for the local model): **route** (`route-phase.ts` — derived links reconciled every run, so a link lost to a failed write heals itself; inference only for threads with no `linked` link), **apply** (`apply-phase.ts` — delta only, `linked`→`review_state='approved'` + embedded, `inferred`→`'pending'` + a **`review_queue` row**), and a `routed_at` marker (without it the phase re-read the newest page forever and never reached older threads).
- **MATCHING WAS TUNED AGAINST REAL DATA, NOT INTUITION — the first version misfiled confidently.** Folding the thread's counterparty into the name tokens cross-matched one side's counterparty against the other's NAME, sending mail about the Hafoka residence, an LA commercial building and a Myton campus all onto the **"Dino Plumbing" opportunity** purely because Dino was the subcontractor. Removed. Also: **Jaccard is wrong for this direction** — it penalises a record for having a long formal name ("Giovanni campus schedule" vs "DUBHES Helper / Giovanni Resilience Campus" scored 0.33), so record matching uses the **overlap coefficient** while clustering keeps Jaccard (thread-vs-thread is symmetric). Two further guards, both from real names: **ambiguity means no match** ("Myton Rail" and "Myton Development" score identically against a thread saying only "Myton" — picking one would misfile half the time), and a **single shared token needs corroboration** (a one-word name is either a distinctive brand like "GridEdge" or a common place like "Tooele", and nothing in the name says which). Result on the real corpus: **26 of 1,617 threads matched, all defensible** (10 West Wendover, 4 Myton Rail, 3 Pre-Fab Steel, 3 DUBHES…). 15-case unit check over the actual portfolio names.
- **Solicitation numbers outrank every similarity score**, with two fixes found by testing: matching is by **containment** (the same number really is stored as "(2) 4-6-034: 019" against a bid citing "Solicitation No. 4-6-034-019"), and the extractor scans **all** regex matches taking the first containing a digit — "Bid Invite: Solicitation No. …" matched on "Bid" first and captured the word "Invite".
- **Multi-lead triage (the Chuck case).** Prompt `lead-triage-2.0` returns `{leads:[…]}` with an explicit rule that separate sites/scopes/owners are separate opportunities while phases of one job are not; `normalizeBatch` accepts the 1.0 single-object shape too (losing a bid invitation to a shape mismatch is worse than accepting either). Migration swaps the unique index to `(thread_id, thread_item)`. **Verified on a realistic Chuck email: 4 opportunities, correctly routed across three business lines** — multifamily→construction, PEMB warehouse→**steel**, parish hall→construction (with DPR-2026-114, prevailing wage and the 100% P&P bond attached to the right one), rooftop HVAC→**dino**. Re-triage corrects in place; `pruneSurplusLeads` removes surplus ordinals **only when untouched** (verified: a promoted lead survived a re-triage that found fewer).
- **⚠ ON CONFLICT CANNOT USE A PARTIAL UNIQUE INDEX.** The first `(thread_id, thread_item)` index carried `where thread_id is not null` and every upsert failed with *"no unique or exclusion constraint matching the ON CONFLICT specification"* — PostgREST has no way to repeat the predicate. The predicate was unnecessary anyway: **Postgres already treats NULLs as distinct**, so web-form leads are unaffected by a plain unique index.
- **⚠ TWO BUGS THE `as never` CASTS HID FROM `tsc`:** the documents insert used `file_path`/`file_size`; the real columns are **`storage_path`/`file_size_bytes`**. Caught by running it, not by compiling it.
- **⚠ `/review` READS `review_queue`, NOT `updates.review_state`** — staged updates would have sat pending forever with nothing surfacing them (the "wired a feature that renders nowhere" failure this repo has hit before). Apply now inserts a `review_queue` row with reason `inferred_email_match`. **And the review route PURGED `raw_content` on approving any `source='email'` update** — a leftover from the Outlook-scraper era where a web link led back to the original. Apply-phase is now the *only* producer of those, and for them the body IS the record, so approval would have deleted exactly what the feature preserves. Purge removed.
- **Undoing a misfiling needs no new UI: rejecting an inferred email update in `/review` DROPS THE LINK.** Without that the thread stays attached and posts again on the next growth, and the reviewer would reject the same misfiling forever. Only ever removes an `inferred` link — a `linked` one is a fact, and one stray message must not sever a project from its own origin.
- **Seeding is what stops the backfill re-posting history.** A derived link is seeded at the thread's **current** message count (the lead was triaged from that whole thread; promotion already carried the conversation across), so only genuinely NEW mail produces an update. Seeding at zero would have re-posted every thread ever read and requeued all 236 leads for scoring — some two hours of model time to tell records what they already knew. An `inferred` link starts at zero on purpose: that record has never seen the conversation.
- **⚠ THE RETRY FIX RESOLVED A LIVE SIX-DAY OUTAGE, found while backfilling.** `mailbox_sync` showed **info@ in `state='failed'` since 2026-09-03** with exactly this error: *"Quota exceeded for quota metric 'Total Query Cost' … units per minute per user"*. The lead mailbox had not been read for six days, because one throttled `listThreads` aborted the run and nothing retried. The dashboard's mailbox alert keys on that state, so it was visible — but the cause was a missing retry, not a missing credential.
- **Also fixed: `googleFetch` had NO RETRY AT ALL.** Gmail meters by query-cost-units-per-minute and a sweep reading whole threads back to back hits it routinely (observed live at 42 threads). `sweepPage` guards `fetchThread` per thread but **not** the `listThreads` call above it, so one throttled listing aborted a whole mailbox's run. Now 5 attempts with `Retry-After` honoured and exponential backoff, treating 429/5xx and **403-with-a-quota-body** as transient (a 403 meaning "no permission" and one meaning "slow down" differ only in the message).
- **Migration `20260909000001_thread_links.sql` (APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded):** `thread_links`, `lead_notes` (following the `*_notes` convention exactly — `update_updated_at` only, **not** `log_activity()`, which dereferences `new.project_id`), `leads.thread_item` + index swap, `thread_clusters.project_id/opportunity_id/confirmed_at` + an app-level `confirmed` state (the column has no check constraint, same as steel's `invoiced`), and `email_threads.routed_at`. Types hand-maintained in `email-sweep/db.ts` and `leads/db.ts` as those tables are deliberately outside `database.ts` (`gen-types` is blocked on this stack).
- **⚠ REJECTING A MISFILING HAD TO REMOVE THE UPDATE, NOT JUST THE LINK.** The project Updates tab renders every row **regardless of `review_state`**, so dropping only the link left the correspondence sitting on the wrong project after a reviewer had explicitly rejected it. Rejection now deletes the update too — scoped to `reason='inferred_email_match'` so the 33 pending updates from the retired Outlook scraper keep their existing behavior. Nothing is lost: the conversation lives in `email_threads` and will be posted to the right record if it is ever matched correctly.
- **⚠ ATTACHMENT DEDUPE HAD TO BE BY NAME, NOT NAME+SIZE.** The first version reused `stageAttachments`' name+size key and still imported duplicates, because **the same document sent from two different threads arrives at different byte sizes** — re-encoded or lightly revised. Measured on this corpus: one briefing at 205,839 and 211,664 bytes, a Myton memo at 134,676 and 134,980. A near-duplicate is not merely untidy: it doubles the document's chunks and biases every retrieval toward whatever was duplicated, degrading the answers the feature exists to improve. The trade-off is deliberate and documented in the code — a genuinely different file sharing a name with one already on the record is skipped and stays in the email. (A draft and a "… Final.pdf" have different names and are correctly kept as two documents.)
- **The apply phase is time-budgeted (10 min ceiling), which it needed and the others did not.** Writing an update is instant; importing an attachment costs a document AI pass, measured at **30-60s each on the local model**. One thread carrying ten drawings is five minutes, so an unlucky batch could have run for hours inside the hourly cron and starved `predecide` — the only phase that makes the review queue SHRINK.
- **Also:** `REVIEW_REASON_LABELS` extracted to `src/lib/utils/review.ts` — there were already two divergent copies and `/decide` was about to need a third; a **"Email → Records"** card on `/settings/health` (this failure class is silent by construction, so it is now counted out loud); lead detail shows **sibling leads from the same email** (computed from the list already loaded, no fetch) and a **"Since this arrived"** activity feed; `scripts/backfill-correspondence.mjs` (refetch/route/apply/retriage, each idempotent and resumable).
- **⚠ BACKFILL MISTAKE, CAUGHT AND REVERTED — the two pipelines need DIFFERENT history windows.** `scripts/backfill-correspondence.mjs` passed `sinceDays: null` to every mailbox. That is right for the deal mailboxes (a project that started two years ago is still the project) and **wrong for the lead mailboxes**, which deliberately read only `DEFAULT_LEAD_HISTORY_DAYS = 90` because a perishable bid invitation stops being a lead. The run pulled **1,219 info@ threads between 90 days and a year old** (back to 2025-09-27) and queued **~14 hours of local-model triage** to turn dead solicitations into expired leads — at the daily lead-sweep cadence, a 16-day drain. Stopped mid-run, script corrected, and **1,260 threads deleted** under a scope guard proving none carried a lead or a link (0 orphans afterwards; threads 2,846 → 1,685 against 1,674 at the start, so 11 genuinely new). **The subtler half: killing the run left `mailbox_sync.page_token` SET with `state='running'`, and `fetchMailbox` RESUMES from that cursor — the next cron would have re-ingested everything just deleted.** info@ was restored to `state='complete'`, cursor cleared, `completed_at` back to its real 2026-09-03 value so the next sweep does a normal 8-day catch-up covering the outage. **Rule: after killing a sweep mid-run, clear `page_token` or the next cron resumes it.**
- **The lead re-triage was measured and then NOT run.** A read-only probe over the 18 threads whose text most looked multi-opportunity found **0 of 18** genuinely describing several deals — the enumeration was bullet formatting in bid invites, and the model correctly returned one opportunity each. Blanket re-triage would therefore have re-scored all 236 leads (±20 points of documented sampling noise, reshuffling the queue on nothing) to find nothing. The useful half happens by itself: a thread that actually GREW is requeued by the refresh and re-triaged under prompt 2.0 automatically, so multi-opportunity splitting applies retroactively to exactly the threads worth re-reading.
- **Backfill run: 25 links applied, 21 documents imported (424 chunks), 0 failed.** Real material that had been sitting in email is now on its project and searchable — the **Myton Rail Initiative predevelopment package** and its support letters, the **Stockton Power Nexus proposal**, the **CFS commission plan**, the West Wendover infrastructure master plan. Retrieval confirmed live: for questions only those files can answer they are the top hits (Stockton 0.693, West Wendover 0.543).
- **Verified against live Gmail and the live DB throughout, not fixtures:** growth detection found 4 genuinely grown threads on moose@'s first page (incl. a **Prefab Steel quote conversation stored as 1 message that is actually 5**); delta slicing unit-checked against real stored threads (split counts match `message_count` exactly); a full promote→link→growth→apply chain produced an **approved, embedded update containing ONLY the new message (145 chars, 1 heading)** and a re-run was a clean no-op; an inferred match staged **pending and unembedded** with a review-queue row. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint **at its documented 21-problem baseline** (a new `set-state-in-effect` was avoided by fetching in the open handler rather than an effect). **All test data removed — 0 residue** (projects back to 15, leads to 787, the test Drive folder trashed, the borrowed thread restored to its real message count).

**Done 2026-09-05 (website deal submissions — Drive folder → lead → project; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard is building a lead form on berwilson.com that captures a due-diligence checklist and creates one Drive folder per deal.** The question this answers: how that folder becomes a project here when the team decides to pursue it, without losing the checklist's structure or drifting from the folder afterwards. His picks (AskUserQuestion): JSON-in-Drive transport, documents copied in and indexed, checklist → `dd_items`, lands in the leads queue for a human to promote, nightly re-sync, and the form creates the folder **as moose@ under the platform's own OAuth client**.
- **The blocker that shaped everything: the platform is tailnet-only, so the form cannot POST to it.** `middleware.ts` admits nine cron paths and login; every inbound integration already works by pulling. Rather than re-expose the Studio (undone deliberately in July), the submission travels as **`_intake.json` written into the folder the form already creates**, and a cron reads it. Fidelity is unaffected — the structure the form collected is the structure that lands, with **no model pass on the way in**.
- **It is a second SOURCE on the existing leads module, not a second module.** A submitted deal is not a project (10 of 15 projects already sit in pursuit/capture/bid carrying notional pipeline); it is the tier before stage one. `leads.thread_id` **dropped NOT NULL** — every Gmail-facing consumer was already written defensively (`gmail-sync`/`draft-reply` filter `.not('thread_id','is',null)`, `score-phase` branches on a null thread), so that was the whole change. `idx_leads_thread` needed **no** alteration: Postgres treats NULLs as distinct.
- **Migration `20260905000001_deal_intake.sql` (APPLIED + PostgREST reloaded):** `leads.source` ('email'|'web_form'), `drive_folder_id`/`drive_folder_url` + a **partial unique index** (the idempotency latch — the 15-minute scan must never make a second lead for one folder), `intake_answers jsonb` (the manifest verbatim, so a mis-mapped question can be re-read rather than re-collected), and `projects.deal_folder_id`. `database.ts` hand-extended for the projects column.
- **`src/lib/deal-intake/`** — `checklist.ts` (the ONE definition, 28 questions across site/title/regulatory/environmental/financial/market/partner/legal/bonding, each carrying its `dd_items` category and severity; the form is built from a copy), `parse.ts` (hand-written validator, no zod — tolerant of everything except a title, accepts three checklist shapes because losing answers to a shape mismatch is the worst failure here), `scan.ts`, `diligence.ts`.
- **`src/lib/drive/import.ts`** — `importDriveFolder()`, modeled on `syncDriveKnowledge` rather than written fresh. Imported documents are stamped **`drive_published_id = <source file id>`**, without which `reconcileDrivePublishing` would upload every imported file straight back into the folder it was read from. Also `readDriveFolderText()`: a web lead's evidence is read **in memory, not staged** — most submissions are never promoted, and staging every one pays storage for deals we pass on.
- **Promotion ADOPTS the folder** (`deal_folder_id` = `drive_folder_id` = the website folder) so `publishRecordToDrive` uses it instead of creating a second one — the form creating it under this platform's OAuth client is what makes it app-created and writable under `drive.file`. Publishing is skipped for an adopted folder (every document is already in it). `originNote` and the player roles branch on source: a web deal's sender is a **"Sponsor / Developer"**, not an "Inviting Contractor".
- **Nightly re-sync** as a second phase of the existing 3:15am `drive-sync` cron (not a twelfth launchd job), running **independently** so a knowledge-folder fault cannot stop promoted projects picking up documents. New **`com.berwilson.cron-deal-intake`** every 15 minutes (installed + loaded), middleware allowlisted, **503 rather than a quiet skip** when unconfigured. New **Deal Intake card on `/settings/health`** with `empty` as its own state.
- **⚠ BUG FOUND WHILE TESTING, PRE-EXISTING AND LIVE: Drive change detection had never once worked.** `prior.drive_modified_at === file.modifiedTime` compares Drive's RFC 3339 `Z` string against a `timestamptz` that PostgREST round-trips with an offset (`…38.096Z` vs `…38.096+00:00`) — never equal. **The nightly knowledge sync has re-downloaded, re-summarized (a model call each) and re-embedded every file every night since August**, and the cron log shows `"updated":1,"unchanged":0` on ten consecutive runs. Fixed with a shared `driveFileUnchanged()` comparing **instants**, used by both the knowledge sync and the new import; the knowledge sync now reports `unchanged: 1` for the first time. **Rule: never string-compare a Drive `modifiedTime` against a stored `timestamptz`.**
- **Verified against the real Drive and the live DB, not fixtures:** 21-case parser/checklist unit check; a throwaway intake folder with a genuine document → scan created the lead with every field correct (`$88,600,000` parsed from a typed string, labels not keys in `key_facts`, the three unanswered required questions as `requirements`), and **run 2 was a clean no-op**; `readDriveFolderText` proved to carry a doc-unique fact and to exclude the manifest; real promotion → project with the folder adopted, document imported/indexed (`complete`, real summary), **21 dd_items of which exactly the 3 unanswered required ones are open**, an unknown checklist key filed under `Other`, both contacts in the directory as players; a document added mid-diligence imported by the nightly phase; **Ber AI retrieved the imported document as the top hit (0.569)** for a question only it could answer; the leads-page PostgREST embed confirmed to still return a thread-less lead; `/settings/health` and `/leads` rendered **authenticated as an admin** with the new card and copy present. `tsc` + `next build` clean, eslint at its documented 21-problem baseline. **All test data removed — 0 residue across leads/projects/documents/chunks/dd_items/project_players/parties, and the Drive folders deleted.**
- **⚠ Richard's manual steps — nothing arrives until these are done:** (1) create the parent **"Deal Intake"** folder in Drive and set **`GOOGLE_DEAL_INTAKE_FOLDER_ID`** in `.env.local`, then restart the app — until then the cron 503s and `/settings/health` says so; (2) build the form against **`deploy/deal-intake-form.md`**, which is the contract (folder created as moose@ under the platform's OAuth client id, `_intake.json` written LAST, checklist keys mirroring `checklist.ts`).
- **Deliberately not built:** any inbound HTTP endpoint; a `dd_templates` table (the form cannot read a tailnet-only platform, so the checklist lives in code); auto-creating a project without a human (§11 preserved); deal-folder import for opportunities and steel deals (the same `importDriveFolder` covers them when wanted).

**Done 2026-09-01 (mp3 recordings were rejected by the file picker — and the Whisper model had vanished; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's report: the Mac would not let him pick an `.mp3` when adding a meeting recording.** The restriction was purely in the browser: both recording inputs used `accept="audio/*,video/*"`, and a browser expands those wildcards through its OWN MIME→extension table — that expansion is where the macOS picker decided to grey out mp3. Nothing server-side objected (the `documents` bucket has `allowed_mime_types = NULL`, i.e. unrestricted) and `afconvert` decodes mp3 natively. **Fix: literal extensions alongside the wildcards**, in one shared `RECORDING_ACCEPT` in `src/lib/utils/meetings.ts` so the two inputs (`MeetingForm` create flow, `MeetingFiles` detail view) cannot drift apart. Scoped to containers `afconvert` can actually decode — offering a file the pipeline would choke on is worse than greying it out. **Rule: `accept="audio/*"` alone is NOT sufficient on macOS/iOS; list the extensions too.**
- **FOUND WHILE VERIFYING — far more serious than the reported bug: `ggml-large-v3-turbo.bin` was GONE.** `WHISPER_MODEL` still pointed at it and `whisperEnabled()` still returned true (it only checks that the env vars are *set*), so the platform looked perfectly configured while every transcription would have died on whisper-cli's opaque `failed to initialize whisper context`. Only the ~575KB dummy `for-tests-*.bin` stubs remained; the real ~1.5GB model was absent. **No data was damaged — both existing meetings have their transcripts and neither is in an error state — because nobody had tried to transcribe since it disappeared.** Re-downloaded (`cd ~/whisper.cpp/models && ./download-ggml-model.sh large-v3-turbo`).
- **Closed the silent-outage class, since the same shape has now bitten repeatedly** (the map archives and the OCR binary are the same pattern: build artifacts living OUTSIDE the repo that can vanish while config still looks right). New `whisperAvailable()` **stats the binary and the model** rather than trusting the env vars; `transcribeAudio` preflights it and throws a message naming the missing file and the exact re-download command; new `probeWhisper()` + a **Meeting Transcription card on `/settings/health`** (`fail` when configured-but-missing, `warn` on a non-Studio host where it is legitimately unset, `ok` otherwise).
- **Verified end-to-end with a real mp3, not just a typecheck:** a genuine 46-second mp3 → `afconvert` → whisper produced an accurate transcript; then the platform's **own `transcribeAudio()`** was called with that file named `board-recording.mp3` and returned 732 correct characters in 1.8s. The failure path was exercised too — pointing `WHISPER_MODEL` at a nonexistent file yields the useful message, not the opaque one. All six containers in the new accept list (`m4a/caf/wav/aiff/aac/flac`) confirmed to round-trip through `afconvert`. `tsc` + eslint (touched files) + full build clean; deployed and the app restarted; **`/settings/health` rendered authenticated shows "Meeting Transcription — Whisper ready"** (checked because this repo has previously wired a feature that then rendered nowhere).
- **⚠ Housekeeping worth knowing: a CONCURRENT Claude session committed `git add -A` at 08:03 mid-way through this work**, sweeping these six files into its own commit (`574820c "feat: delete a lead from the queue with one click"`). Nothing was lost and the tree is correct, but the message describes only half of what landed. History was deliberately NOT rewritten — another session was actively working in the same repo. **When two sessions share this folder, `git add -A` will absorb the other's in-flight edits.**

**Done 2026-08-31 (the brief goes weekly — Mondays, TL;DR first, ordered by priority; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's call: a daily brief was too often to be read; make it weekly on Mondays, lead with a TL;DR, and order it by priority.** All three are prompt + schedule changes — no new data, no migration.
- **Cadence:** `com.berwilson.cron-daily-brief` gained `Weekday 1` (Monday 6:30am local); **patched and reloaded on the live launchd agent**, and the template in `deploy/` matches. **The route path stays `/api/cron/daily-brief`** — renaming it would mean re-installing the launchd job, editing the middleware allowlist and the deploy script's service loop for zero user-visible gain. Everything the reader sees says weekly (title is now `Weekly Brief — week of …`).
- **The dedupe guard had to widen with the cadence.** It checked "already generated today", which for a weekly brief would let a Tuesday retry or a hand-triggered run write a second brief for the same week. It now measures from Monday 00:00 UTC.
- **Prompt rewritten (`weekly-brief-v2`).** A **TL;DR** section leads: 3-5 one-line bullets, most consequential first, what/who/by-when — someone who reads only that knows the week. Sections below it are in priority order (Decide This Week → Overdue and Slipping → Steering Check → The Week Ahead → Capital Raise → Portfolio Pulse → Decisions Pending Follow-Through), with an explicit rule to rank items **inside** each section by money at stake and deadline proximity, overdue outranking not-yet-due. Word budget 600 → 700.
- **The Chat cap is why TL;DR-first also matters mechanically:** `broadcastBrief` trims at 3800 chars from the **tail**, so the most important content is now structurally the part that survives a trim. Measured on a realistic dry run: 528 words / **3096 chat chars**, comfortably under the cap.
- **Health check retuned:** "Daily Brief Cron" → **"Weekly Brief Cron"**, threshold 36h → **8 days**. A missed Monday still fails, but a brief written Monday must not read as broken by Sunday.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + eslint (touched files) + full build clean; launchd reports the new calendar interval (`Weekday => 1`); the live route returns 401 unauthed and, authed, correctly answers *"Brief already generated this week"* against this morning's existing brief (the guard working); and the **new prompt was dry-run against the local model with realistic data** — TL;DR first, everything ranked, 78.6s. Deployed (app 200).

**Done 2026-08-18 (task "why" polish + per-member task digest notifications; DEPLOYED to the Studio + MIGRATED):**
- **Context:** team is scaling as big projects land; members are getting abbreviated (`member`) logins. Two asks: make task "why" easy to capture but discreet when unused, and give each member a reliable daily push of their overdue/upcoming work (they're often off the tailnet, so the message must CARRY the tasks, not just link).
- **"Why" was already fully built** (schema + API + `BoardTask` carry `what`/`why`/`how`; detail sheet edited them). UI polish only, **no schema/API change:** (1) board tiles show `why` as a muted one-line sub-title when present (hidden for done/empty); (2) the detail sheet collapses empty what/why/how to a subtle `+ Add why` affordance (re-collapses on blur-if-empty; per-field `expanded` state seeded from loaded values); (3) quick-add gained a collapsed **"+ Add details (why / what / how)"** disclosure (collapsed by default; API already accepts the fields).
- **NEW outbound-email capability (the app had none).** Originally via Microsoft Graph `Mail.Send`; **re-pointed at Gmail `users.messages.send` on 2026-08-24** when Graph was removed (see that entry) behind a channel abstraction so Telegram can drop in later. `src/lib/notify/index.ts` (`notify({channel,to,subject,html})`; only `email` wired), `sendMail()` added to `microsoft-graph.ts`, `Mail.Send` added to `SCOPES`. **REQUIRES a one-time reconnect via `/api/email/oauth`** (health page shows a "Reconnect Mailbox" action until the token carries Mail.Send).
- **Per-member daily digest.** `src/lib/notify/task-digest.ts`: `buildMemberDigests()` groups open due-dated tasks by active member-with-email into Overdue + Due-next-7-days (quiet if neither); `renderDigestEmail()` returns subject + HTML that **lists the tasks inline** (title, days-overdue/due, project/opp tag, `why` sub-line) — the "Open my tasks" link (`APP_URL`) is only convenience. New query `fetchTasksForDigest()` in `src/lib/tasks/queries.ts` (separate from `fetchOpenTasks`/`TaskSummary` which the AI briefs share). New cron `src/app/api/cron/task-digest/route.ts` (CRON_SECRET fail-closed, `maxDuration=300`), added to the middleware public allowlist, **idempotent per member per day** via new `notification_log` table.
- **launchd cron** `com.berwilson.cron-task-digest` (**Monday 7:00am local, weekly**) — template in `deploy/`, added to `deploy-to-studio.sh`'s service loop, and **installed + loaded live on the Studio**. New env **`APP_URL`** (set to the tailnet URL in the Studio `.env.local`).
- **`/settings/health`** gained a **Task Digest** card; the mailbox card now reports the Google Workspace connection instead (the Microsoft card is gone).
- **Cloud-reference cleanup (per Richard):** `package.json` `gen-types` no longer uses `--linked` (now `--db-url $SUPABASE_DB_URL`); `README.md` rewritten from the create-next-app/Vercel boilerplate to the self-hosted reality.
- **Schema:** migration `20260818000001_notification_log.sql` — **APPLIED to the Studio Postgres + PostgREST schema reloaded**; `database.ts` hand-extended. `tsc` + `next build` clean. Verified live: `/api/cron/task-digest` returns 200 (`candidates:0` right now — no open task has a due date yet) and 401 unauth; app restarted on the new build (HTTP 200).
- **Deploy caveat:** these edits were made **directly on the Studio copy** (`~/berwilson-platform`). `deploy/deploy-to-studio.sh` rsyncs from the **MacBook source repo with `--delete`** — so the same changes must be mirrored into the MacBook repo (and committed to GitHub) or the next MacBook-driven deploy will overwrite them. **Also pending:** the one-time Microsoft reconnect for `Mail.Send`. **Future:** Telegram channel (branch in `notify/index.ts` + `telegram_chat_id` on `team_members`); per-member send prefs only if asked.


**Done 2026-07-29 (Meetings — recording upload in the create flow, Opportunity-level meetings, attendee pick lists, contact activity logging; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's report: he went to record a meeting and saw only a "paste transcript" box — the Whisper upload/transcribe he expected looked missing.** Root cause was placement, not a missing feature: the Whisper pipeline (07-28) only lived in the meeting **detail** view (reachable after saving); the **create** form (`MeetingForm`) offered just a paste box. Fixed + three additions he asked for. His picks (AskUserQuestion): **audio + video** recordings (afconvert extracts the audio track from .mp4/.mov — verified afconvert lists `mp4f` decode); tagging a contact **only logs the meeting** on their profile (no auto-add to project players).
- **One-shot recording upload in the create form:** `MeetingForm` gained a "Recording (audio or video)" picker; on **Create record** it saves the meeting, uploads the file to the new meeting id (reuses the exact `signed-upload-url` → `uploadToSignedUrl` → `POST /api/documents {meeting_id}` flow from `MeetingFiles`), then fires `POST /api/meetings/[id]/transcribe` and advances to the detail view (which already polls `transcription_status`). Copy tells the user Whisper writes the transcript + summary automatically.
- **Video support:** `MeetingFiles`/`MeetingDetail` treat `audio/*` **or** `video/*` as the transcribable "recording" (input `accept="audio/*,video/*"`, inline `<video>` player); transcribe route allows video mime; `whisper.ts` unchanged (afconvert decodes the container). Bucket already unrestricted + 500MB on the Studio.
- **Opportunity-level meetings (3rd scope):** migration `20260802000001_meeting_opportunity_scope.sql` (**APPLIED to the Studio DB** + PostgREST restarted; `database.ts` hand-extended) adds `meetings.opportunity_id` (FK→opportunities on delete set null) + index, widens `meetings_scope_check` to `company|project|opportunity`, and replaces the pairing constraint → `meetings_scope_target_check` (exactly the matching target set per scope). `MeetingScope`/`meetingScope`/`OPPORTUNITY_KINDS` in `meetings.ts`; `parse.ts` requires opportunity_id + index_ai defaults ON for project/opportunity; `access.ts` opportunity→`canAccessOpportunity`; embedding needs no change (opportunity meetings have project_id null + board false → document-scoped chunk). A **Meetings section** renders on the opportunity detail page via `MeetingsView scope="opportunity"`.
- **Attendee pick lists (new `MeetingAttendees.tsx`, used on all 3 scopes):** a "＋ Ber Wilson team…" dropdown (active `team_members`, carries `team_member_id`+`party_id`), a contacts-directory type-ahead (reuses the meeting-intake `ContactPicker` pattern, sets `party_id`), and manual name/title/company fallback. `MeetingAttendee` jsonb shape extended with `party_id`/`team_member_id`; `parseAttendees` carries them (accepts `title`/`company` aliases). Pages pass `teamMembers`+`contacts` via new server helper `src/lib/meetings/picker-data.ts`.
- **Contact activity logging:** new `src/lib/meetings/attendee-activity.ts` `syncAttendeeActivity` — idempotent reconcile (via `metadata->>meeting_id`) of `activity_log` rows (`table_name='parties'`, `action='meeting_attended'`) for each attendee with a `party_id`; called from POST + PATCH (when attendees change); DELETE sweeps them (no FK cascade to activity_log). Surfaced as a **Meetings section on the contact Activity tab** (`/contacts/[id]?tab=activity`) linking to the record.
- **Verified:** tsc + eslint (touched files) + full build clean; migration applied + **rolled-back live psql round-trip** (company/project/opportunity inserts succeed, opportunity-without-opportunity_id rejected by the constraint, 0 rows persisted); PostgREST 200 for `meetings?select=opportunity_id`; `metadata->>meeting_id` REST filter returns the row (activity idempotency); afconvert `.mp4` decode confirmed; deployed (app 200; `/company/board` 307, `/api/meetings` 401/POST 401 unauthed). **Eyeball when convenient (authed):** create a project meeting from the form with an `.m4a` AND an `.mp4`, watch the banner poll to transcript+summary; add a team member + a searched contact + a manual attendee, save, and confirm the contact shows the meeting under Activity (and that removing them on edit clears it, no dupes); create an opportunity meeting and confirm Ber AI retrieval.

**Done 2026-07-28 (Meetings — local Whisper transcription: audio → verbatim transcript → auto-summary; DEPLOYED + INFRA):**
- **Richard's follow-up to the Meetings module: add local Whisper transcription** (more accurate than iPhone native for multi-speaker/jargon/long-form legal records). Three-artifact model he specified: **audio** (verbatim voice) + **transcript** (Whisper literal text) + **summary** (AI, useful, non-authoritative). Board **minutes** stay hand-authored; the auto-summary never touches them.
- **Whisper runs 100% locally on the Studio** — `whisper.cpp` built from source with **Metal** (cmake via a `~/whisper-venv`, since the box has no brew/cmake; `~/whisper.cpp/build/bin/whisper-cli`), model **`ggml-large-v3-turbo`** (~1.5GB, `~/whisper.cpp/models/`). **No ffmpeg** — the macOS built-in **`/usr/bin/afconvert`** decodes m4a/aac → 16kHz mono WAV, which whisper.cpp reads natively. LM Studio uses ~20GB of the 36GB RAM; turbo adds ~1.6GB only during a run (CLI per-file, no resident service). **To swap to max-accuracy large-v3 or rebuild:** `download-ggml-model.sh large-v3` + point `WHISPER_MODEL` at it. See [[whisper-transcription-studio]].
- **Code:** `src/lib/ai/whisper.ts` (`transcribeAudio(buffer, fileName)` — spawns afconvert then whisper-cli `-bs 5` for accuracy, auto language, temp-file cleanup; `whisperEnabled()` gated on `WHISPER_BIN`/`WHISPER_MODEL`). `src/lib/meetings/transcribe.ts` (`runMeetingTranscription` — background: download audio → Whisper → set `meetings.transcript` + save an **immutable `Transcript — {title}.txt` document** (doc_type `transcript`, delete-and-replace on re-run) → **auto-summary only if none yet** (reuses `extractMeeting`, never overwrites minutes) → refresh the minutes doc; settles `meetings.transcription_status` idle|processing|complete|error, never throws). New column `meetings.transcription_status` (migration extended + applied). `POST /api/meetings/[id]/transcribe {document_id, force?}` (fires the background job, no-ops if already transcribed unless `force`) + `GET /api/meetings/[id]` (poll meeting+files). Transcript doc embeds for Ber AI when `index_ai` (project-scoped chunk / is_company for board), same gating as the minutes doc.
- **UI:** audio upload in `MeetingFiles` **auto-triggers** transcription (`onAudioUploaded`); `MeetingDetail` shows a status banner (Transcribing…/ready/failed) + **Transcribe / Re-transcribe** button, and **polls `GET /api/meetings/[id]`** every 5s while processing, lifting the finished transcript + summary + new transcript file up to the parent. Env wired into the Studio by `deploy/deploy-to-studio.sh` (`WHISPER_BIN`/`WHISPER_MODEL`, Studio-specific paths).
- **REGRESSION found + fixed (was live briefly):** the meetings migration's bucket line `allowed_mime_types = allowed_mime_types || array[audio…]` — on the Studio `allowed_mime_types` was **NULL (unrestricted)**, and `NULL || array` collapses to **audio-only**, which silently began **rejecting pdf/text/docx uploads platform-wide**. Caught by the transcription test (transcript `.txt` + minutes `.md` uploads failed). Fixed: bucket reset to NULL (unrestricted, its original state — audio was never MIME-blocked there; only the 500MB size bump mattered) + the migration UPDATE made NULL-safe (`case when allowed_mime_types is null then null else … end`). **Lesson: never `NULL || array` on a bucket's allowed_mime_types.**
- **Verified end-to-end on the Studio** (alias-loader script against the live stack): real `say`-generated m4a → afconvert → whisper-cli produced an accurate verbatim transcript ("$150,000 over 12 months"), the local LLM auto-summary ran, all **three artifacts** (audio + `transcript:….txt` + `Meeting —….md`) were created and **cascade-deleted cleanly** (0 orphans); tsc+eslint+build clean; deployed (whisper env present in the running app; transcribe route 401 unauthed). **Eyeball when convenient:** upload a real `.m4a` in the UI and watch the banner poll to "Transcript ready," then confirm the transcript + summary + `Transcript —….txt` file appear.

**Done 2026-07-28 (Meetings & Interaction Records — board/governance register + per-project client-interaction log; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: track board meetings + per-project client interactions for liability/compliance, with a place to upload the iPhone audio recording + a transcript/script.** His picks (AskUserQuestion): **store audio + paste the iOS-generated transcript** (no Whisper/new AI service); **manual board minutes** (authoritative legal text, no AI wording risk) but **AI-assist for project interaction summaries**; board register lives as a **Company → Board tab**; **per-meeting "Include in Ber AI" toggle** (board OFF by default, project ON) for confidentiality.
- **Why a new module:** the existing Meeting Notes Intake (`/intake` Meeting tab, `email_intake_sessions`) is *ephemeral* — it fans a transcript onto other records and keeps no browsable meeting entity. Compliance needs a **durable, browsable record with an approval state**. New `meetings` table is that entity (NOT the intake sessions).
- **New `meetings` table** (migration `20260801000001_meetings.sql`, **APPLIED to the Studio DB** via ssh+docker psql + PostgREST restarted; `database.ts` hand-extended, `Meeting` alias): scope company|project (+project_id, check-constrained as a pair), kind board|client|site_visit|call|internal|other, title/date/time/location, meeting_type_label/chair/secretary (board), attendees jsonb, summary, **minutes** (authoritative body), **transcript** (pasted), decisions jsonb, status draft|approved (+approved_at/by), confidential, **index_ai**, minutes_document_id. Plain-text vocab + all helpers in `src/lib/utils/meetings.ts` (`composeMeetingDocument`, badge maps); shared write validation in `src/lib/meetings/parse.ts` (create + partial PATCH); access in `src/lib/meetings/access.ts` (`canAccessMeeting`: admin always, project→canAccessProject, board→admin-only).
