**Done 2026-07-03 (activity-log user attribution + email-intake copy fix):**
- **The activity log now shows WHO did it.** App writes go through the service-role client, so `auth.uid()` was null inside `log_activity()` and every entry logged as "system". Fix: `createAdminClient(actor?)` can stamp `x-actor-id`/`x-actor-email` headers onto the PostgREST request; new `actorAdminClient()` (`src/lib/auth/viewer.ts`, built on the request-cached `getViewer()`) resolves the signed-in user and returns a stamped client; migration `20260704000006_activity_actor_attribution.sql` (**APPLIED to prod**) teaches the trigger to fall back to those headers when `auth.uid()` is null. Swapped into every user-initiated mutation route on tracked tables (tasks create/patch/delete, projects delete + server actions, updates save, documents insert/update/delete, certification docs, review approve/reject, proposals intake/confirm/upload-chunk, email-ingestion confirm). Routes already on the session-scoped server client (compliance/dd-items/financing/milestones/stage/updates PUT) were always attributed — untouched. Crons/AI/background writes still log as "system", which is accurate. **Rule for new code: user-initiated mutations on tracked tables use `actorAdminClient()`, not `createAdminClient()`.** Verified end-to-end (trigger test in a rolled-back transaction + a PostgREST header-forwarding echo test). No schema change — gen-types not needed.
- **Email Research copy fixed:** the loading state said "Keep this tab open" — wrong. The run is a synchronous server function (`maxDuration` 300s) that completes even if the browser navigates away; the pending session is staged at the end and shows under Recent sessions for later review. Copy now says leaving is safe.

**Done 2026-07-03 (user management + role-based access — foundation):**
- **Four role presets** (`src/lib/auth/permissions.ts`): `admin` (everything), `executive` (Tasks + Objectives, full edit), `project_manager` (granted projects/opportunities + tasks within them; grants on a parent project cascade to children), `member` (own task list only). Anything not explicitly allowed for a role is admin-only by default — new routes are born private.
- **Schema** (migration `20260704000005_user_access.sql`): `team_members` gains `auth_user_id` (unique, links to Supabase Auth), `email`, `role` (existing Richard/Eric rows set to admin); new `access_grants` table (member × project|opportunity). `database.ts` hand-extended.
- **Enforcement, three layers:** (1) middleware resolves the signed-in user's role and gates page/API path prefixes (redirect to `/tasks` / JSON 403); (2) `src/lib/auth/viewer.ts` — `getViewer()` (request-cached), `canAccessProject` (walks parent chain), `canAccessOpportunity`, `filterTasksForViewer`, `canCreateTask` — wired into: tasks API (list filter, create/patch/delete guards, notes), team-members POST, projects list/detail-layout/new/actions/PATCH/stage/DELETE/parents, documents upload/insert/delete/signed-url, milestones POST/PATCH, opportunities list/detail/edit/actions/PATCH/notes/docs; (3) RLS unchanged — still defense-in-depth (§8); move to grant-keyed RLS when external partners get accounts.
- **Safety rules in `getViewer` (mirrored in middleware):** pre-migration (columns missing) → everyone admin (today's behavior, code ships first); linked → their role; bootstrap (no linked, active **admin** exists yet) → unlinked users are admin (fixed same day — the first version ended bootstrap on ANY link, so inviting a PM first demoted the unlinked executive logins to member mid-session); unlinked once an admin is linked → member. Last-active-admin demotion blocked server-side.
- **People-data rule (Richard's call):** executives = full edit on tasks/objectives; PMs get a **limited players list** on granted projects (name/role/company/email — no `/contacts` profile links, no add/edit/remove, no DD notes). Directory/contacts stay admin-only.
- **AI + cross-portfolio surfaces are admin-only** for now: Ask Ber AI dock, ⌘K palette/search, quick upload hidden for non-admins in the shell; `/api/ai/*`, `/intel`, dashboard, review, activity, email intake, proposals all outside non-admin allowlists. Per-grant RAG scoping is deliberate future work.
- **`/settings/users` (admin-only; sidebar System group + mobile More):** role legend, member cards (role dropdown, activate/deactivate, grant chips + editor for PMs), invite modal (new person or link an existing unlinked member; email invite via `admin.auth.admin.inviteUserByEmail` → existing `/auth/confirm` → set-password flow; already-registered emails just link instead of failing). API: `api/admin/users` (GET/POST, 409 pre-migration), `api/admin/users/[id]` (PATCH role/active/grants).
- **Known v1 limits:** PM mutations on updates/diligence/financing/players/parties are blocked by the API allowlist (read-only on their project pages); executives see project names as task tags (by design — they run the whole board). `tsc` + eslint + full `next build` clean. Local runtime smoke impossible (Supabase env vars are marked Sensitive in Vercel and pull empty; pre-existing situation).

- **Agent streams.** `/api/ai/agent` gains SSE mode (`stream: true`): live tool-activity events ("Checking search knowledge base…") + token-by-token answer streaming; legacy JSON mode kept. `runAgent` now uses `generateContentStream` with optional `onToolCall`/`onTextDelta` callbacks; `maxDuration=300`.
- **Ambient Ask Ber AI.** New `AskBerAIDock` (global slide-over, mounted in the shell): header button, **⌘J/Ctrl+J**, or `window` event `open-ber-ai`; stays mounted when closed so the conversation survives reopen. Context-aware — auto-scopes to the project on `/projects/[id]*`. The ⌘K palette has a pinned "Ask Ber AI: <query>" first row that hands the typed query to the dock. The two inline `AskBerAIPanel` embeds (dashboard, project layout) were deleted — one surface now.

**Done 2026-07-02 (universal RAG + in-platform Email Research — n8n retired):**
- **Feature A — universal cross-CRM query.** The RAG architecture was sound but coverage had holes; closed them:
  - **Full PDF text is now indexed.** All three document-upload routes (`api/documents/upload`, `api/documents`, `api/opportunities/documents`) run a second Gemini transcription pass (`src/lib/ai/document-text.ts`, PDFs ≤15MB, `maxTokens 60000`), store it in new `documents.extracted_text` / `opportunity_documents.extracted_text`, and embed the FULL text instead of the 2-3 sentence summary (summary remains the fallback for oversize/failed extraction). `maxDuration=300` on those routes.
  - **Opportunities are searchable.** New chunk columns `opportunity_id` / `opportunity_document_id` / `source_type`; new embedders in `embeddings.ts` (`embedOpportunityDocument` / `embedOpportunitySnapshot` (delete-and-replace on create/edit/PATCH) / `embedOpportunityNote` / `embedOpportunityReport`), all built on a shared `insertChunkTolerant` helper that drops unknown columns (PGRST204) and detects the old check constraint (23514) → warn+skip pre-migration. Wired into opportunity create/edit/PATCH, notes POST, doc upload, and email-ingestion confirm.
  - **4 new agent tools** (`agent-tools.ts`): `list_opportunities`, `query_opportunity` (fuzzy name resolve + notes + doc metadata), `search_tasks` (assignee/project/opportunity/status filters, latest note), `get_document_content` (reads `extracted_text` so the agent can quote sources; tolerant retry). Agent prompt now explains the opportunities-vs-projects split. `search_knowledge_base` + synthesize citations label opportunity chunks (`Opportunity: <name>`).
  - **One-time backfill:** `api/admin/backfill-embeddings` (batched; targets `project_documents` / `opportunity_documents` / `opportunities` / `opportunity_notes`; 409 with a clear message pre-migration) + `BackfillCard` on `/intel` that loops until done (stall guard). Remove the card after the backfill runs clean.
  - Migration **`20260703000001_universal_rag.sql`**: chunk columns + relaxed `chunks_source_check` + `extracted_text` columns + `match_chunks` DROP/recreate with **identical args** and extended return shape (so `match-chunks.ts` needed no change; the old RPC pre-migration just returns rows without the new fields).
- **Feature B — Email Research moved fully in-platform (n8n / Mac Studio / local Qwen retired).**
  - New `src/lib/integrations/graph-search.ts`: `searchConversations` (Graph `$search`, KQL-escaped, client-side date filter — `$search` can't combine with `$filter`/`$orderby`; groups hits by `conversationId`, newest-first, cap 15) and `fetchConversationMessages` (`$filter=conversationId eq`, **no $orderby** — Graph rejects the combo; JS sort, most recent 30 kept). **Multi-mailbox (added same day):** every run sweeps `RESEARCH_MAILBOXES` = tuaone@ + info@ + moose@berwilson.com using tuaone's single OAuth grant via `/users/{mailbox}/…` — requires the `Mail.Read.Shared` scope (added to SCOPES; **reconnect via `api/email/oauth` once to pick it up**) plus tuaone having delegated read access to the shared mailboxes in M365. Per-mailbox failures degrade to a note in the report; threads/messages deduped across mailboxes by `conversationId`/`internetMessageId`.
  - New `api/email-research/run` (`maxDuration=300`, auth-gated): search → per-thread transcript (`extractPlainText`, 6k chars/msg) → attachments (deduped name+size across reply chains, max 3/thread; inline/>10MB/non-PDF-image skipped with a note line in the report) → Gemini flash extraction per attachment → one markdown report (~190k char cap, oldest threads trimmed with a note) → `analyzeEmailReport` → `{session_id}` → client redirects straight to the `/email-ingestion/[id]` review screen. No auto-confirm — human review stays mandatory.
  - `EmailResearchForm` reworked: synchronous run with a "takes 1–4 minutes" loading state + time-range select (90d/1y/all); redirects to review on success. Manual paste on `/email-ingestion` still works as a fallback.
  - **Removed:** `api/email-research/trigger`, `api/email-ingestion/inbound` (+ its `middleware.ts` public-allowlist line), the `n8n/` directory, and the three n8n env vars (see §7 — Richard deletes them from Vercel). `SYSTEM_USER_ID` kept as the auth fallback.
  - **Confirm-time RAG tie-in:** on email-ingestion confirm, project-kind stores the report as an approved `updates` row (visible on the project's Updates tab) + `embedUpdate`; opportunity-kind runs `embedOpportunityReport` + snapshot. The handoff document itself becomes queryable from `/intel`.
- `tsc` + eslint clean on all touched files; `next build` clean. Both features pushed to main (auto-deploys).

**Done 2026-07-02 (Email Research trigger + automatic ingestion) — SUPERSEDED same day: the n8n trigger/inbound plumbing described below was replaced by the in-platform run above; kept for history:**
- **Closed the loop both ways** so reports arrive automatically instead of being copy-pasted. Two distinct secrets: `N8N_WEBHOOK_SECRET` (outbound trigger) and `INGESTION_INBOUND_SECRET` (inbound delivery) — never reused, never client-side.
- **Trigger:** new authenticated page **`/email-research`** (`EmailResearchForm`) — search term + optional export label + "Run Email Research". Fire-and-forget: it POSTs to `api/email-research/trigger`, which (auth-gated) makes a server-to-server POST to `process.env.N8N_WEBHOOK_URL` with header `X-Webhook-Secret: N8N_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and body `{ searchTerm, exportLabel }`, returns as soon as the webhook is dispatched (10s abort cap), and never leaks the URL/secret. Confirmation tells the user the report will show under Email Ingestion > Recent.
- **Inbound:** new **`api/email-ingestion/inbound`** (called by n8n, not a browser) — added to `middleware.ts`'s public allowlist (exact path `=== '/api/email-ingestion/inbound'`); its only auth is header `X-Ingestion-Secret === INGESTION_INBOUND_SECRET` (401 otherwise). Accepts `{ raw_text, label }` and runs the **exact same shared processing path** as the manual paste flow. Lands as a `pending` session under Recent — **no auto-confirm**; the human review/confirm step stays mandatory.
- **Refactor (no behavior change):** the analyze core was extracted into `src/lib/email-ingestion/analyze.ts` (`analyzeEmailReport({ rawText, label, userId })`, `EmailIntakeError` with HTTP status, `SYSTEM_USER_ID`). Both `api/email-ingestion/analyze` (manual paste/upload — user resolution + storage read stay in the route) and `api/email-ingestion/inbound` call it, so there's one source of truth. The manual paste/upload flow is unchanged (same request/response contract).
- **Env:** `N8N_WEBHOOK_URL`, `N8N_WEBHOOK_SECRET`, `INGESTION_INBOUND_SECRET` documented in §7 (values supplied separately, server-only). **Nav:** `/email-research` added to sidebar Intelligence group, mobile More, command palette. `tsc` + eslint clean.

**Done 2026-07-02 (Email Ingestion module):**

Closes the "email an opportunity → Ber AI assesses → you decide → records created" loop for existing Outlook threads. **Done 2026-08-24 (debug pass — mail intake unstuck, AI reaches the whole app, steel cost basis fixed):**
- **The mailbox sweep had silently stopped ingesting new mail.** `fetchAllMailboxes` skipped any mailbox whose `state` was `complete`, and both mailboxes reached `complete` on 2026-08-24 at 04:44 — so from that moment the hourly cron re-summarized the existing backlog and fetched nothing, forever. The file's own docstring claimed the opposite ("re-running after completion picks up only new mail"), which is what the fix now makes true. A finished backfill drops into a **catch-up pass**: page one of a window derived from `completed_at` (elapsed days + 2, capped at 30), max 3 pages, stopping at the first page with nothing new — Gmail lists newest-first, so that page proves everything below it is already stored. Counters accumulate, `started_at` is preserved, and `completed_at` becomes the marker the next window measures from (no migration needed). Verified live: both mailboxes caught up, skipped 14 + 71 known threads, and picked up 2 genuinely new ones (533 → 535).
- **Follow-ups on a staged deal no longer propose a second record.** `clusterUnassigned` only ever attached new threads to `open` clusters, so a reply on an already-staged deal formed a fresh cluster and would have been staged as a SECOND pending project/opportunity for the same deal. Staged clusters now absorb follow-ups (and stay staged, so nothing is re-proposed); `dismissed` is still excluded, since a thread on a rejected deal deserves reconsideration. This never bit only because the fetch bug meant no new mail was arriving.
- **A sales rep's deal saved with no cost basis at all.** In `saveServices`, cost came from the form only when `canSeeFinancials` — and the form itself gates cost derivation the same way — so a `steel_sales` user creating a deal produced line items with `cost = null`. That reads as **100% margin**, and commission is paid on commissionable margin, so the rep was credited a commission on the full sale price. Cost for per-SF services (materials/assembly) is now derived **server-side** from `cost_per_sqft ?? DEFAULT_STEEL_COST_PER_SQFT` × square feet whenever nothing survived the form, and the basis used is persisted. A deliberate `0` from a financials user is still respected.
- **The AI could not see most of the app.** `agentTools` covered projects/opportunities/investors/tasks/documents and nothing else — asked about steel, priorities, meetings, or correspondence it would answer from an empty result. Added **14 read-only tools** in `src/lib/ai/agent-tools-modules.ts`, spliced into `agentTools` and `executeToolCall`: steel (`list_steel_deals`, `query_steel_deal`, `get_steel_summary`, `get_steel_payouts`, `get_steel_marketing`), `list_objectives`, `search_meetings` + `get_meeting_content` (confidential minutes withheld), `search_email_threads` + `get_email_thread`, `get_intake_status`, `get_recent_activity`, `list_team_members`, `get_dino_summary`. The sweep tables go through `sweepDb()` — they post-date the last type generation. The local path builds its declarations from the same array, so all 36 tools work on Qwen as well as Gemini. `prompts/agent.ts` gained routing lines plus two paragraphs: steel's commission-payable-only-when-collected rule, and the fact that correspondence often exists for a deal with no CRM record yet (search email before concluding nothing exists).
- **Data purged** (backed up first to `~/berwilson-data/backups/purge-*.sql.gz`): 4 exact-duplicate contacts (same email, zero references anywhere — verified against all 11 FKs into `parties`); 4 placeholder steel deals (`Architect deal 1/2`, `LA deal 2`, `Playground housing extension` — no customer, no SF, no price); and **IHC Steel's missing cost basis** backfilled at the $20/SF default. That one deal was the single biggest distortion in the app: 100,000 SF at $2M with no cost read as $2M of pure margin and projected Richard a ~$200k commission. Steel now reports **4 deals, $510k margin on $3.6M revenue (14%)** instead of 8 deals at 70%, and total projected commissions fell from $260k to $60k.
- **Also corrected:** two definitions of "won" were being mixed in one answer (`isOpenStage` = not paid/not lost, vs marketing's `isWon` = order placed or beyond), so the steel summary reported a deal as both open and won. It now reports `in_pursuit` / `won` / `collected` / `lost` with the definitions stated inline. Stale build cache (macOS ` 2`/` 3` duplicate files) was breaking `tsc`; removed, and the stale `CLAUDE 2.md` snapshot (which still claimed Microsoft Graph) deleted. The "Reality" and "Working" lines above were themselves out of date — corrected to Mac Studio + Gmail.
- **Verification:** `tsc` clean, `next build` clean, eslint unchanged from baseline (14 errors / 6 warnings, all pre-existing `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect`, `react-hooks/purity`, and `no-img-element` — data-loading effects and `<img>` usage, not runtime faults). Every new tool was exercised against the live database through a temporary route, which was removed and the middleware allowlist restored byte-identical.
- **Merge with the Studio's Aug 18 push (same session).** `origin/main` carried two commits that never reached the MacBook (the Studio working-tree sync + the weekly task digest), while the local branch carried the Gmail migration — so the histories had diverged and `git fetch` was itself broken by a stale duplicate ref (`.git/refs/remotes/origin/main 2`, the same macOS ` 2`/` 3` duplication that broke `tsc`). Removed the ref, then merged: kept the Gmail-era side everywhere the two disagreed (mailbox health, the disabled `gen-types` guard, the middleware allowlist minus the dead Microsoft OAuth callback), and kept the digest work. **The digest's outbound email was the one real casualty** — `src/lib/notify` imported `sendMail` from the deleted `microsoft-graph.ts`. Ported it: `sendMail()` now lives in `google-workspace.ts` on Gmail `users.messages.send`, and `gmail.send` (send-only; it cannot read or modify) was added to `SCOPES` and to `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs`. Existing refresh tokens are unaffected — the refresh call does not send `scope` — but they were minted without it, so **the digest cannot actually send until each mailbox re-consents**: `node scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs --only <addr>`. `sendMail` detects that case and returns that instruction instead of a bare 403, and the Task Digest health card says the same.
- **Not done / open:** the 12 `set-state-in-effect` findings are a real refactor across 12 components and were left alone deliberately. ~~The Studio still runs the previous build~~ **DEPLOYED 2026-08-24 11:28** — the mail catch-up is live and confirmed working (see the entry below).

**Done 2026-08-25 (gap-closing pass after the lead build — silent outages, agent blindness, no push, unbacked secrets; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Mail was dead and NOTHING said so — the worst of the five.** After the crash took the OAuth tokens, `/api/cron/email-sweep` returned 503 *before* the sweep started, so no `mailbox_sync` row was ever written. Both mailboxes still read `state=complete`, the dashboard alert keys on `state='failed'`, and so the platform looked perfectly healthy while no mail had reached the CRM for hours. This is exactly the failure class `/settings/health` was built for in July, reappearing through a path that bypassed it — health is pull-only, and nobody opens it when nothing looks wrong. **New `recordSweepUnavailable(mailboxes, reason)` in `email-sweep/db.ts`**, called from both cron routes' unconfigured branch: upserts every mailbox it owns to `state='failed'` with the reason. Safe against resume — `fetchMailbox` decides where to restart from `completed_at`, never from `state`, so a flagged mailbox still catches up over a short window instead of re-reading years; the next good run overwrites it, so it self-heals. Never throws (recording a failure must not turn a 503 into a 500). **The dashboard now also carries the reason** (`last_error` joined into `mailboxAlert`) rather than sending the reader to another page to find out why — during an outage "why" is the whole question. Verified live: both crons flag their mailboxes, and the dashboard renders "Mailbox sweep failing (moose@…) — New email is no longer reaching the CRM — Google Workspace is not configured."
- **Ber AI could not see leads.** On 08-24 the headline finding was "the AI could not see most of the app" and 14 tools were added to fix it — then this build shipped a whole surface with none, so "what bids are due?" would answer from nothing. Three read-only tools added to `agent-tools-modules.ts`: **`list_leads`** (route/status/recommendation/`due_within_days` filters; marketing rejects excluded unless explicitly asked for), **`query_lead`** (full extraction + requirements + attachments + the whole fit assessment incl. the questions to answer before bidding), **`get_leads_summary`** (awaiting decision, split by destination, pursue/consider counts, overdue and closing bids, how many were filtered as marketing). The agent prompt gained a routing line and a paragraph stating the thing the model must not get wrong: **a lead is NOT pipeline** — nobody owns it, promotion is what makes it real, and it must never be counted in pipeline value or presented as work we are doing. All three exercised live against real rows.
- **Nothing pushed a hot lead at anyone.** The queue only helps someone who opens it; a `pursue` lead with a ten-day bid date sitting unread is the miss the module exists to prevent. New `notify-leads.ts` + a `notify` phase on the lead sweep: one **digest** (never one mail per lead — a Monday burst of eight should read in thirty seconds), cards ordered by bid date with urgency read off that date alone, the fit line, and the first concern. Migration `20260825000002_lead_notifications.sql` (**APPLIED**) adds `leads.notified_at` + a partial index, so each lead is announced **once** — re-sending every morning until someone acts is how a notification becomes noise, and noise is how the next real bid gets missed. A failed send leaves `notified_at` null so the next run retries rather than losing them. **Inert until `LEADS_NOTIFY_EMAIL` names a recipient** — who gets told is a business decision and mailing a guessed address is worse than not mailing. All three paths verified (skips cleanly unset; retries on send failure; renders correctly).
- **`.env.local` and the OAuth tokens were unbacked-up single-copy secrets** — the nightly job covered Postgres and the storage volume and nothing else, and the crash had just demonstrated the loss mode on the one file holding the OAuth client secret and every refresh token. `~/supabase-selfhost/backup.sh` (backup `.bak-presecrets-20260825`) now builds a **secrets bundle** alongside the dump: `.env.local` + the tokens file, tarred under `umask 077`, `age`-encrypted to the same offsite recipient, pushed to the mini, and the **plaintext deleted immediately whatever happens** — unlike the db/storage artifacts it is never kept locally in the clear. Only files that exist are bundled (tar exits nonzero on a missing member, and a missing tokens file — the normal state between a loss and a re-consent — must not stop `.env.local` being backed up). Dry-run verified: 1.7KB tar at mode 600, encrypts, plaintext gone; undecryptable on the Studio, as designed.
- **Promoting a lead now keeps the relationship.** Bid invitations come from the same handful of GCs repeatedly, but promotion recorded the firm as a client *string* while the estimator who sent it existed nowhere searchable. `promoteLead` now find-or-creates **two** directory contacts — the organisation and the person (with their email and phone) — tagged `inbound-lead`, and on a project links both as players with prose roles ("Inviting Contractor", "Bid Contact") matching the free-text voice already in `project_players`, not a slug vocabulary the directory doesn't use. Wholly non-fatal: a directory miss never fails a promotion that already created records. Verified end to end, then all test rows removed (0 residue across projects/parties/players/leads/threads).
- **Also fixed:** `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs` requested neither the new `drive.readonly` scope nor the info@ mailbox — re-consenting with it would have minted tokens that 403 on the first Drive call and left the lead sweep with no credential at all. It now mirrors `SCOPES` and unions `GOOGLE_LEAD_MAILBOXES`, with a comment saying the two lists must stay in step.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + full `next build` clean; eslint on every touched file clean (the 2 survivors are the documented pre-existing `react-hooks/purity` `Date.now()` misfires, unchanged in count); deployed and smoke-tested. **Still blocked on credentials** — see the lead-list entry above; the OAuth re-consent gates mail, and `LEADS_NOTIFY_EMAIL` / `GOOGLE_DRIVE_KNOWLEDGE_FOLDER_ID` / `DINO_LEAD_EMAIL` are documented in `.env.local` and §7 but deliberately unset.

**Done 2026-08-26 (Workspace overhaul shipped — the CRM writes back into Gmail, Drive, and Chat; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **The problem the whole pass exists to solve: Ber Intelligence is tailnet-only and most of the team cannot reach it.** Everything below pushes information OUT to where people already are, while the platform stays the system of record. Nothing is read back in — one-way by design, so Drive and Gmail never become a second source of truth.
- **Scopes are now TIERED, and the tier is decided in exactly one place.** New `scopesFor(mailbox)` in `google-workspace.ts`: `SCOPES` (all mailboxes) + `PRIMARY_ONLY_SCOPES` (moose@ — Docs write, `drive.file`) + new `LEAD_ONLY_SCOPES` (info@ — `gmail.modify`). `gmail.modify` is the broadest grant in the file (read/write/label/trash), so it is deliberately withheld from the deal mailboxes, which stay read-only. `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs` **parses all three arrays out of the app source** and `probeScopeCoverage()` holds each mailbox to its OWN tier — previously it checked every mailbox against one flat list, which would have reported moose@'s Docs write as a gap on info@. **`contacts` is now READ+WRITE**, superseding `contacts.readonly`.
- **Leads → Gmail labels** (`lib/leads/gmail-sync.ts`, `lib/integrations/gmail-write.ts`). The triage verdict is written onto the thread as a `Ber AI/…` label, so an estimator scanning info@ sees which invitations are worth bidding **without a login, a VPN, or the app**. It also makes the marketing filter auditable: 79 of 107 threads were rejected as marketing, and a rejection you can only inspect inside the platform is one nobody checks. Exactly one platform label per thread — the previous one is recorded on the lead and removed when the verdict moves, and **only the label this platform applied is ever removed**, so anything a human filed the thread under is theirs and stays. `refreshLeadLabel` fires on promote/forward/status-change so a decision reaches the mailbox in seconds rather than at tomorrow's cron.
- **Leads → Gmail drafts** (`lib/leads/draft-reply.ts`). A `pursue` invitation needs an answer within a day; that reply was being written late or not at all while the local model sat idle 94% of the time. The platform now writes it and **stops** — it creates a DRAFT and never sends. That is the entire safety model: the §11 "mail never becomes a record/action without human review" invariant is satisfied *structurally*, because an unsent draft does nothing on its own. `pursue`-only (a `consider` lead is one a human still has to think about, and a drafted reply quietly lobbies for it), and `gmail_draft_id` is a one-per-lead-ever latch — a draft a human deleted is not re-created, because deleting it WAS the decision.
- **Records → Drive** (`lib/drive/publish.ts`, `api/drive/publish`). A project's RFP, drawings, and addenda are unreachable for most of the company most of the time; publishing copies them into a Drive folder. Idempotent per document via `drive_published_id`, so re-publishing uploads only what is new. Folders are **created, not looked up by name** — two records may legitimately share a name and merging their bid packages is worse than an untidy folder list. Uses `drive.file`, which grants access to app-created files ONLY, so it is safe to hold alongside `drive.readonly`. Called quietly from `promoteLead` (a Drive hiccup must never read as "promotion failed") and wired as a button on **projects, opportunities, and steel deals** — admin-only, matching the API's own guard, because publishing is a sharing decision.
- **Google Chat as a second notify channel** (`lib/notify/chat.ts`). Chosen over Telegram — the seam's original target — because the space already exists and nobody has to install an app or create an account. Deliberately an INCOMING WEBHOOK, not a Chat app: an app would let people ask Ber AI from the space, but that needs Google to call INTO a tailnet-only platform with no public endpoint. Outbound works today; inbound is blocked by network shape, not effort. The lead digest is **hand-written in Chat's dialect** rather than converted from the email — a space is skimmed on a phone, so it is terser. Threaded per day so a digest reads as one conversation. `notified_at` is stamped once ANY channel delivers; retrying for a second channel would re-announce every lead to the one that already worked, and a duplicate digest is how a digest starts being ignored.
- **Migration `20260826000001_workspace_integrations.sql` — APPLIED.** Every column records what the platform has ALREADY written to something outside itself (a label applied, a folder created, a file published), which is precisely what makes each write idempotent. Without them a daily cron re-labels and re-uploads forever. `meetings.drive_file_id` carries a partial unique index so the (unbuilt) transcript importer is idempotent by construction.
- **BUG FOUND: the Drive button rendered nowhere.** `DocumentsTab` had been given `driveFolderUrl` / `canPublish` props but `projects/[id]/documents/page.tsx` never passed them; both are optional and `canPublish` defaulted falsy, so the entire feature was invisible in the UI. Fixed, and the same wiring added to `OpportunityDocuments` and `SteelDealFiles`. In `SteelDealFiles` the publish control is gated on `canPublish` **alone, not on `canEdit`** — a steel rep may attach files but may not share them out of the tailnet, so neither permission should imply the other.
- **Deliberately deferred (schema exists, no code):** Meet transcript import (`meetings.drive_file_id`) and directory → Workspace contacts sync (`parties.google_contacts` / `google_contacts_hash`). The `contacts` write scope is granted ahead of its code, accepted knowingly to avoid consenting three mailboxes twice.
- **THREE REAL BUGS CAUGHT IN VERIFICATION, all by testing ONE record before letting a sweep touch 107.**
  1. **`leads.thread_id` is NOT a Gmail thread id.** It is a FK to `email_threads.id` — a UUID of this platform's own making; Gmail's id lives in `email_threads.gmail_thread_id`. Labelling, drafting, and the UI deep link all passed the UUID straight to Google, which answers `400 Invalid id value`. Fixed so **no caller can get it wrong**: `applyLeadLabel` resolves the Gmail id itself via new `resolveGmailThreadId()` (a plain `LeadRow` read with `select('*')` has no join, and the API routes hold exactly that), while the sweep and the leads page use the `GMAIL_THREAD_EMBED` PostgREST embed to avoid N+1. **Rule: any read that will go on to touch Gmail must carry `email_threads(gmail_thread_id)` — `thread_id` is ours, not Google's.**
  2. **A `pursue` lead could never be drafted once there were more scored leads than the limit.** `draftLeadReplies` selected 20 leads ordered by bid date and *then* filtered to `pursue` in JS, so the limit bounded the wrong set — 28 scored leads already exceed it, and a pursue lead below the cut would be fetched away by leads that were never candidates. The verdict filter moved into the query.
  3. **Drafts were addressed to robots and to ourselves.** The recipient was "whoever spoke last", which for a plan-room notification is `noreply@bidnet.com`, and for a live thread is very often **info@berwilson.com — our own mailbox**. New `replyAddressFor()` walks backwards skipping both no-reply senders and every mailbox this platform reads, returning null when no external human ever spoke; such leads are counted `noReply` and skipped as a normal outcome rather than a failure (it also saves a wasted ~75s model call). 25 unit checks cover the address logic.
- **Verification limit worth knowing: there are currently ZERO `pursue` leads** (22 consider, 6 pass, 79 spam), so `draftLeadReplies` is a structural no-op and cannot be exercised by simply running the sweep. Drafting was verified by running it standalone against one `consider` lead with the filter temporarily widened, then deleting the draft and clearing the latch columns.
- **New env:** `GOOGLE_CHAT_WEBHOOK_URL` (+ `_<KEY>` per-space variants); `LEAD_GMAIL_LABELS` / `LEAD_DRAFT_REPLIES` / `LEAD_CALENDAR_SYNC` were already read by the code but documented nowhere — all three now in `.env.local` and §7. Unset = the feature is ON (labels/drafts/calendar) or inert (Chat), never broken.
- **Verified live against the Studio, not just typechecked:** scope coverage `ok` on all 3 mailboxes; a single lead labelled and read back **out of Gmail** (label on the thread, INBOX/UNREAD/CATEGORY_UPDATES untouched); the verdict transition Consider → Promoted → Consider leaving exactly one platform label each time; the full sweep **106 labelled / 0 failed in 32.6s, second run a complete no-op** (79 Filtered / 22 Consider / 6 Pass, matching the queue exactly); Drive publish of a real 3-document project, idempotent on re-run, files confirmed present in Drive at `My Drive / Ber Intelligence / Projects / West Wendover` with real byte counts; a draft created against a live thread — correctly threaded, `In-Reply-To` set, `DRAFT` label only (unsent), body citing the $20M bonding cap and the prefab angle from the company profile. **All test residue removed**: 3 test drafts deleted (Richard's 94 pre-existing drafts untouched), 0 draft latches left, verdicts restored to 22 consider / 6 pass with no stray `pursue`.
- **Not verified, needs an eyeball:** the Gmail deep link's `mail/u/?authuser=…#all/<id>` URL form (a browser-side behaviour this session cannot exercise), the publish button's appearance on the three document surfaces, and Google Chat (no webhook configured — digests go to email, which works).

**Done 2026-08-26 (Workspace as the delivery layer — contacts sync, nightly Drive publishing, brief into Chat; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Context: Richard asked how well the Gsuite tools are being leveraged, because employees are getting Workspace access who will NOT be put on the platform (Tailscale is painful), reserving Ber Intelligence for corporate/HQ work.** The audit that drove this pass found the real problem is not the split — it is that **5 of 7 active team members have no email address recorded and only 2 have logins.** The whole `steel_sales` role, Kanban board, and commission engine were built for Colton and Jason, who have never logged in and whose addresses are not on file; the weekly task digest can reach **zero people** (and only 1 of 18 open tasks has a due date at all). **The recommendation given: fix access for people who DO the work (the blocker is literally two email addresses), and use Workspace for people who only need to KNOW.** Rejected the "reps run their own CRM off a Drive lead drop" option — it forfeits the commission audit trail (you pay off numbers you cannot see), the $30/SF floor, and exec visibility, and it is not the zero-work option it sounds like since leads are not published to Drive at all. **The line drawn: Workspace carries information to people; the platform is where work is recorded. One-way, always.**
- **Directory → Google Contacts** (`src/lib/contacts/workspace-sync.ts`, `src/lib/integrations/google-contacts-write.ts`). A CRM contact now autocompletes in Gmail for someone who never opens the platform. The `contacts` write scope was already granted on all three mailboxes and the `parties.google_contacts` / `google_contacts_hash` columns already existed — **zero lines of code had ever been written against them**, so this needed no migration and no re-consent. Every synced contact is filed in a **"Ber Intelligence" contact group** in each mailbox, which is what stops dozens of rows landing indistinguishably among someone's own contacts and makes the whole push reversible in one action. Change detection hashes the assembled Person (not the row), so a column change that does not alter the contact costs nothing. **Retirement is handled, not just creation**: an archived party, or one that loses its last contactable field, is DELETED from the mailboxes — "the CRM put something in my Gmail and won't take it back" is how an integration gets switched off. Only the fields the CRM is actually authoritative for are overwritten (`OWNED_FIELDS`), so a birthday someone adds in Gmail survives.
- **BUG FOUND IN TESTING (would have been silent forever): a contact deleted by hand in Gmail was never restored.** The stored pointer and the stored hash both still looked clean, so the hash short-circuit meant the sync never made the call that would have noticed — it reported "unchanged" and that person stayed missing from the mailbox permanently. Fixed with `listGroupMembers()`: **one listing per mailbox per run** gives the authoritative member set, and any stored resourceName missing from it is confirmed with a direct read before being discarded (a contact merely dragged OUT of the group must not be duplicated). A failed listing returns null and every pointer is assumed good — treating "could not look" as "gone" would recreate the entire directory on one transient error. New `restored` counter. **Rule: a content hash cannot detect a change made on the far side of the API; something must observe the remote state.**
- **Nightly Drive publish reconcile** (`src/lib/drive/reconcile.ts`). Publishing shipped in the 08-26 Workspace pass as a button, the button worked, and **one project out of fifteen had ever been pressed** — so the Drive folder the team was told to read was effectively empty and taught them to stop looking. A reading surface that depends on someone remembering to populate it is not a reading surface. The reconcile is driven by the DOCUMENTS rather than the records (a record with nothing attached needs no folder, and empty directories make the useful ones harder to find). Chosen over hooking the three upload routes: those have three different shapes, upload is not the only way a document arrives, and "in Drive by morning" is fine for a reading surface. The button stays for "I need it now".
- **Domain sharing is now self-healing.** `shareWithDomain` only ever ran at root *creation*, so if that permission were removed, publishing would carry on reporting success while the documents became invisible to everyone but moose@ — the worst shape of failure this integration can have. New `ensureDomainShared()` verifies it every run and re-adds it if absent (`wasShared` in the result). Verified by deleting the permission and watching the reconcile restore it.
- **The morning brief now posts to Google Chat** (`src/lib/notify/broadcast-brief.ts`). The daily-brief cron's own docstring said it stored the brief "for viewing at `/briefs`" — **a page that does not exist**. It has been generating a genuinely good brief every morning and writing it to a table with no reader. New `markdownToChatText()` (Chat is NOT markdown: `**bold**` and `##` render literally; its dialect is `*bold*`, `_italic_`, `<url|label>`) — kept separate from `htmlToChatText` because the input is a different language. 3800-char cap with an explicit trim marker, one thread per day. Best-effort and strictly after storage, so a Chat outage costs the post and nothing else. **`resolveChatWebhook` now falls back to the default space** when a keyed space is unset, so a caller can name its intended destination (`brief`, `leads`) on day one and the dedicated space can be created later with no code change; without the fallback, naming a key silently meant "deliver nowhere".
- **Three new health checks** — Drive Document Publishing, Contacts Sync, Google Chat — all reporting **coverage rather than pass/fail**, because "not everything is in Google" is the correct steady state (a party with no address and no phone is deliberately skipped). The Drive one is the check the publishing feature needed and did not have: nothing anywhere reported that 1 of 15 projects had been published. Chat is a config read, not a live test — probing an incoming webhook means posting to the space, and a check that spams the room on every page load is worse than no check.
- **Two new launchd crons, installed + loaded live:** `com.berwilson.cron-contacts-sync` (2:45am — the quiet block, and nobody's autocomplete changes mid-compose) and `com.berwilson.cron-drive-publish` (3:45am, AFTER the 3:15 knowledge sync so the two Drive jobs never share the credential). Both added to the middleware public allowlist (self-guarding via `CRON_SECRET`) and to `deploy-to-studio.sh`'s service loop.
- **Verified live against the real Workspace, not just typechecked:** 10-case unit check of the markdown converter; full People API round-trip (create → group → update-with-etag → delete, no residue); **22 contacts created across all 3 mailboxes** in 3m40s, re-run idempotent in 0.2s; full lifecycle on a throwaway party (create → edit updates in place with no duplicate → archive retires it and clears the pointers → row deleted, group back to 22); hand-deletion detected and restored with a fresh resourceName; **30 documents across 8 record folders published in 52s, 0 failures**, re-run a clean no-op, contents confirmed by listing Drive; root confirmed shared `domain: berwilson.com, role: reader`; domain permission deleted by hand and restored by the reconcile; today's real brief rendered to Chat dialect with zero markdown leftovers. `tsc` + eslint (touched files clean) + full `next build` clean; deployed and smoke-tested (both crons 401 unauthed, 200 authed, all 11 launchd services loaded).
- **⚠ Richard's manual steps:** (1) **`GOOGLE_CHAT_WEBHOOK_URL` is still unset** — Chat → the space → Apps & integrations → Webhooks → Add webhook, then put the URL in `.env.local` and restart. Until then the brief posts nowhere and lead digests stay email-only. Treat the URL as a secret (it carries its own auth token). (2) **Get Colton's and Jason's email addresses** → `/settings/users` → Grant access; the route needs an address only as the account identifier and **sends no email** (the self-hosted stack can't). (3) `DINO_LEAD_EMAIL` is still unset, so the Dino forward button 400s. (4) Put due dates on some tasks or the Monday digest keeps finding nobody.
- **To undo the contacts push entirely:** delete the "Ber Intelligence" contact group's members in each mailbox, then `update parties set google_contacts = '{}'::jsonb, google_contacts_hash = null`.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** Google Sheets as a steel rep surface (recommended against — it is a second UI for a module that already has a good one; the fallback design if access genuinely fails is a writable Sheet split by FIELD OWNERSHIP — rep-owned columns flow in, platform-owned money columns locked with protected ranges — so no field has two writers and there is nothing to reconcile); Meet transcript import (`meetings.drive_file_id` still schema-only, and Whisper already does this locally); Google Groups for notification addressing (`LEADS_NOTIFY_EMAIL` is still a hardcoded address list); Forms for field intake.

**Done 2026-08-31 (business-card scanner — photo → researched contact; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard's ask: photograph a business card from his phone, have the AI research the company on it, and create a contact with a summary of what they do and where they might fit Ber Wilson — purging the photo afterwards.** Shipped as **Scan Card** on the Directory toolbar (`/contacts`, contacts tab only). The input carries `capture="environment"`, so on a phone the button opens the camera directly; desktop browsers ignore it and show a file picker.
- **OCR runs ON-DEVICE through Apple's Vision framework** — `scripts/ocr/card-ocr.swift`, a ~60-line binary built by `zsh scripts/build-ocr.sh` into `~/.local/bin/bw-ocr` (**outside the app dir**, like whisper-cli and the map archives, so a deploy rsync `--delete` can't remove it). This is the same spawn-a-system-binary pattern as `afconvert`/whisper.cpp. **Deliberately NOT a vision LLM:** there is no VL model loaded in LM Studio (Richard's 08-25 call — one is 36GB of contention), and sending a photograph of someone's card to a cloud model would have been the one place this feature leaked real data. Vision ships with macOS: no model download, no resident RAM, **307ms** per card.
  - It reads the image through **ImageIO, not NSImage**, so the EXIF orientation tag is honoured — a photo taken with the phone held sideways is otherwise handed to Vision rotated, and rotated text does not recognize. Verified against a 90°-rotated card.
- **The photo is never stored anywhere.** It is posted as multipart, written to a temp file only for the length of the recognizer run, and deleted in `finally`. It never touches Supabase storage. Only the recognized *text* is kept (on the party's `enrichment_notes.raw_text`, so a bad parse can be checked against what was actually read).
- **Pipeline (`src/lib/contacts/card-intake.ts`):** local model parses the recognized lines into fields → **grounded web research on the COMPANY only** → local model writes the company summary, the fit read, and directory tags against the real `getCompanyContext()`. The person is not searched — that is Enrich Profile's job. Only the company name and website leave the machine, exactly as the existing Enrich Profile buttons already do. Research failure is **non-fatal**: the draft is still built from the card alone and the review screen says so in amber.
- **Nothing is created without confirmation** (§11). `POST /api/contacts/scan-card` returns a draft and creates nothing; `POST /api/contacts/scan-card/confirm` creates the contact from the reviewer's *edited* draft. The review dialog is fully editable (7 fields + summary + fit + removable tags) and shows the sources and the raw recognized text behind disclosures. Confirm also **find-or-creates the company in the vendor directory** and links it (`party_entities`), mirroring the manual Add Contact flow so both doors produce the same shape, and **embeds the contact for RAG** (`embedPartyEnrichment`) so Ber AI can answer "who did I meet who does structural engineering?". A card with only a company on it is a real card — it falls back to an organization record rather than rejecting the scan. Both routes are **admin-only by default-deny** (`/api/contacts` is in no `permissions.ts` allowlist).
- **Quality is genuinely good, checked against a REAL firm, not just a fixture.** A fabricated card correctly produced "research is thin, scope unverified" rather than a hallucinated business — the honesty instruction holds. A real one (Okland Construction) returned real facts (≈1,400 employees, $1.76B 2024 revenue, CM-at-Risk/Design-Build/IPD, self-performs concrete, Mountain West + Hawaii) and a **sharp** fit read: too large to meet prime-to-prime given the **$20M bonding cap**, but a real opening as a **prefab steel subcontractor** on their institutional scopes. That is the judgement the feature exists to produce.
- **Two prompt corrections made from watching the first run:** ALL-CAPS card names are normalized to Title Case (genuine acronyms left alone), and the model is told to tag what they DO — the first run emitted `unverified-firm`, which is a confidence statement, not a directory tag.
- **A transient Gemini 503 ("high demand") cost one of the two research queries on a live run**, so `researchWithRetry` retries once after 2.5s on 429/503/500/timeout. The whole value of the feature is the research; losing it to a momentary spike is not worth showing anyone.
- **`/settings/health` gained a Business Card Scanner card.** The recognizer is a build artifact living outside the repo, so a fresh machine or a wiped `~/.local` removes the feature silently — the button stays visible and fails only when someone actually photographs a card. That is the exact silent-outage class this codebase keeps rediscovering, so it is checked. New optional env `CARD_OCR_BIN`.
- **Verified live end-to-end through HTTP with a real minted session, not just typechecked:** OCR on a plain and a rotated card; the full pipeline twice (fabricated + real firm); `/contacts` 200 rendering the button and `/settings/health` 200 showing "Card recognizer ready"; a real multipart POST of a card photo → 200 draft in 91s → confirm → contact created → detail page rendering the fit notes; **1 RAG chunk** confirmed. **All test data removed — 0 residue** (party, chunks, party_entities, and the auto-created entity). `tsc` + full `next build` + eslint clean on every touched file; deployed (app 200, both routes 401 unauthed).
- **Worth knowing:** a scan takes **~80-95s** — OCR is instant, the two local-model calls and the grounded search are the cost. The review dialog states the wait. **Deliberately not built:** multi-card batch upload, a back-of-card pass, and auto-running Enrich Profile on the new contact (it is one click away on the detail page, and doubling the wait to ~3 minutes at the moment of meeting someone is the wrong trade).

**Done 2026-08-28 (audit pass — two silently-dead surfaces, one indefinite hang, and stuck documents; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Richard asked for an audit across the use cases.** Build gauntlet was already clean (tsc, eslint at its documented 21-problem baseline, build) and all 53 authenticated routes render, so everything below was found by exercising the app against live data rather than by reading it. Referential integrity came back clean on every FK checked (chunks→documents/updates/projects, tasks→assignee/project, leads→thread): 0 orphans.
- **THE DAILY BRIEF HAD BEEN DEAD FOR TWO DAYS AND NOTHING SAID SO.** No brief existed for 08-27 or 08-28; the cron log's last entry was 08-26 and its error log held one line — `curl: (28) Operation timed out after 988742 milliseconds`. Root cause: **`src/lib/ai/local.ts` had no timeout on any of its three fetch calls**, so a wedged LM Studio (or a socket stalled across one of the Studio's `Maintenance Sleep` windows — `pmset` shows it DarkWake/sleep cycling all day even with `sleep 0`) hangs the caller forever. Not hypothetical: one `email_threads` row is still marked `failed` with LM Studio's own `Engine protocol predict stream timed out after 600000ms`.
  - Added hang guards: whole-call cap on `localChat` and on the streaming connection (`LOCAL_AI_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 15 min), an **idle** cap between stream chunks (`LOCAL_AI_STREAM_IDLE_TIMEOUT_MS`, default 3 min — a long answer is legitimate, a silent socket is not, and only the gap tells them apart), and 2 min on embeddings. The reader is cancelled on failure so the socket isn't leaked, and `localStallError` turns the bare "This operation was aborted" into a message naming the host and the fix. **These are hang guards, NOT a token budget** — generation stays unbudgeted per Richard's 07-11 call; nothing legitimate comes close (slowest observed is a ~95s brief).
  - Verified by generating the missing brief: **55.5s, posted to Chat, 08-28 filled**.
  - **Worth knowing:** `/settings/health` WOULD have caught this (its brief-freshness check fails >36h) — but health is pull-only, and a brief that fails posts nothing to Chat, so a missing brief is indistinguishable from a quiet morning. A push on brief failure is the obvious follow-up; not built.
- **`search_email_threads` errored on EVERY call** — one of the 14 tools added 08-24 specifically so the AI could see the whole app, and the agent prompt tells the model to search correspondence before concluding no record exists. It filtered `summary::text.ilike` inside an `.or()`, and **PostgREST's logic-tree parser rejects `::` casts** (`failed to parse logic tree`; it accepts `->>`/`->` only). Rewritten as an OR chain over `subject` plus the jsonb fields via `->>` (deal_name, counterparty, summary, key_facts — `->>` serialises the array so an ilike still matches inside it), with the value **double-quoted** so a query containing a comma or paren can't be parsed as more logic-tree structure. Verified live: 15 hits for "steel", and "quantum" matches a thread whose subject is "Defense Tech Park Carolina" — i.e. the jsonb path really is being searched; `steel, framing (west)` now returns 0 cleanly instead of erroring.
- **Every contact detail page silently showed no "mentions".** `.filter('mentioned_parties::text','ilike',…)` — same cast, different failure: PostgREST **drops** the cast in a plain filter and hands raw jsonb to ilike, so the query died with `operator does not exist: jsonb ~~* unknown`. A failed query returns null, which is indistinguishable from "no matches", so the section rendered empty everywhere and the page still returned 200 (which is why the route sweep missed it — the same class as the 07-13 `milestones.title` bug). `mentioned_parties` is a jsonb array of `{name, role, company}` and `updates` holds **38 rows**, so it now fetches candidates and matches in JS, preserving the fuzzy intent with no migration. **A second latent bug in the same block:** the exclusion built `not.in.(null)` when the contact had no linked projects, which errors on a uuid column — so that path was broken twice over; the filter is now only applied when there are ids. Verified: "Eric" → 8 updates, "Thumbtack" → 3 (matched on company), "Danny Wang" → 1.
- **Report snapshots dangled at "Indexing…" forever.** `saveReportDocument` never set `embedding_status`, so every email/meeting report `.md` sat at the `pending` default while nothing was ever coming back to index it (the content is indexed via the update row / `embedOpportunityReport` by design). Now written as `'skipped'` — scoped to the `documents` inserts, because `opportunity_documents` has no such column (the compiler caught that).
- **Data repaired: 5 documents were not indexed, now 0.** Two `.docx` uploaded 08-24 (the Mutual NDA template and the DUBHES Helper Utah filing) had **no extracted text and no chunks at all** — invisible to Ber AI — one stranded at `processing`, one at `pending`, both stalled leftovers of an app restart mid-pass rather than a code fault (the pipeline itself re-ran them cleanly: 7 and 3 chunks with real summaries). Two `.md` report snapshots settled to `skipped`. Documents are now **47 complete / 3 skipped, zero pending/processing/error**.
- **All 37 read tools exercised against live data — 0 failures** after the fix (plus `draft_status_report`, 50s, real output). Useful confirmations along the way: the tool layer is honest about ambiguity (`query_investor` asks for an id when several match) and about semantics (`get_steel_payouts` states commissions are payable only once collected; `list_leads` states a lead is not pipeline).
- **Verified:** tsc + full `next build` clean; eslint clean on all four touched files (baseline unchanged at 21 problems — 12 `set-state-in-effect`, 6 `no-img-element`, 2 `purity`, 1 `prefer-const`, all pre-existing and documented). Deployed to the Studio and smoke-tested.
- **Not fixed, deliberately:** the 12 `set-state-in-effect` lint errors (a per-component refactor, unchanged since 07-05); the 381-thread summarize backlog and 104 pending intake sessions (both draining normally, not faults); and `git status` showed the repo clean with **no `* 2.*` Finder duplicates** for once.

**Done 2026-08-27 (Google Meet transcripts import themselves into the review queue; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **Richard's ask: executives record their meetings in Meet, get a transcript and summary, and use them to update projects and opportunities — while the steel team records outside the platform entirely.** His picks (AskUserQuestion): **auto-poll each executive's Drive** (over pasting a link); **match the calendar event, let AI suggest the record, human confirms** (over auto-routing); and for steel, **build nothing at all** — they use Meet and Drive, the platform never looks.
- **The feature is a feeder, not a pipeline.** `analyzeMeetingNotes` already did every hard part — one model pass for the recap, `findMatchingProjects` + a name ilike to pre-match the projects/opportunities the meeting touched, `matchExtractedParties` for attendees, staging a `pending` `email_intake_sessions` row (`intake_kind='meeting'`) whose confirm step fans out onto many records at once with tasks and attendees. So the import is ~250 lines that fetch a transcript and hand it to that. Nothing new was built where something existed.
- **Nothing is created automatically.** §11's invariant is the reason: the platform may read, summarize and propose, but a recording becomes a record only when a person says so. Imported sessions land in the same `/intake` Meeting tab as a pasted transcript and wait.
- **New `src/lib/meetings/meet-import.ts`.** Reads only `MAILBOXES` (moose@ + tuaone@ — the executives). Per mailbox: resolve the Meet Recordings folder → list new transcripts → export the Doc to text (`fetchDriveFile` already did this for the knowledge sync) → find the calendar event → stage. First run reaches back **14 days**, not all history: a Drive with two years of calls would stage hundreds of sessions into a queue nobody could face, and the value here is tomorrow's meetings. 12 per run (each is one ~40s local-model pass).
- **`listMeetTranscripts` is scoped to the Meet Recordings folder**, deliberately, rather than searching the whole Drive for docs named "…Transcript" — an executive's own file called "Interview Transcript" is not a meeting recording, and importing it would put a stranger's words in front of the reviewer. `GOOGLE_MEET_FOLDER_ID` overrides the folder for a Workspace that files Meet output elsewhere.
- **Calendar match is on title within a ±1-day window**, not on the Meet link: Drive exposes no conference id on the transcript file, so title-plus-time is the strongest join available. A miss is harmless — the AI still reads the transcript, it just doesn't get the invitee list handed to it up front.
- **Idempotency is enforced by the database, not by the importer remembering.** Migration `20260827000001_meet_transcript_import.sql` (**APPLIED**, PostgREST reloaded) adds `email_intake_sessions.drive_file_id` + a partial unique index, and `analyzeMeetingNotes` gained a `driveFileId` param so it is written **in the same INSERT** — a second run racing the first loses at the index, not halfway through. The latch is here rather than on the existing unused `meetings.drive_file_id` because that column marks a durable record in the board/compliance register, and an internal Meet call is not a governance record; both can coexist.
- **Recordings without transcripts are COUNTED, never imported.** A .mp4 is hundreds of megabytes and Whisper already covers uploads. Reporting the number is what stops "Meet transcription is switched off in the admin console" from looking identical to "nobody had any meetings" — the same silent-outage class as the 08-25 mail failure.
- **New `/api/cron/meet-import`** (middleware allowlist, `CRON_SECRET` fail-closed) + **`com.berwilson.cron-meet-import`** — **every 2 hours 8am–6pm at :20**, offset from the hourly email sweep so the two don't collide on the local model, and silent overnight because Meet writes nothing at 3am. A meeting ending at 2pm is staged by 2:20; nightly would be useless for a summary somebody wants the same day. **Installed + loaded live**; added to `deploy-to-studio.sh`'s service loop. Unlike the Drive knowledge sync, an unconfigured Google is a **503 here, not a quiet skip** — if credentials vanish, meetings stop arriving and "no new meetings" looks exactly like "fine".
- **`/settings/health` gained a Meet Transcript Import card** with `empty` as a first-class state distinct from `failed`, because until someone records with transcription on, Google never creates the folder and finding nothing is correct behaviour.
- **Verified with a real Drive round-trip, not just a typecheck:** 4/4 file-name→title cases and both calendar-match cases; a test folder + a genuine Google Doc transcript created in moose@'s Drive with a decoy doc beside it (**decoy correctly excluded**); the importer produced a session with the right label, both attendees, **3 tasks, 2 decisions, and the referenced project matched**; a second run was a clean no-op (`imported:0 alreadyImported:1`, still exactly 1 session); a real folder named "Meet Recordings" created to prove the by-name lookup, then removed. **All 4 Drive files and the session deleted — 0 residue.** `tsc` + eslint (touched files clean) + full `next build` clean; deployed; live endpoint 401 unauthed / 200 authed.
- **⚠ Not yet doing anything, and this is expected:** neither executive Drive has a **"Meet Recordings" folder** — Google creates it only after the first call recorded with transcription switched on. **Richard's manual step:** in a Meet call use Activities → Recording **and** Transcripts (and confirm both are enabled in Workspace admin console → Apps → Google Meet). After the first recorded call the importer picks it up within two hours; the health card says exactly this until then.
- **Deliberately NOT built:** anything for steel (his explicit call — they record in Meet and keep files in their own Drive, the platform never reads them); Whisper fallback for recordings that have no Meet transcript; and importing into the `meetings` compliance register (`meetings.drive_file_id` is still schema-only — that would be the path if board minutes ever want the same treatment).

**Done 2026-08-27 (mobile UX pass on the task board — from a responsive review at ~400px; DEPLOYED, no migration):**
- **Context: an outside responsive review of `/tasks` at ~406px.** Its structural verdict was right — no horizontal overflow anywhere, no console errors, the sidebar→bottom-tab collapse is sound. The findings were refinements, plus **two genuine CSS-specificity bugs the review couldn't have seen from outside**.
- **BUG 1 — every side sheet was 3/4 width on every screen, and callers' overrides were silently dead.** `data-[side=right]:w-3/4` is an *attribute selector* (specificity 0,2,0), so it out-specified every consumer's plain `w-full sm:max-w-xl` (0,1,0) — twMerge can't dedupe across a variant prefix, so `cn()` never resolved it. Task detail rendered at 300px of a 406px screen with a dead strip down the side; `LeadDetailSheet`'s `sm:max-w-xl` and `SteelEditDrawer`'s `sm:max-w-2xl` were **also being clamped to `max-w-sm` on desktop** for the same reason. Fix: the width/height defaults moved OUT of the `data-[side]` variants into plain classes chosen from the `side` prop in JS, where twMerge resolves the override the caller expects. Side sheets are now full-bleed below `sm`. **Rule: never put an overridable default behind a `data-[…]` variant — an attribute selector beats the utility a caller passes in.**
- **BUG 2 — `heading-tight` silently defeats `truncate`.** `text-wrap: balance` sets `text-wrap-mode: wrap`, the same longhand `white-space: nowrap` feeds, and wins on cascade order. The app-bar `<h1 truncate heading-tight>` was truncating in intent only and wrapped to two lines in the band between the mobile and sidebar layouts (~715–864px) — the review saw the symptom and attributed it to the search box. AppHeader now uses `tracking-[-0.02em]`; the utility keeps `balance` for the multi-line headings that want it, with a comment not to pair the two.
- **Completion was a 20×20 target with no way back.** Completing pulls the row out of the list, everything below jumps up, and a second tap lands on a different task — the reviewer did exactly that and had to go into Archive to repair it. The circle keeps its size; an **absolutely-positioned `-inset-3` overlay grows the tap area to 44×44 without moving the row** (padding on the button would shift the title). The status write was split out of the click handler into `setTaskStatus()` so the toast can carry **Undo** running the same path in reverse.
- **Touch targets, everywhere they were short:** header buttons and filter selects and the Open/Archive/Blocked controls `h-11 sm:h-8`; assignee chips `h-11 sm:h-9`; chat textarea + send `44px` below `sm`; Decide's kind chips `min-h-11 sm:min-h-0`; weekly-report toolbar controls `h-11 sm:h-9`. "Manage team"/"Weekly report" wrapped *inside* their pills at 390px — now `whitespace-nowrap` with short labels (Team / Report) below `sm`.
- **The list was below the fold on a phone.** Seven assignee chips wrapped to three rows and four record-filter selects stacked one per row — a full screen of chrome before the first task. Now: chips are **one sideways-scrolling row** below `sm`, and the four record filters collapse behind a **Filters button with an active-count badge** (`hidden sm:flex`, so desktop is unchanged). First task card moved from off-screen to **y=395** at 390×844.
- **Detail sheet:** meta grid is `grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2` so the due date stops clipping to "Aug 18, 2" (the four full-width fields had to become `sm:col-span-2` — `col-span-2` in a 1-col grid creates an implicit column). The three collapsed affordances are inline-blocks in a `space-y` stack, so they rendered flush as **"+ Add what+ Add why+ Add how"** — they now share one `gap-x-4` row.
- **Chat placeholder clipped its second line.** The auto-grow measures `scrollHeight`, and **a placeholder contributes nothing to scrollHeight**, so it could never see the overflow. Fixed by keeping the placeholder on one ellipsised line (`placeholder:whitespace-nowrap`), not by growing the box.
- **Weekly report:** toolbar is opaque (at `bg-white/95` the report ghosted through and read as a rendering overlap rather than a sticky bar) and fits **one 65px row at 390px** instead of three stacked rows (121px) — the "Scope" label and the long print verb shorten below `sm`.
- **The "Colton is a chip but not in the roster" report was a scroll artifact, not a data bug** — both lists render the identical `members` array; at `max-h-64` the 7th person sat below the fold with no affordance. The dialog now headers a count ("7 people") and scrolls to `min(60vh,24rem)`.
- **Verified against production at a real 386px CSS viewport** (same-origin iframe — Chrome can't size a window below ~640px, and the page was zoomed; an iframe evaluates media queries against its own viewport, which is a faithful harness): complete hit box 44×44 on all 18 rows with the circle still 20×20 and a tap 3px inside the corner landing on the button; chips 44px in a row that scrolls 891px in a 378px track; Filters hides/reveals the four selects and badges "1"; **full round-trip on a throwaway task — complete → `done` → toast Undo → `open` → back on the board → deleted** (no real data touched); sheet `width:386px left:0 right:0` with "Aug 18, 2026" unclipped and the three add-links 16px apart; both h1s `nowrap` at 860px; weekly bar 65px/one row at 390 and 57px with full labels at 1100. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint clean on every touched file (the 1 survivor is the documented pre-existing `set-state-in-effect` at TaskDetailSheet:147).
- **Not done:** the reviewer suggested the task sheet become a bottom sheet on phones — it's a full-bleed right sheet instead, which reaches the same "uses the whole screen" outcome with no animation/primitive change. The 12 `react-hooks/set-state-in-effect` errors are still a per-component refactor and were left alone.

**Done 2026-08-26 (bonding capacity recorded — the top-cited gap closed):**
- **Richard: "Keep bonding under 20 million for now."** Recorded as **$20M on BOTH `bonding_capacity` (single-project) and `aggregate_bonding`**. He gave one number without splitting the two; taking it as the ceiling for each is the conservative reading — it can only under-state capacity, never lead to committing to a bond that cannot be placed. Revise when a surety letter states real separate limits.
- **Made it actually bite.** A capacity number the assessor never tests against is decoration, so a disqualifier was appended: work requiring more than $20M in surety bonding (single or aggregate) is out of scope. Bonding was the **most-cited gap in the entire backlog — 55 mentions**, more than double certifications.
- **Written to BOTH sources, because only one of them binds.** Retrieval testing on 08-26 proved the Drive document outranks the profile for credential questions, so the database alone would have been ignored again. The document's bonding line and gap-table row now both state the $20M ceiling, and the file was re-synced (8 chunks re-embedded). Verified in the assembled AI context: `- **Bonding:** single project $20.0M / aggregate $20.0M` plus the disqualifier line.
- **Gotcha worth remembering:** `getCompanyContext()` emits the bonding line **unconditionally**, so before this it read `$0 / $0` rather than being absent — the assessor was being told capacity was zero, which is worse than silence and may explain some of the hedging. Any numeric profile field left null renders as a hard zero, not as "unknown".
- A re-score of all 28 leads is running against the complete profile. **Read the verdicts, not the scores** — the ±20 point variance measured earlier still applies.

**Done 2026-08-26 (knowledge document corrected in place; calendar deadlines live; score precision measured):**
- **Richard confirmed the DBE/MBE/WBE/SBE certifications are real.** The "needs certificate verification" caveat was a conservative placeholder written 2026-07-03 when no certificate had been sighted, and it had propagated into BOTH the database disqualifier AND the Drive knowledge document. Retrieval testing proved the document was the binding half: for a certification query its credential table was the **top hit at 0.642 similarity**, outranking the profile, so correcting the database alone changed nothing.
- **Measured, not assumed: fixing the database by itself did NOT help.** All 28 leads re-scored against corrected certifications: average **54.9 → 53.5 (−2.2)**, 18 down / 6 up / 3 unchanged, still zero `pursue`. Recording 9 credentials (incl. Utah GC #14235441-5501, GSA MAS, SAM.gov/CAGE/UEI) was correct and worth keeping, but certifications were never the constraint.
- **THE IMPORTANT FINDING — fit scores are precise to about ±20 points.** The same leads, same correspondence, same profile, re-scored: Sky Mountain Golf 40→65 (+25), Residences at Millcreek 60→40 (−20), RFQ 2027-06 62→42 (−20). Nine of 28 moved more than 10 points **in both directions on unchanged inputs**. That is local-model sampling variance, not signal. **Do not treat the numeric score as a ranking** — the pursue/consider/pass verdict and the concerns list held steady across runs while the numbers swung. Any future feature that sorts or thresholds on `fit_score` is building on noise.
- **The Google Doc was corrected in place via the Docs API** (7 replacements, one batchUpdate guarded by `requiredRevisionId` so a concurrent human edit could not be clobbered): four credential rows Flagged→Active, the gap-table row, the set-aside disqualifier, and a sharper bonding note. Verified afterwards: 0 remaining occurrences of "Flagged", "Needs certificate verification", "verified in hand", "certificates need verification".
- **The disqualifier was NARROWED, not deleted.** The document itself states the SBA 8(a) pathway is "in development — not yet active", so Buy Indian Act / IIP / tribal 8(a) set-asides genuinely remain out of scope; only the DBE/MBE/WBE/SBE half was wrong. It now reads as tribal/NHO-only and states explicitly that DBE/MBE/WBE/SBE set-asides ARE in scope.
- **New `PRIMARY_ONLY_SCOPES`.** Docs write can edit or delete *any* Google Doc in the account it is granted on — the first genuinely powerful permission here against a codebase of read-only scopes. Only the Drive-sync mailbox needs it, so the consent script requests it for `PRIMARY_MAILBOX` alone: **moose@ gets 8 scopes, tuaone@ and info@ stay at 7.** Re-consenting after removing the entry hands the permission back; that is the intended way to close the window.
- **Calendar deadlines are LIVE.** 10 events written to moose@ (bid dates, site visits, RFI cut-offs) with 3-day and 1-day reminders. **Bug found and fixed by running it:** Google event ids are base32hex — digits and letters **a-v only** — so the obvious `bw` prefix (w is outside the alphabet) produced "Invalid resource id value" on every insert. Now prefixed `lead`. Idempotency proven: run twice, still exactly 10 events, no duplicates.
- **Verified:** doc read back and confirmed correct; Drive re-sync updated the document in place (8 chunks re-embedded, stale caveat no longer retrieved); `tsc` + build clean; deployed.

**Done 2026-08-25 (leverage pass — idle model put to work, five queues become one, deadlines reach the calendar; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED):**
- **The measurement that drove all of it.** Over 7 days the local model ran **856 calls / 10.6 busy hours = 6.3% utilisation** — idle 94% of the time, during a *busy* week with a full mailbox backfill. Meanwhile **104 intake sessions sat pending, the oldest six weeks old**, plus 14 undecided leads and 35 review-queue rows. The free resource was idle and the scarce one — the executive's attention — was the bottleneck. Every change below moves work from the second to the first.
- **Pre-decide phase (`src/lib/email-ingestion/predecide.ts`, migration `20260825000003` APPLIED).** Attaches a recommended **disposition** to each pending session — create / merge / dismiss — with a one-sentence reason and a `headline`: the single fact that would change the reader's mind. `/intake` now sorts create-first, shows the merge target inline, and prints the headline under each row, so most sessions are decidable from the list. **Deliberately NOT a threshold on `fit_score`:** fit measures whether we should PURSUE, which is a poor proxy for whether something is a RECORD. Sampling the backlog found an approved **LOI scored 15** and a live **Utah County IDIQ pursuit scored 30** — a fit threshold would have discarded both. It asks a separate question and only auto-acts on dismissals above **0.85 confidence**; creating anything still requires a human (§11 untouched). Runs as the last sweep phase on leftover budget. Live results were sharp — it caught a merge into an existing West Wendover record and surfaced headlines like "250 ADU units at $105k–$115k, awaiting follow-up call".
- **One `/decide` surface** (`src/app/decide`, `DecideClient`). Inbound bids + staged correspondence + low-confidence extractions in a single ranked list, each row linking into its existing review UI — it replaces the *need to visit five screens*, not the screens themselves. **Ranking differs from the email on purpose:** here a hard deadline outranks quality (`weight()` = overdue 2000 / closing-within-7-days 1000 / pursue-or-create 100 / + score), because a bid can stop being available; the emailed digest is quality-first per Richard's instruction. Verified live: the two leads closing in 7 days lead the list at 72 and 55, ahead of a 74 closing in 15 days. **Deliberately excludes open tasks** — a task is work already decided on, and mixing it in turns a decision list back into a to-do list. Nav: Decide joins `primary` (carrying the review badge), Leads moves to `intelligence`, and the Review Queue row is **removed from nav** but stays routable.
- **Lead deadlines now reach Google Calendar** (`src/lib/leads/calendar.ts`, new `calendar.events` scope). Bid dates, site visits and RFI cut-offs existed **only inside a tailnet-only app most of the team cannot reach** — a mandatory pre-bid site visit was effectively invisible. Written as all-day events keyed `lead:<id>:<kind>` via a caller-supplied Google event id, so a daily sweep never duplicates and **a date that moves MOVES** (409 → PATCH) rather than leaving a stale deadline behind. Reminders at 3 days and 1 day. Only pursue/consider leads get events — calendaring every marketing blast would make the calendar the next thing nobody reads. **Requires one re-consent** (see below); until then it fails softly with a message naming the fix.
- **Emailed digest is now ranked best-first** (Richard's instruction): pursue before consider, then fit score, with the bid date breaking ties only. Sorted at both the query and inside `renderLeadEmail`, so no caller can produce a different order.
- **Nav prunes itself.** New `NavItem.hideWhenEmpty` — a module with no rows is hidden rather than shown as a dead end. Applied to **Dino (0 rows)**: it is mid-acquisition, so hiding beats deleting work about to be needed, and it **un-hides with no code change** the moment the first revenue row lands. (Correcting an earlier overstatement of mine: nav is **18 destinations, not 29** — that count had included `alsoMatches` paths.)
- **Two error messages that would have misdirected during an outage:** `explainTokenError` gained an `insufficientPermissions` branch (was dumping raw Google JSON; now names the re-consent command), and its `accessNotConfigured` branch no longer hard-codes "Gmail, Calendar and People" — it surfaces Google's own message, which names the exact API **and the project number that matters**. That mattered in practice: the Drive API was enabled on two freshly created projects before the right one, because Google checks the API against the project owning the **OAuth client**, not the project on screen.
- **A scope-drift bug I introduced, and the structural fix.** `calendar.events` was added to `SCOPES` in `google-workspace.ts` but not to the SECOND hand-maintained copy in `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs`, so Richard's re-consent minted tokens without it — the exact failure the comment above that list warned about, and the second time in one day (drive.readonly was the first). **The script no longer keeps its own list: it parses `SCOPES` out of the app source at run time**, exits loudly if it cannot, and drift is now impossible rather than merely discouraged. Duplicating a constant across a runtime and its setup tool guarantees this eventually; don't reintroduce it.
