- **New `probeScopeCoverage()` + a "Google Permissions" health card.** Adding a scope does nothing to tokens already minted — they keep working for everything they had and 403 on the new call, which surfaces as a raw permissions error at the worst moment. The probe refreshes each stored token (Google returns the granted scope list in the same response), diffs it against `SCOPES`, and names both the mailboxes and the missing scopes with the re-consent command. Verified live: it correctly flagged all three mailboxes as missing `calendar.events`.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + full `next build` clean throughout; pre-decide exercised on real sessions (6 → 5 create / 1 merge, ~22s each) then run across the backlog; `/decide` and `/intake` rendered authenticated with chips, headlines and deadline ordering; ranking re-checked against live rows; Dino confirmed absent from the rendered nav while Decide is present. **Still pending Richard: one re-consent for `calendar.events`** — `node scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs`.

**Done 2026-08-25 (Inbound Lead List — info@ triage → fit score → promote/forward; DEPLOYED + MIGRATED; CREDENTIALS PENDING):**
- **The structural gap: unqualified inbound had nowhere to live except as a `project`.** That is why 10 of 15 projects sit in pursuit/capture/bid carrying ~$50B of notional pipeline into the dashboard, map, and daily brief. A **lead** is the tier BEFORE stage one — it arrived, nobody owns it, and it expires with its bid date. Promotion (assigning an owner) is what turns it into a project, an opportunity, or a steel deal. `info@berwilson.com` receives the bid invitations, ITBs, and plan-room notices mixed with a majority of marketing, and **none of it reached the platform** — the sweep only read moose@ and tuaone@.
- **Two mail pipelines, deliberately kept apart.** `email_threads.pipeline` ('deal'|'lead', default 'deal') records which side a thread came in on, set at fetch time from the mailbox. Deal mail is clustered into multi-thread pursuits and staged as `email_intake_sessions`; lead mail is **one thread = one lead**, triaged and scored on its own and **never entering that queue** (104 pending sessions there already). `MAILBOXES` vs new **`LEAD_MAILBOXES`** (`GOOGLE_LEAD_MAILBOXES`, default info@) is the whole switch; `fetchMailbox`/`fetchAllMailboxes` take a `pipeline` option.
- **Marketing is dropped at the Gmail edge, where it costs nothing.** `leadExclusions()` appends `-category:promotions -category:social -label:bw-filtered` to the sweep query, so an excluded thread is never fetched, stored, or scored — and a human can retire a repeat marketing sender by writing one Gmail filter (`bw-filtered`) instead of shipping code. Override wholesale with `GMAIL_LEAD_EXCLUSIONS`. What survives Gmail is triaged by the model; **rejects are kept as `status='spam'` with a plain-language `spam_reason`, not deleted** — the "Show filtered" toggle over those rows is what makes the filter trustworthy.
- **Migration `20260825000001_leads.sql` — APPLIED to the Studio DB.** `leads` (43 columns: what arrived, what the model extracted, the fit assessment in the same shape `assessFit` returns, staged attachments, where it went) + `email_threads.pipeline` + `documents.drive_file_id`/`drive_modified_at`. `score_state` (pending|scored|failed|skipped) is separate from `status` (new|reviewing|promoted|forwarded|ignored|expired|spam) so a scoring failure is retryable without touching the human decision. **`sector` is deliberately plain text, not the `project_sector` enum** — the model's output is not trusted enough to be constrained at the column; promotion casts and validates. Unique index on `thread_id` (re-triaging updates in place rather than stacking duplicates). `update_updated_at` only — **not `log_activity()`**, which dereferences `new.project_id` (the trap already documented on opportunities and the sweep tables).
- **Own orchestrator, not another phase in `runSweep()`.** `runLeadSweep()` fetches, then scores, then triages, then expires. Two reasons the pipelines don't share a clock: a long deal backfill would starve lead triage indefinitely, and a fault in one must not stall the other. Scoring runs **before** triage (40% of remaining budget) so a long triage pass cannot leave yesterday's leads unscored forever. First sweep reads **90 days**, not all history — a two-year-old ITB is not a lead.
- **Fit scoring reuses `assessFit()`** — same Commercial/Operational/Financial/Compliance lenses and the same output the proposal wizard renders, so a lead and a proposal are judged against one picture. Attachments are pulled from the thread and staged before scoring.
- **Promotion and forwarding are the two exits.** `POST /api/leads/[id]/promote` creates a project, opportunity, or steel deal and stamps `promoted_*_id`. **Dino leads leave by email**: Dino's team has neither platform logins nor Tailscale access, so `POST /api/leads/[id]/forward` sends the brief with the original files attached (`DINO_LEAD_EMAIL`) and records `forwarded_to`/`forwarded_at` — chosen over a `dino_leads` table for exactly that reason. This required **attachment support in `sendMail()`** (new `MailAttachment`, multipart/mixed with a random boundary; capped at 15MB against Gmail's 25MB message limit), plumbed through `notify()`.
- **Nightly Drive → knowledge sync (`/api/cron/drive-sync`, 3:15am).** `assessFit` already asks for a "RELEVANT BER WILSON EVIDENCE" block retrieved from `is_company` chunks; without documents behind it every lead is scored against a paragraph of profile text. Pointing the sync at a Drive folder of capability statements, past performance, and credentials is what turns a generic score into a grounded one. Change detection is `(drive_file_id, drive_modified_at)` — an unchanged file costs one list entry and nothing else; an edited one is re-downloaded and re-indexed **with its old chunks removed first**, so a revision cannot leave both versions in the index contradicting each other. Adds the read-only `drive.readonly` scope. Skips cleanly (200, `skipped:true`) when `GOOGLE_DRIVE_KNOWLEDGE_FOLDER_ID` is unset.
- **Surfaces:** `/leads` (queue: route/status filters, bid-date ordering, fit badge, "Show filtered" over the spam rows) + `LeadDetailSheet` (full extraction, fit card, attachments, promote/forward/ignore). Nav item (Radar icon, primary group). Admin-only by default-deny — `/leads` and `/api/leads` are in no `permissions.ts` allowlist. **Dashboard + attention wiring:** leads with a bid date inside 14 days and a pursue/consider recommendation and no decision surface in **Needs Attention**, and `computeAttention` gained the matching category (so the agent's `get_attention_items` sees them too).
- **launchd:** `com.berwilson.cron-lead-sweep` (**daily 5:30am**, ahead of the workday — overnight invitations are triaged and scored before anyone opens the queue; deliberately off the hourly deal sweep's clock so the two never compete for the local model) and `com.berwilson.cron-drive-sync` (3:15am). Both **installed + loaded on the Studio**; both added to the middleware public allowlist (self-guarding via `CRON_SECRET`) and to `deploy-to-studio.sh`'s service loop.
- **Verified:** `tsc` + full `next build` clean (all 7 lead routes compile); migration confirmed applied column-by-column (43 columns, 5 indexes, RLS policy, trigger); **rolled-back live psql round-trip** — insert against a real thread, the exact dashboard query returns it, the unique-per-thread index rejects a second lead on the same thread, 0 rows after rollback; every `LeadRow` field exists in the DB; deployed (app 200, `/leads` 307 unauthed, `/api/leads` 401, both crons 401 unauthed and degrading correctly when unconfigured).
- **⚠ NOT YET RUNNING — three credentials/config items, all needing Richard:**
  1. **The Google OAuth tokens were lost in the 2026-08-25 crash** (see the entry below). Until the mailboxes are re-consented, the lead sweep returns 503 and reads nothing. **`gmail.readonly` on info@ has never been granted** and `drive.readonly` is new for every mailbox, so this re-consent is required by this build regardless of the crash. `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs` was updated to cover both: it now requests `drive.readonly` and unions `GOOGLE_LEAD_MAILBOXES` into its mailbox list (**it previously covered only the two deal mailboxes — a scope or mailbox added to `google-workspace.ts` but not to that script mints tokens that 403 at the first call**).
  2. **`GOOGLE_DRIVE_KNOWLEDGE_FOLDER_ID`** — unset, so the Drive sync no-ops. Needs the id of the Drive folder holding capability statements / past performance / credentials.
  3. **`DINO_LEAD_EMAIL`** — unset, so forwarding a plumbing/HVAC lead 400s until an address is set.
- **Deliberately deferred:** Ber AI agent tools over `leads` (the queue is a human surface first); per-route notification on a high-scoring lead; a lead → contact link in the directory.
- **LIVE 2026-08-25 (evening).** All three mailboxes consented (info@ new; moose@ re-consented for `drive.readonly`, which only IT needs since `PRIMARY_MAILBOX` is what the Drive sync impersonates). **info@ holds 411 threads, 390 new** after the Gmail-side marketing exclusions — 21 were already captured from the other mailboxes, so the cross-mailbox dedupe works on the lead side too. First real triage results: **26 threads → 7 real leads, 19 filtered as marketing** with plain-language reasons (Zillow/Thumbtack/real-estate alerts, an IT penetration-testing bid, a hearing-officer solicitation — all correctly rejected and kept for audit). Scoring is discriminating rather than uniform: a Utah industrial flex building scored **consider 72**, a landscape package on a sewage lift station **consider 55**, and an out-of-state waterline replacement at Detroit Metro Airport **pass 40**. At ~40s/thread the 390-thread backlog drains over roughly five daily runs; steady state is a few threads a day.
- **Ordering bug found and fixed on the first live run:** triage consumed the entire remaining budget, so a lead read this morning could not be scored — and therefore could not be announced — until the NEXT day's run. For a bid invitation with a deadline that is most of the value gone. The orchestrator now holds back `TAIL_SHARE` (25%) of the budget for a **second scoring pass after triage**, so leads reach the queue scored and notifiable in the same run; the two passes' counters are merged, with the tail's `remaining`/`outOfTime` winning since it ran last. Verified live (3 scored in a run whose triage produced the leads).
- **CONFIGURED + LIVE 2026-08-25 (night).** `LEADS_NOTIFY_EMAIL=moose@,tuaone@` (comma-separated — `sendMail` puts the list straight in the `To:` header), `GOOGLE_DRIVE_KNOWLEDGE_FOLDER_ID` set to the "Knowledge Library for Ber Intelligence" folder, and **`APP_URL` set** — it was never set on this box, so until now the digest rendered **no link at all**. Digests verified delivered to both mailboxes and read back out of Gmail: subject "4 new leads", four **per-lead deep links** plus the queue link, all absolute tailnet URLs. `/leads` gained **`?lead=<id>`** support (mirroring `/tasks?task=`) so a link opens that lead's detail sheet on mount instead of dumping the reader on the queue to find it again. Drive sync runs clean (`seen:0` — the folder is real, listable by moose@, and simply empty so far).
- **"Drive isn't working" was indistinguishable from "the folder is empty", so both are now stated out loud.** A clean sync reports `seen: 0`, which reads exactly like a failure. Two additions: **`scripts/check-drive.mjs`** (standalone — reads the token file and calls Google directly, so it diagnoses the credential path whether or not the app is running; four checks ordered so the first failure is the thing to fix: scope → API-enabled-on-the-credential's-project → folder reachable by the impersonated mailbox → contents, each with its exact remedy), and a **Drive Knowledge Sync card on `/settings/health`** with its own `empty` state ("Connected, but the folder is empty") distinct from `failed`. `probeDriveKnowledge()` translates the three failure modes — API not enabled, missing scope, folder owned by another account — into the specific fix rather than a raw Google error.
- **The Drive API had to be enabled on the project that owns the OAUTH CLIENT (247991610437), not whichever project you are looking at.** It was first enabled on two freshly created projects with no effect; Google checks the API against the credential's project. Worth remembering — the error names the project number, so read it.
- **`explainTokenError` named the wrong APIs.** Its `accessNotConfigured` branch hard-coded "Enable the Gmail, Calendar, and People APIs" — written before Drive existed, so the day the Drive sync first ran it pointed the reader at three APIs that were already enabled. It now surfaces Google's own message, which names the exact API and carries a direct enable link for the project.

**Incident 2026-08-25 (Studio froze and restarted mid-build; `~/berwilson-data` is gone — Google access DOWN, offline basemaps lost):**
- **What happened:** the Studio froze during the lead-list build and restarted (~10:24). Everything came back — Colima + all 8 Supabase containers healthy, launchd services loaded, the app serving 200, the database intact and backed up (nightly backup ran normally at 02:30 that morning, 6.5M db / 263M storage, offsite copy pushed). **`~/berwilson-data/` did not.** The whole directory is absent.
- **What lived there, and the blast radius:**
  - **`google-oauth-tokens.json`** — this file holds the OAuth **client id + client secret AND every mailbox's refresh token**. Losing it kills **all** Gmail, Calendar, Contacts, and Drive access at once, and it cannot be regenerated from anything on the box. Confirmed live: the last email-sweep run logged `{"error":"Google Workspace is not configured"}`. So the deal sweep, the lead sweep, `/calendar`, meeting prep, party enrichment, and the weekly task digest are all down until re-consent.
  - **`maps/us.pmtiles` (~17GB) + `maps/world.pmtiles` (186MB)** — the offline basemaps. `/map` will show its "basemap not installed" notice until they are re-extracted (runbook in `deploy/README.md`; go-pmtiles is still at `~/.local/bin/pmtiles` on the Studio). Nothing but the map is affected.
  - **`backups/purge-*.sql.gz`** — the pre-purge snapshots from the 08-24 data cleanup. Superseded by the nightly backups; no action.
- **Evidence, not inference:** the directory is absent from `$HOME`; free space rose from the ~205GB recorded on 08-24 to 224GB, consistent with ~17GB of map archives disappearing; a full-disk `find` located no copy of the tokens file elsewhere. **The cause was not determined.** `~/.Trash` is unreadable from a shell (TCC), so **check the Trash in Finder before re-extracting 17GB of maps** — if the directory is sitting there, restoring it recovers both the tokens and the basemaps instantly and no re-consent is needed.
- **Done in response:** `~/berwilson-data/{maps,backups}` recreated; `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs` corrected (it was missing `drive.readonly` and the info@ mailbox — see the lead-list entry); `GOOGLE_LEAD_MAILBOXES` added to `.env.local`. **Recovery is one command once the OAuth client JSON is re-downloaded** (Cloud console → APIs & Services → Credentials → the "Ber Intelligence" OAuth 2.0 Client ID → Download JSON — the copies in `~/Downloads` are for different, unrelated clients): `node scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs ~/Downloads/client_secret_*.json`, which walks all three mailboxes.
- **Lesson worth acting on: `~/berwilson-data` is unbacked-up single-copy state holding the platform's most load-bearing secret.** The nightly backup covers Postgres + storage and nothing else. The tokens file is ~1KB — it belongs in the nightly `age`-encrypted offsite push alongside the database dump.
- **RESOLVED same day — it was in the Trash.** Richard restored the folder intact: tokens file (mode 600, untouched) plus both map archives (18GB `us.pmtiles`, 178MB `world.pmtiles`). Nothing had to be re-extracted or re-consented for the deal side. Verified after restoring: both stored refresh tokens still mint access tokens, the deal sweep ran and picked up new mail (tuaone 460 → 476 threads) with both mailboxes back to `state=complete`, calendar reads returned a real event, and map tiles render at street level for Salt Lake City, Ogden, Nuku'alofa and Tirana. **The app MUST be restarted after the file returns** — `_oauthStore` is cached in module scope, so replacing the file under a running process changes nothing.
- **The lesson was acted on immediately:** the secrets bundle now runs nightly (see the gap-closing entry above) and was run by hand on restore — `secrets-20260825-1827.tgz.age` (2.6KB) is on the mini. **A repeat of this incident is now a decrypt, not a re-consent.**
- **`scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs` no longer needs the Cloud console.** The stored token file already carries the OAuth client id + secret, so a re-consent — adding a mailbox, or picking up a newly required scope — reuses it and goes straight to the browser. Only a genuine first run, or a lost token file, needs a downloaded client JSON (still accepted as an argument, which overrides the stored client). This removes the single hardest step from the recovery path.
- **Still open after the restore (both pre-date the crash, neither caused by it):** info@ has never been consented, so the lead sweep cannot read it; and the stored tokens were minted before `drive.readonly` existed, so the Drive knowledge sync would 403 — that scope is only needed on **moose@**, which is the mailbox `driveKnowledgeFolderId`/`listFolder` impersonate (PRIMARY_MAILBOX). tuaone@ needs nothing further.

**Done 2026-08-25 (LM Studio consolidated to one chat model + one embedding model; no code change):**
- **Richard had 4 models loaded/installed and wanted the minimum useful set.** Decided from the real workload, not intuition: over 45 days / 957 local calls, **output tokens exceed input tokens on every task** (thread summary 2,111 in → 2,354 out; email intake 2,073 → 4,852; fit assessment 2,789 → 3,088). Latency here is **generation-bound**, and generation speed on Apple Silicon is memory-bandwidth ÷ **active** params. The MoE reads ~1.7GB/token (~3B active at Q4); a dense 27B reads ~15GB/token — **roughly 3× slower per token on this box**, i.e. ~2min instead of ~40s on every extraction, brief, and agent turn. A dense model reasons slightly better per token and does not remotely pay for that.
- **FOUND: LM Studio had the WRONG model resident.** `qwen/qwen3.8-27b` was loaded at 16GB with no TTL while `.env.local` pins `qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b`. Both sets of weights are 36.6GB on a 36GB box, so every platform AI call was forcing a load/evict cycle instead of hitting a warm model. The 27B had **never served a single platform request** (all 957 local calls in `ai_queries` are the MoE).
- **Final state (LM Studio state only — no code, no env, no deploy):** `qwen/qwen3.6-35b-a3b` resident, **context 40960, parallel 1, no TTL**; `text-embedding-qwen3-embedding-0.6b` resident. Context was 61,696 — the largest real prompt measured is ~9.3k tokens (meeting intake), so the reclaimed KV cache is better spent as headroom. Parallel was 4; the sweep is strictly sequential (`for (const row of rows)`, `summarize-phase.ts:134`), so extra slots bought nothing and each cost its own KV cache. **Qwen3.8-27B-MLX-4bit deleted — 15GB reclaimed** (190→205GB free). Memory after: 23% free with both models resident.
- **The embedding model is NOT optional.** All **745 chunks** were embedded with it (1024→768 truncate + renormalize, `local.ts:284-310`). A change means wipe + re-embed; a mismatch breaks retrieval **silently** rather than erroring.
- **Nomic Embed was NOT deleted — it ships bundled with LM Studio** (`~/.lmstudio/.internal/bundled-models/`), so it returns on the next app update and is only 80MB. It has zero references in `src/`. Leave it; deleting it is churn.
- **Verified through the platform's own code** (alias-loader, not synthetic pings): `embedQuery` → 768 dims, L2 norm 1.0000; `match_chunks` → 5 real chunks; a real JSON extraction returned a valid object with the expected keys and correct assignees; `runAgent` autonomously called `get_steel_summary` and answered correctly ($3.6M revenue / $510k margin, matching the 08-24 data purge); `probeLmStudio()` reports ok. Throughput 64-76 tok/s vs the ~72 tok/s baseline — unchanged, as expected since the model itself didn't change. The win is no more load/evict thrash.
- **Confirmed while here (was an open item in the 07-07 cutover notes): LM Studio IS a login item**, so it comes back after a Studio reboot.
- **Known behavior, not fixed:** after a reboot nothing is preloaded, so the first call JIT-loads the model (~25s) and LM Studio then applies its **`jitModelTTL` of 3600s** — i.e. it unloads after an hour idle. The hourly email sweep keeps it warm in practice; the 6:30am brief after an idle night pays ~25s once. Raise that TTL in Developer → Server Settings if it ever matters.
- **Deliberately not done:** no vision model (Richard's call — PDFs already extract text locally via `unpdf`, audio goes through Whisper, so the gap only affects true image uploads, and a VL model competes for the same 36GB); no higher quant (Q5≈25GB / Q6≈29GB leaves no room for KV cache alongside Colima's 1.4GB and Whisper's 1.6GB transcription spikes).

**Done 2026-08-24 (health-check false alarm + Gmail send scope granted; DEPLOYED + VERIFIED):**
- **Richard reported `/settings/health` showing `Missing: GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY`. His config was fine — the CHECK was wrong.** The required-env block hardcoded that variable, but this deployment runs **per-mailbox OAuth (mode 3)**, which `getAccessToken()` selects by the *ABSENCE* of the two service-account vars. So a correctly configured Studio failed that check permanently, and the remedy it printed (set the key, redeploy) would have moved the box to a mode the org policy blocks. Fixed: the check now calls `isGoogleConfigured()` — the same predicate the runtime uses — instead of naming any single var. **Rule: never assert a specific Google env var in a health check; the valid var set is mode-dependent.**
- **Three adjacent messages in the same file assumed service-account mode** and would have sent Richard down the wrong path during a real outage. All now branch on `mailbox.signingMode === 'per-mailbox OAuth'`: (1) the "Connected" detail claimed *"access does not expire, nothing to reconnect"* — untrue for OAuth, where a password change or an admin revoking app access kills a refresh token; it now says so and gives the recovery command; (2) "disconnected" pointed only at `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_KEY_FILE`; (3) "broken" pointed only at the domain-wide-delegation console. OAuth mode now gets the `setup-google-oauth.mjs --only <addr>` path in all three.
- **`gmail.send` granted — the digest can actually send now.** The refresh tokens were minted 2026-08-23, before `gmail.send` joined `SCOPES`; verified against Google's token endpoint that both mailboxes held only readonly scopes. Richard re-consented both; re-verified live that **both now carry `gmail.send`**. Tokens copied to the Studio and the app **restarted** — `_oauthStore` is cached in module scope, so replacing the file under a running process changes nothing until restart. **Rule: any token-file swap needs `launchctl kickstart -k gui/$(id -u)/com.berwilson.platform`, not just an scp.**
- **Send path proven end to end, not just the scope:** called the platform's own `sendMail()` on the Studio via the alias-loader pattern, `moose@` → `moose@` (inside the Workspace, no outside recipient); Gmail accepted it and a follow-up Gmail query confirmed **1 delivered message**. That is the exact path the weekly task digest uses.
- **`scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs`: `prompt` is now `select_account consent`** (was `consent`). With only `login_hint` set, a browser already signed into an unrelated account (a personal gmail.com) can sail past the picker onto a consent screen for the WRONG account. `login_hint` happened to auto-fill correctly this time — luck, not guarantee. The picker is now forced. (Consent screen is **Internal**, so no verification prompt and no 7-day refresh-token expiry.)
- **Confirmed healthy while here:** the 08-24 mail catch-up fix is deployed and working — the sweep picked up new threads after reaching `complete` (moose 84→98, tuaone 486→522 at the 17:01 UTC run; 535 threads stored, 395 summarized, 140 pending, draining hourly). All five launchd services loaded. `tsc` + `next build` clean; eslint unchanged from baseline. macOS Finder-duplicate artifacts (`routes.d 3.ts`) had returned and were breaking `tsc` again — removed.
- **Still untested: the digest's CONTENT path.** `buildMemberDigests()` finds **0 candidates** because no open task carries a due date, so the Monday 7:00am run would go out empty. Put due dates on a few tasks, then trigger `/api/cron/task-digest` and read the result — note it is **idempotent per member per day** via `notification_log`, so a manual run consumes that day's slot.


**Done 2026-08-23 (Outlook → Google Workspace + the mailbox sweep):**
- **Auth replaced, not ported.** Microsoft Graph is gone: `microsoft-graph.ts`, `graph-search.ts`, the `/api/email/oauth(+callback)` flow, and the `integrations/types.ts` stub are deleted. New `src/lib/integrations/google-workspace.ts` authenticates with a **service account using domain-wide delegation** (no SDK — §11 intact) and impersonates each mailbox directly.
- **Auth is per-mailbox OAuth, NOT a service account.** Three modes exist in `google-workspace.ts` and `getAccessToken()` selects by config; only the third is usable here:
  1. downloaded service account key → **blocked**: the Cloud org enforces `iam.managed.disableServiceAccountKeyCreation` (Google's auto-applied security baseline, inherited, and IT would not carve out an exception in time);
  2. service account + IAM `signJwt` (Google signs with its own managed key, no key ever downloaded) → **works, unused**: needs `gcloud` on the Studio for a caller credential. Kept in the code; set `GOOGLE_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL` to switch to it;
  3. **per-mailbox OAuth refresh tokens** → **IN USE**. Desktop-app OAuth client (loopback redirect, the only client type that allows it), one browser consent per mailbox via `scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs`, tokens at `~/berwilson-data/google-oauth-tokens.json` (mode 600, on both machines, outside the app dir). Selected by the ABSENCE of the two service-account env vars. `/settings/health` reports the live mode.
  - **The regression to accept:** a refresh token can be revoked (password change, admin revoking app access), so "reconnect the mailbox" exists again in a smaller form. Recovery: `node scripts/setup-google-oauth.mjs --only <addr>` then scp to the Studio. `scripts/verify-google-auth.mjs` walks the whole chain and names the broken link.
  - Service account (unused) client ID `111229421328742032282`; OAuth client `247991610437-…` in project `berwilson-website` (now displayed as "Ber Intelligence"). **Nothing is stored and nothing expires**, so the entire AADSTS "reconnect the mailbox" apparatus in `system-health.ts` and `/settings/health` collapsed into one "can we mint a token" probe. Read-only scopes: `gmail.readonly`, `calendar.readonly`, `contacts.readonly`, `contacts.other.readonly`. Setup procedure: **`deploy/google-workspace-setup.md`**.
- **Three dead tables dropped**, not migrated: `email_tokens`, `graph_subscriptions`, `processed_emails` (the latter two had zero reads/writes anywhere in `src`), plus the unused `outlook_web_link` columns. `email_tokens` also carried a real hole — it granted `select` on raw access + refresh tokens to every authenticated user.
- **New mailbox sweep** (`src/lib/email-sweep/`, migration `20260823000001`) — reads ALL history in `moose@` + `tuaone@` and populates the CRM. Four resumable phases: **fetch** (page through Gmail, checkpoint the page token after every page → `mailbox_sync`), **map** (one AI call per thread → `ThreadSummary` with a deal/operational/noise triage, → `email_threads`), **cluster** (deterministic union-find grouping on canonical deal name + shared *external* participant → `thread_clusters`), **reduce** (one cluster → one `pending` review session via the existing `analyzeEmailReport`). Driven hourly by `com.berwilson.cron-email-sweep`; `POST /api/email-sweep/run` and `GET /api/email-sweep/status` for manual control.
- **Why map/reduce.** Measured on the Studio (2026-08-23): `qwen3.6-35b-a3b` at 64k loaded context reads one thread in ~25-50s. Feeding a whole deal's raw correspondence to the intake prompt would blow context and degrade badly, so the model only ever sees one thread (map) or a page of summaries (cluster reduce). Budget **~8-14h per 1,000 threads**; a full two-mailbox backfill runs for days. The queue drains **newest-first** so the live pipeline lands first. `analyze.ts`'s input cap is now provider-aware (`maxInputChars()`: 200k Gemini / 40k local) and it gained `documentText`, so the model can read a summary while the record still stores the real correspondence.
- **Gmail vs Graph, encoded:** `q` filters dates server-side (the client-side date pass is gone); a thread arrives whole in one call; but attachments are NOT inline — every file costs a `messages.attachments.get`, so `email-research/run` now only downloads bytes when analysis or staging actually wants them. Critically, **`threadId` is per-mailbox** — the same conversation in both mailboxes has two ids — so all cross-mailbox dedupe goes through the RFC 2822 `Message-ID` fingerprint.
- **Also rewired:** `/api/calendar/events`, `/api/meeting-intake/calendar-events`, and the daily-brief cron onto a provider-neutral `CalendarEvent` shape; party enrichment onto the People API (`graph_done` → `directory_done` end-to-end); the dashboard's mailbox alert off token staleness (which no longer exists) onto `mailbox_sync.state === 'failed'`. `middleware.ts` swaps the OAuth callback allowlist entry for the sweep cron. `tsc`, eslint, and `next build` all clean.

- **APPLIED + LIVE (2026-08-23).** Migration applied, deployed, and the first sweep has run. Fetch phase complete: **533 unique threads** (84 in moose@, 487 in tuaone@, 38 deduped as the same conversations across both — the Message-ID fingerprinting works). Summarizing runs hourly from there; ~25-50s/thread means the backlog drains in roughly 4-7 hours, not the days a bigger mailbox would have taken.
- **⚠ The DB is self-hosted on the Studio — `supabase db push --linked` targets the WRONG database.** `supabase/.temp/linked-project.json` still pointed at the retired hosted project (`qauclkrdejgtpywqixho`, AWS us-west-2) long after the move to self-hosted Supabase under Colima on the Studio. Pushing would have migrated the dead cloud DB and silently left the live one untouched. Fixes applied: `supabase/.temp/` is now **untracked and gitignored**, and `npm run gen-types` **fails loudly** with an explanation instead of running against the wrong target.
  - **How to apply a migration now:** `cat supabase/migrations/X.sql | ssh richardwhite@100.86.79.4 'export PATH=$HOME/.local/bin:$PATH DOCKER_HOST="unix://$HOME/.colima/default/docker.sock"; docker exec -i supabase-db psql -U postgres -d postgres -v ON_ERROR_STOP=1 --single-transaction'`. Back up any table being dropped first (`pg_dump --data-only`); `email_tokens` was dumped to `~/berwilson-data/backups/` before this migration dropped it.
  - **Type regeneration is currently impossible** and deliberately not depended on: the Supabase CLI runs `postgres-meta` in a container and needs a DB host reachable from both host and container, which Colima doesn't provide. `src/lib/email-sweep/db.ts` carries hand-maintained row types plus an untyped client precisely so the sweep never needs `database.ts`. Harmless — output tracing only shapes serverless bundles and this runs as a long-lived `next start`. Documented at the top of that file; do not "fix" it by hard-coding credential paths.
